Family archive

175 people · 84 records read in the original · 300 images at full resolution · Calabria, Abruzzo, the Garfagnana, Piedmont and Argentina

Fully translated. Two things stay in the original on purpose. The Italian quoted from the manuscripts is never translated — that is the source, and it stands as written. And the names of archives, collections and registers stay in Spanish or Italian, because that is how you would have to quote them to an archivist. The captions under the images come from the archive's own filenames and are in Spanish.

Loose ends

Ancestor

Domenico Pizzi

In the records: «il fu Domenico Pizzi» in the 1841 record

A stonemason from Atessa, in Abruzzo. He is the oldest person in the entire tree. His son Leone was born around 1761, which places him in the generation of 1730-1740. He was already dead by October 1841.

Married Santa Menna

Parent of Leone Pizzi

Birth
c. 1730-1740, presumably Atessa (province of Chieti, Abruzzo)
Trade
muratore (stonemason) — the same trade as his son, his grandson and his great-grandson
Son
Leone Pizzi (c. 1761 – 26 October 1841)
Spouse
Santa Menna, also deceased by 1841
Death
not located — before October 1841
Where this comes from
the death record of his son Leone: Atessa, Morti 1841, record 293

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026, and with this the tree gains a whole generation. The death record of his son Leone (Atessa, 26 October 1841, record 293, downloaded and transcribed in Leone's file) says «figlio del FU DOMENICO PIZZI di professione MURATORE». The «fu» says that he was already dead in 1841; the trade, that masonry runs in the family — Leone is a muratore (mason), his grandson Domenico is a muratore, and the 1869 record repeats it. His own death record is out of reach: the civil status in the Abruzzi begins in 1809 and he, with a son born c. 1761, almost certainly died before that. What would remain are the parish registers of Atessa, in the Archivio Diocesano di Chieti-Vasto.

Ancestor

Santa Menna

Mother of Leone Pizzi, from Atessa. Born around 1730-1740. She is the oldest documented woman in the tree, and the first mother any record of this era bothers to name.

Married Domenico Pizzi

Parent of Leone Pizzi

c. 1730-1740, presumably Atessa
Son
Leone Pizzi (c. 1761 – 26 October 1841)
Spouse
Domenico Pizzi, muratore
Death
not located — before October 1841; the record calls her «quondam»
Surname
MENNA is one of the commonest surnames at Atessa: record number 1 of the births of 1869 is that of a Maria Arcangela Menna

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026, and it is worth underlining how exceptional this is. In the four death records from Rende read in this same genealogy, ALL FOUR leave the mother's name blank. Here, in Atessa and fifty years earlier, the form of the Kingdom of the Two Sicilies requires both boxes to be filled in and the clerk fills them: «e della QUONDAM SANTA MENNA». «Quondam» is the Latin for «fu»: she had already died by October 1841. She is given neither trade nor domicile —those two dashes are left empty— but the name is there, and with it a new maternal branch beginning in the middle of the eighteenth century.

Ancestor

Leone Pizzi

A stonemason from Atessa, of the rione di Santa Croce, born around 1761. He was widowed three times. He died on 26 October 1841, aged eighty, and his record — the oldest in this whole genealogy — names his parents. His son Giuseppe's record, six years later, names Leone: it is the link that joins the two halves of the Atessa tree.

What the manuscript says

Num. d'ordine 293 — Pag. 147
L'anno mille ottocento quarantuno, il dì ventisette del mese di Ottobre, alle ore
quindici d'Italia, avanti di noi Giuseppe Salvucci Secondo Eletto pel Sindaco
impedito ed uffiziale dello Stato civile del Comune di Atessa, Distretto di Vasto,
Provincia di Abruzzo Citeriore, sono comparsi Luigi Tiroli di anni trenta, di
professione contadino, regnicolo, domiciliato in questo Comune nel rione del
Castello, e Giuseppe Ciccarelli di anni quaranta, di professione contadino,
regnicolo, domiciliato in questo medesimo Comune nel rione di Santa Croce, i quali
han dichiarato, che nel giorno ventisei del mese di Ottobre anno corrente, alle ore
diciassette è morto nella sua casa LEONE PIZZI di anni ottanta, vedovo in primo
voto di Anna Iano, in secondo voto di Nora Iacobitti, ed in terzo voto di Erminia
Vitulli, nato in Atessa, di professione muratore, domiciliato ivi nel rione di
Santa Croce, figlio del fu DOMENICO PIZZI di professione muratore, domiciliato
————, e della quondam SANTA MENNA, domiciliata ————.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and forty-one, on the TWENTY-SEVENTH day of the month of OCTOBER, at FIFTEEN HOURS ITALIAN STYLE.

Before us, GIUSEPPE SALVUCCI, Second Elect for the Mayor being indisposed, and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of ATESSA, District of VASTO, Province of Abruzzo Citeriore, there appeared LUIGI TIROLI, aged thirty, by trade a farmhand, a subject of the kingdom, resident in this comune in the QUARTER OF THE CASTELLO, and GIUSEPPE CICCARELLI, aged forty, by trade a farmhand, a subject of the kingdom, resident in this same comune in the QUARTER OF SANTA CROCE.

Who declared that on the TWENTY-SIXTH day of the month of OCTOBER of the current year, at SEVENTEEN HOURS, there died IN HIS OWN HOUSE LEONE PIZZI, AGED EIGHTY, WIDOWED in first marriage of ANNA IANO, in second marriage of NORA IACOBITTI and in third marriage of ERMINIA VITULLI, BORN AT ATESSA, by trade a STONEMASON, resident in the same place in the quarter of Santa Croce, SON OF THE LATE DOMENICO PIZZI, by trade a STONEMASON, AND OF THE LATE SANTA MENNA.

In execution of the law we proceeded together with the said witnesses to the deceased person, and we ascertained his actual death. We thereupon drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers, and having read it aloud to the informants, it was signed by us on the day, month and year stated above, the parties having declared that they could not write. — Giuseppe Salvucci; Giovannantonio Bassani, chancellor.

What you need to know about this record

  • EIGHTY YEARS OLD IN OCTOBER 1841: he was born c. 1761. It is by a wide margin the earliest date of birth in the whole tree; the second earliest, Pietro Chiappetta, is c. 1780.
  • THE RECORD NAMES HIS PARENTS, and this is the find of the batch. «Figlio del FU DOMENICO PIZZI di professione MURATORE, e della QUONDAM SANTA MENNA». Two full names, a trade, and both marked as dead («fu» and «quondam»). With Leone born c. 1761, his parents were born c. 1730-1740: THE TREE GAINS AN ENTIRE GENERATION, and for the first time reaches the eighteenth century.
  • AND IT NAMES THE MOTHER. Of the four Rende death records read in this same genealogy, all four leave the mother's box blank. The form of the Kingdom of the Two Sicilies, fifty years earlier, fills it in. Santa Menna is the first maternal grandmother in the whole corpus to have a name of her own.
  • THREE WIDOWHOODS. «Vedovo in primo voto di Anna Iano, in secondo voto di Nora Iacobitti, ed in terzo voto di Erminia Vitulli». He buried three wives and died alone. Which of the three is the mother of the branch that follows is unresolved: it would take the marriage of Giuseppe Pizzi or his birth record.
  • MASONS FROM FATHER TO SON. His father muratore (mason), he himself muratore, and in 1869 his presumed grandson Domenico Pizzi declares himself muratore before the mayor of Atessa. Three consecutive generations in the same trade, in a world of records where everybody is contadino (peasant).
  • THE DISTRICT IS SANTA CROCE. One of the two informants, Giuseppe Ciccarelli, lives in the same rione. By 1869 the family has moved to the rione del Castello.
  • THE DOUBTFUL READING IS THE SECOND WIFE'S FORENAME: «Nora Iacobitti» could be «Rosa». The surname Iacobitti reads without difficulty. That of the first informant, TIROLI, also appears in 1891 (Donato Tiroli, informant on Domenico Pizzi's record): the same family attesting to the deaths of the Pizzi fifty years apart.
  • THE REGISTER HAS NO INDEX. It ends in blank forms (records 352 and 353 unused), so the year 1841 closed with some 351 deaths. Interpolation was necessary.
Ancestor

Rosa Iacobitti

Second wife of Leone Pizzi and mother of Giuseppe Pizzi. Born around 1765. She is the only one of Leone's three wives in the bloodline, and she is known only from her son's death record.

Married Leone Pizzi

The forename
ROSA. The 1841 record was read at the time as «Nora Iacobitti», with the doubt noted; that of 1847, in another hand, writes Rosa plainly

No record obtained yet

NEW IN AUGUST 2026, and she arrives on the very line that links her son to her husband. The death record of GIUSEPPE PIZZI (Atessa, 29 April 1847, record 106, transcribed in his own file) says «figlio del fu LEONE PIZZI di professione muratore, e della fu ROSA IACOBITTI». That «della fu» does two things at once: it names her and it declares her dead. WHAT IT SETTLES. Leone Pizzi was widowed three times —of Anna Iano, of Rosa Iacobitti and of Erminia Vitulli— and his own record, that of 1841, names all three without saying which of them bore him children. For a time that left three possible great-great-grandmothers and none of them certain. The 1847 record chooses: it is her. The other two leave the bloodline. WHAT IS NOT KNOWN: when she was born, whose daughter she was and when she died. Her marriage to Leone would be earlier than 1787 and therefore earlier than the Napoleonic civil status, so one would have to go to the parish registers of Atessa, in the Archivio Diocesano di Chieti-Vasto. The surname IACOBITTI belongs to the area and also appears in Atessa in 1899.

Ancestor

Santo Bosco

In the records: «figli di fu Santo» in the 1865 record

Father of Maria Bosco, from Marano Principato. Born around 1760-1775. He is new as of August 2026 and is the oldest name in the whole RICCHIO branch — the one that runs down to Filomena Chiappetta through her great-grandmother Carmina Ricchio.

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY RECORD on 26 August 2026. The death record of his daughter MARIA BOSCO (Castrolibero, 11 August 1865, record 11, downloaded and transcribed in her own file) says «figli di FU SANTO». The printed form of the Kingdom of the Two Sicilies asks there only for the given name, because the surname is that of the deceased: BOSCO. The «fu» says that he had already died by August 1865. WHY IT MATTERS: until 25 August 2026 it was not even known that Maria Bosco existed. The death record of her daughter Carmina (Rende, 1883) named only the father and left the mother's box blank. First an index gave Maria Bosco, then the 1865 record confirmed her, and along the way released her father. Two new generations in two days, in the branch about which least was known. WHAT IS NOT KNOWN: anything else. Neither trade, nor domicile, nor whom he was married to — the 1865 record expressly declares that Maria's mother «s'ignora». His own death record would be from Marano Principato, earlier than 1865; his marriage and his birth are almost certainly parish matters.

Ancestor

Pietro Chiappetta

A day labourer from Castrolibero, father of Raffaele Chiappetta. Born around 1780. He was already dead by December 1880.

Parent of Raffaele Chiappetta

Three facts in a single line

Everything known about him fits into one line written on 4 December 1880, when two day labourers from Rende went to the town hall to report the death of his son Raffaele. The clerk noted that the deceased was the son «da fu Pietro, bracciante, domiciliato in detto Castrolibero». It is all there: what he was called, what he lived by and where. And the «fu», which on these forms means deceased: by December 1880 Pietro had been dead for some time.

His son was born around 1805, so he must have been born between 1775 and 1785. That puts him out of reach by the civil route: civil registration in Calabria begins in 1809, and before that date there are only parish books. His death record, on the other hand, could well exist, in the Castrolibero death registers before 1880.

There is a clue about his wife, whom the 1880 record leaves unnamed. In three marriages of other Chiappettas gathered in the blog corpus — Castrolibero 1832, Marano Principato 1839 and Castrolibero 1849 — the groom's parents are «Chiappetta, Pietro» and «Veltri, Pasqualina». If it is the same Pietro, she was called Pasqualina Veltri. It is not confirmed: no record read so far says so of him directly.

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026. The death of his son Raffaele (Rende, 3 December 1880, record 135, downloaded and transcribed in Raffaele's file) says «da FU PIETRO, bracciante, domiciliato in detto Castrolibero». Three facts in one line: that he was called Pietro, that he was a day labourer and that he lived in Castrolibero; and the «fu», which says that by December 1880 he was already dead. His own record would have to be looked for in Castrolibero, and with a son born c. 1805 the window for his birth falls around 1775-1785 — beyond the reach of the civil status, which in Calabria begins in 1809. His death, on the other hand, could well be there: Castrolibero has registers from 1809.

Ancestor

Carmine Molinaro

He is taken to be the father of Teresa Molinaro, but her death record does NOT name him. The name comes from the index alone.

Married Carmina Covello

Parent of Teresa Molinaro

Two blank boxes

On 20 December 1879 the death record of his daughter Teresa was drawn up at Rende. The form had space for both parents. The clerk wrote «da fu ______» and «e dalla fu ______» and left both names blank — something he did not do on the other three records of that same generation, where he did put the father's name. That record had been obtained precisely on the expectation that it would name him.

So Carmine Molinaro remains what he was before: an index line with no document behind it. The only thing the record genuinely adds is the «fu»: whoever Teresa's father was, he had died before December 1879. And a correction of spelling, which is no small thing when you are searching indexes: the surname is written MOLINARO, with an o, in the body of the record, in the marginal heading, in the register's own annual index and in his grandson's marriage of 1873. The «Mulinaro» of the modern indexes is a distortion.

One attractive hypothesis remains, with no record to support it. In the Marano Principato marriages of 1839, Antonio Chiappetta, son of Pietro Chiappetta and Pasqualina Veltri, marries Saveria Maria Mulinaro, daughter of Carmine Mulinaro and Carmina Covello. If our Raffaele — also a son of Pietro — married Teresa — a daughter of Carmine — they would be two brothers married to two sisters. It fits, but the marriage of Raffaele and Teresa was searched for at Marano Principato in 1838 and 1841-1845, page by page, and it is not there: among those forty-six marriages no Chiappetta appears.

WARNING, August 2026. The death record of his daughter Teresa (Rende, 19 December 1879, record 94) was downloaded on the expectation that it would name the parents, because the form of these registers has a box for both. IT DOES NOT NAME THEM. The clerk wrote «da fu ______» and «e dalla fu ______» and left both names blank, something he did not do in the other three records of this same batch, where he did put the father's name. So «Carmine Mulinaro» remains what he was: an index entry with no document behind it. The only thing the record really contributes is the «fu»: whoever Teresa's father was, he had died before December 1879. And that the family surname is written MOLINARO, not «Mulinaro».

Ancestor

Carmina Covello

She is taken to be the mother of Teresa Molinaro. No record read so far names her.

Married Carmine Molinaro

Parent of Teresa Molinaro

The mother nobody wrote down

Her name comes from the same index line as her husband's, and the document that was meant to back it up does not. In her daughter Teresa's death record of December 1879 the box reads «e dalla fu ______ domiciliata in ______» and is empty.

This is not an isolated slip, it is a pattern. Of the four death records of that generation read in 2026 — Teresa Molinaro in 1879, Raffaele Chiappetta in 1880, Carmina Ricchio in 1883 and Domenico Massei in 1891 — all four omit the mother's name; three of them do give the father's. In these Rende registers maternal descent is the first thing to be lost. The oddest case is that of 1891: the clerk left the name blank and yet filled in «domiciliata in Rende». He knew where that woman had lived and did not know what she was called.

Of Carmina Covello, therefore, the only documented fact is that Teresa's mother had died before December 1879. She is kept in the tree, marked as unconfirmed.

The same situation as her husband, and for the same reason. The death record of her daughter Teresa (Rende, 1879, record 94) has the mother's box —«e dalla fu ______ domiciliata in ______»— and it is blank. She is kept in the tree on the strength of the index, marked as unconfirmed. The pattern is worth noting: of the four death records of this generation read in 2026, ALL FOUR are silent on the mother's name. Three of them do give the father's. In these Rende registers maternal filiation is the first fact to be lost.

Ancestor

Giuseppe Ricchio

A day labourer from Marano Principato, father of Carmina Ricchio. Born around 1800. He was already dead by December 1883, and he never left his village.

Married Maria Bosco

Parent of Carmina Ricchio

Resident in life at Marano

On 12 December 1883, at the town hall of Rende, his son-in-law Giovanni Chiappetta reported the death of Carmina Ricchio, which had occurred the previous day at noon. In identifying the deceased, the record calls her the daughter «da fu Giuseppe, bracciante, domiciliato in vita in Marano Principato».

That «in vita» — in life — is what turns the phrase into a usable fact. It does not merely say that Giuseppe had died: it says where he lived until the end. His death record is not to be sought at Rende, where his daughter died, but in the Marano Principato registers before 1883.

Marano Principato did not appear in this tree until that run of readings. It suddenly turned out to be the home village of two of Filomena's four grandparents: Carmina Ricchio and Teresa Molinaro. Giuseppe, a day labourer like almost every man in this line, was presumably born there around 1800, reckoning back from his daughter's birth around 1828.

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026, and with a clue as to where to look for him. The death of his daughter Carmina (Rende, 11 December 1883, record 163, transcribed in her own file) says «da FU GIUSEPPE, bracciante, domiciliato IN VITA in Marano Principato». That «in vita» is gold: it not only confirms that he had died, it says where he lived until the end. His death record ought therefore to be in the registers of MARANO PRINCIPATO, earlier than 1883 — a comune that until this batch did not appear in the tree and which now turns out to be the town of origin of two of Filomena's four great-grandparents. CORRECTION OF 26 AUGUST 2026, and it runs the other way. The death record of his wife MARIA BOSCO (Castrolibero, 11 August 1865, record 11) calls her «VEDOVA DEL FU GIUSEPPE RICCHIO» and places her living in CASTROLIBERO, contrada Feudo, although born in Marano Principato. That is: the family had moved to Castrolibero, it was there that their daughter Carmina married in 1849 and there that his wife died. The «domiciliato in vita in Marano Principato» of the 1883 record was said by a son-in-law eighteen years after her death and twenty-eight after his, and it probably confuses the town of origin with the last domicile. The index places his death in CASTROLIBERO, Morti 1855, record 28 — which is where it must be looked for, and not in Marano Principato.

Ancestor

Maria Bosco

A countrywoman born in Marano Principato around 1797, wife of Giuseppe Ricchio and mother of Carmina Ricchio. She died a widow at Castrolibero, contrada Feudo, on 11 August 1865, aged sixty-eight. Until 25 August 2026 no one knew she existed.

What the manuscript says

…è morta nella casa di sua abitazione MARIA BOSCO vedova del fu GIUSEPPE RICCHIO, nato in Marano Principato, figli di FU SANTO ———— di professione ———— domiciliato ————, e di s'ignora ———— domiciliata ———— di anni SESSANTOTTO di professione CONTADINA domiciliata in Castrolibero, Contrada Feudo.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and sixty-five, on the TWELFTH day of the month of AUGUST, at TWENTY hours, before Us, PIETRO PERRI, acting mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of CASTROLIBERO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared FERDINANDO STILLATO, SON-IN-LAW OF THE DECEASED, aged thirty, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident at Castrolibero, contrada Feudo; and GIACINTO BOSCO, aged thirty, by trade a bracciale, of the kingdom, resident in this comune.

Who declared that on the ELEVENTH day of the month of AUGUST of the current year, at SIXTEEN hours, there died IN THE HOUSE OF HER DWELLING MARIA BOSCO, WIDOW OF THE LATE GIUSEPPE RICCHIO, born at MARANO PRINCIPATO, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE SANTO — by trade (blank), resident (blank) — AND OF A MOTHER UNKNOWN (blank), AGED SIXTY-EIGHT, by trade a CONTADINA, resident at CASTROLIBERO, CONTRADA FEUDO.

In compliance with the laws we proceeded together with the said witnesses to the deceased person and ascertained her actual death. We thereafter drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers, and having read it to the informants, it was signed by Us on the day, month and year stated above, the informing witnesses having declared that they could not write.

What you need to know about this record

  • WHAT IDENTIFIES THIS WOMAN AND NO OTHER is the SPOUSE: «VEDOVA DEL FU GIUSEPPE RICCHIO». In Castrolibero and Marano Principato there are several intertwined Ricchio and Bosco families, and the spouse column was the one that had to decide. It decides. And everything else fits: born in MARANO PRINCIPATO, which is where her daughter Carmina was born and where the 1883 record places Giuseppe Ricchio; and died a WIDOW, consistent with his death in 1855.
  • SANTO BOSCO, CONFIRMED. «figli di FU SANTO». The printed form asks only for the forename because the surname is that of the deceased. The «fu» says he had already died. It is an entire new generation on the branch by which Filomena Chiappetta descends from the Ricchio, and the earliest name on that branch.
  • MARIA ESPOSITO, NOT CONFIRMED — AND MOREOVER CONTRADICTED. The blog index gave both parents, «Bosco, Santo — Esposito, Maria». The record confirms the first and denies the second: where the printed form asks for the mother's name, the clerk wrote «S'IGNORA» —it is unknown— and left empty the three rules for the name, the trade and the residence. It is not an omission like the one in Carmina Spizzirri's record of 1864, where the boxes were simply left blank: here the clerk takes the trouble to write a word where a name ought to go. READING NOTE, to be honest: the word is perfectly clear letter by letter —S, i, g, n, o, r, a— and admits two readings, «s'ignora» (it is unknown) or the courtesy title «Signora» placed where the name should be. In both cases the result is the same and it is the one that matters: THERE IS NO MOTHER'S NAME IN THE RECORD AT ALL. MARIA ESPOSITO remains index evidence and nothing more, and has not been entered into this tree as a person.
  • SIXTY-EIGHT YEARS OLD IN AUGUST 1865: born c. 1797. With Carmina born c. 1828 she had her at about thirty-one, which is normal. With Cherubina, whom the indexes put in 1849, she would be fifty-two, which is no longer so: either Cherubina is not her daughter, or one of the declared ages is rounded. It is noted and unresolved.
  • SHE LIVED IN CASTROLIBERO, CONTRADA FEUDO, not in Marano Principato. The family had moved: there her daughter Carmina married in 1849, there her husband died in 1855 and there she dies. This corrects the reading that had been made of the 1883 record, which called Giuseppe Ricchio «domiciliato in vita in Marano Principato» — the son-in-law who reported it almost certainly confused the village of origin with the last residence.
  • THE FIRST INFORMANT IS A SON-IN-LAW AND HE IS NOT OURS. «FERDINANDO STILLATO, GENERO DELLA DEFUNTA», aged thirty. Carmina Ricchio was married to Domenico Antonio Mazzei and lived in Rende, so Stillato is the husband of ANOTHER daughter. The candidate is CHERUBINA RICCHIO, whom the indexes give as born in Marano Principato in 1849 and dead in Castrolibero in 1901 (record 18). Unchecked, but it is the thread to pull.
  • THE SECOND INFORMANT, GIACINTO BOSCO, aged thirty, bracciale (day labourer), bears the surname of the deceased. Nephew or close relative; nothing in the record makes it precise.
  • WHERE THE MOTHER MIGHT TURN UP: in the marriage of Maria Bosco to Giuseppe Ricchio, which from Carmina's age would be c. 1825-1828 in Marano Principato, or in Maria's own birth, c. 1797, which is already parish and earlier than the civil status.
Ancestor

Filippo Massei

In the records: Filippo Mazzei in the 1891 record

A day labourer from Rende, father of Domenico Massei. Born around 1795. He was already dead by November 1891.

Parent of Domenico Massei

From Rende, not Castrolibero

He appears in a single sentence, in the death record of his son Domenico, drawn up at Rende on 9 November 1891: «dal fu Filippo, bracciante, domiciliato in Rende».

That sentence corrected an assumption that had stood for some time. The Massei were taken to be people of Castrolibero, because Angela, Domenico's daughter, had been born there around 1858. But the record says that Domenico was born at Rende around 1821 and that his father was resident at Rende. The family had been from Rende all along; it was Domenico who moved, and who came back.

His death record should be in the Rende death registers before 1891 — collections already known and accessible — with nothing to go on but the name and the trade. His birth, around 1795, is out of reach. And the surname: the 1891 record spells it MAZZEI, with an unmistakable double z, while the records of the branch that lives on (1873, 1877, 1894) spell it MASSEI. Both forms are real and depend on the clerk, not on the bearer.

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026. The death of his son Domenico (Rende, 8 November 1891, record 112, transcribed in his own file) says «dal FU FILIPPO, bracciante, domiciliato in Rende». With the son born in Rende c. 1821, this branch turns out to have been of Rende all along, and not of Castrolibero as had been supposed. His death record ought to be in the Morti of Rende earlier than 1891 — which are on Antenati and within reach — with no clue beyond the name and the trade. His birth, c. 1795, falls outside: the civil status in Calabria begins in 1809.

How it was located
this register carries no alphabetical index either: it ends in blank forms, records 308-309 unused. Two records per image, and on each folio of the register go records 2P−2 and 2P−1; but the image→folio correspondence is NOT constant (folio = image − 36 in December, folio = image − 32 in April), so the formula that served for 1841 fails here by eight records. Eight probes: 191, 188, 185, 87, 80, 83, 85 and 86. The decisive step was seeing that record 108 was already of 17 May: that shut 29 April in between records 102 and 107, six records and three images

What the manuscript says

Num. d'ordine 106
L'anno mille ottocento quarantasette, il dì trenta del mese di Aprile, alle ore
quattordici d'Italia, avanti di noi Luigi Rossi Secondo Eletto pel Sindaco
impedito ed uffiziale dello stato civile del Comune di Atessa, Distretto di Vasto,
Provincia di Abruzzo Citeriore, sono comparsi Domenico Fidelibus di anni
cinquantotto, di professione contadino, regnicolo, domiciliato in questo Comune nel
rione del Mercato, e Nobile Domenico di anni sessanta, di professione contadino,
regnicolo, domiciliato in questo Comune nel rione del Castello, i quali han
dichiarato, che nel giorno ventinove del mese di Aprile anno corrente, alle ore
ventidue è morto nella sua casa GIUSEPPE PIZZI di anni sessanta, marito di Antonia
di Iorio, nato in Atessa, di professione muratore, domiciliato ivi nel rione di
Santa Croce, figlio del fu LEONE PIZZI di professione muratore, domiciliato ————,
e della fu ROSA IACOBITTI, domiciliata ————.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and forty-seven, on the THIRTIETH day of the month of APRIL, at FOURTEEN HOURS ITALIAN STYLE.

Before us, LUIGI ROSSI, Second Elect for the Mayor being indisposed, and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of ATESSA, District of VASTO, Province of Abruzzo Citeriore, there appeared DOMENICO FIDELIBUS, aged fifty-eight, by trade a farmhand, a subject of the kingdom, resident in this comune in the QUARTER OF THE MERCATO, and DOMENICO NOBILE, aged sixty, by trade a farmhand, a subject of the kingdom, resident in this same comune in the QUARTER OF THE CASTELLO.

Who declared that on the TWENTY-NINTH day of the month of APRIL of the current year, at TWENTY-TWO HOURS, there died in his own house GIUSEPPE PIZZI, aged SIXTY, HUSBAND OF ANTONIA DI IORIO, born at Atessa, by trade a STONEMASON, resident in the same place in the QUARTER OF SANTA CROCE, SON OF THE LATE LEONE PIZZI, by trade a STONEMASON, resident ————, AND OF THE LATE ROSA IACOBITTI, resident ————.

In execution of the law we proceeded together with the said witnesses before the deceased person and ascertained his actual death. We thereafter drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers and read to the informants; it was signed by us on the day, month and year stated above, the informants having declared that they could not write.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE RECORD THAT HOOKS ON THE GENERATION OF 1730. Until today, that Giuseppe Pizzi was the son of Leone was stated only by a family block without a single citation, the same one that had already erred three verified times. Now the document says it: «figlio del fu LEONE PIZZI di professione MURATORE». With that line there enter the tree, now proven, Leone and —through Leone's own record of 1841— his parents Domenico Pizzi and Santa Menna, who belong to the generation of 1730-1740.
  • AND IT RESOLVES THE SECOND LOOSE END, THAT OF THE THREE WIVES. Leone was widowed three times and his 1841 record does not say which of them bore him children. This one does: Giuseppe's mother is ROSA IACOBITTI, the second wife. Anna Iano and Erminia Vitulli fall outside the bloodline.
  • IN PASSING IT CORRECTS A READING. In the 1841 record the second wife's forename was noted as doubtful, «Nora Iacobitti (it could be Rosa)». Here, another hand and six years earlier, writes ROSA without ambiguity. Two different clerks, the same surname, and the forename now settled.
  • THE TRADE PASSES FROM FATHERS TO SONS, AND NOW THERE ARE FOUR IN A ROW. Domenico the elder (c. 1730) muratore, Leone (c. 1761) muratore, Giuseppe (c. 1787) muratore, and Domenico (c. 1824-1830) muratore. A hundred and fifty years of masons in the same village, and two of them in the same district, the rione di Santa Croce.
  • IT ALSO CLOSES WITH THE 1891 RECORD. This one gives him as «marito di ANTONIA DI IORIO»; the death of his son Domenico, forty-four years later, names the mother as «fu Antonia di Iorio, filatrice». It is the same woman, and the two records confirm one another without having been consulted together.
  • THE AGES TALLY WITHOUT FORCING ANYTHING: Leone was born c. 1761 and Giuseppe c. 1787, twenty-six years between father and son.
  • A DETAIL OF THE YEAR: the immediately preceding record, no. 105, is for another death on that same 29 April, a two-month-old girl. April 1847 was running in Atessa at the rate of one death a day; in May the pace falls to less than half.

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026, and it is a small victory against custom. The death of her son Domenico (Atessa, 28 September 1891, record 238, transcribed in his own file) says «e da FU ANTONIA DI IORIO, FILATRICE, domiciliata IN VITA in Atessa». In Atessa, the same printed form of the Kingdom of Italy, with the same printed gap, was in fact filled in. Her death ought to be in the Morti of Atessa earlier than 1891.

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026. The death record of his son Giuseppe —Marano Marchesato, 6 March 1863, record 24, downloaded and transcribed in Giuseppe's file— says «figlio di FU LORENZO... e di FU GIULIA CHIAPPETTA». The «fu» gives him as already dead in 1863. Until that reading, Lorenzo Palermo was no more than a box in a blog index. His own death record is not within reach by the easy route: with a son born c. 1773, he would normally have died between 1800 and 1840. If it was after 1809 it would be in the Morti of Marano Marchesato, which are on Antenati from 1822; if before, only the parish of S. Maria del Carmine remains. Warning: in Marano at least eight Palermo families live side by side at the same time, and the index for 1809-1826 carries another Lorenzo Palermo married to a Francesca Chiappetta who is NOT this one — or at least it is not proven that he is.

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026, and it is worth underlining how infrequent this is. In the four death records from Rende read in this genealogy, ALL FOUR leave the mother's name blank; and in the record of her own daughter-in-law, Carmina Spizzirri (Marano, 1864), the two boxes for father and mother are likewise left empty. Here, by contrast, the clerk of Marano filled them in: «figlio di fu Lorenzo... e di FU GIULIA CHIAPPETTA». The reading of the surname is firm —Chiappetta, not Chiappella— and it agrees with what the blog index gave. No trade or domicile is noted for her: those dashes were indeed left blank.

Death

Parish extract of death

Rende · folio 48 of the book of the dead · 20 August 1793

Archive
Archivio di Stato di Cosenza
Collection
Stato civile della Restaurazione > Rende > Registro 29 «Matrimoni, processetti» 1837
Register
ark an_ua35976841 · container hash: LPAZyGB (269 images)
Page
230 of 269 — dossier 20, that of the wedding of his granddaughter Carmina
Original
book of the dead of the parish of SANTA MARIA MAGGIORE of Rende, folio 48. The extract was issued by the parish priest Balthasar Bartucci on 19 October 1837, «ad fidem pro usu Matrimonii»
Parish priest in 1793
Nicolaus Rovella
How it was located
it was not sought: it turned up inside the marriage dossier of 1837, among the documents the couple had to produce in order to marry

What the manuscript says

Anno Domini 1793 die vigesimo Augusti Rendis — MICHAEL VOLPENTESTA VIR THERESIAE TENUTA, aetatis suae ann. septuaginta, omnibus SS. munitus, in C.S.M.E. animam suam Deo reddidit, ejusque corpus ad Ecclesiam Parochialem delatum, et post horas duas ibidem sepultum fuit. — Nicolaus Rovella, Rector.

Translation

I, the undersigned rector of the parish church of the town of Rende, under the invocation of SANTA MARIA MAGGIORE, having consulted the book of the dead, certify that I have found the following entry on folio 48, namely:

«IN THE YEAR OF OUR LORD 1793, ON THE TWENTIETH DAY OF AUGUST, AT RENDE — MICHELE VOLPENTESTA, HUSBAND OF TERESA TENUTA, aged SEVENTY years, fortified with all the sacraments, rendered his soul to God; and his body was carried to the parish church and there buried two hours later. — Nicolás Rovella, rector.»

And I so certify for the purpose of marriage. At Rende, 19 October 1837. Baltasar Bartucci, rector.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS DOCUMENT RESCUES TERESA TENUTA. Three words —VIR THERESIAE TENUTA— which turn her from an index name into a documented person. She had gone the whole project without a page of her own because the 1823 record leaves the mother's box blank, and it had been decided not to take her as good.
  • IT IS THE OLDEST DOCUMENT IN THE TREE, and it carries the chain back to 1723 — seventy years before 1793. Ahead of Domenico Pizzi and Santa Menna, who were around 1730.
  • HE DIED IN RENDE, NOT IN MARANO. The Volpentesta already had a link with Rende in the eighteenth century, two generations before his granddaughter Carmina settled there. And he married in THAT VERY CHURCH, Santa Maria Maggiore, in 1837. The family went back to the place it had left.
  • He was buried inside the parish church two hours after dying. In August, in Calabria, the haste explains itself.
  • The blog index dated this death to 1793 and got it right. But the same index gave that of his son Vincenzo as 1827 when the record is of 1823. Being right once does not make it reliable: it remains a lead, not proof.
  • 29 AUGUST 2026 — HE NOW HAS CHILDREN, AND THEY HAVE DATES. Since he died in Rende, his children had to be baptised in Rende, and the baptisms of that parish begin in 1731 and are transcribed in the Calabrian blog that was already on disc. Swept year by year, THREE children of his and of Teresa Tenuta appear, always with the same formula «Volpintesta, Michele — Tenuta, Teresa»: PIETRO GAETANO (1767, record 13), VINCENZO MERCURIO (1770, record 48) and GREGORIO GIOACCHINO (1773, record 41). The middle one could be our Vincenzo; that is discussed on his own page, because there is a clash of dates and of village that cannot be papered over.
  • CAUTION: IN RENDE THERE WERE AT LEAST FOUR MICHELE VOLPINTESTA AT THE SAME TIME. One the son of Tommaso and Isabella Pellegrino, married in 1758 to Orsola Pellegrino and widowed the following year, who married again in 1759 to Arcangela Pranno and had children in 1761, 1763, 1765, 1768, 1771 and 1774. Another married to Saveria Santanna in 1764. The name on its own is worth nothing: what identifies ours is ALWAYS the wife's name, Teresa Tenuta.
  • HIS MARRIAGE IS NOT IN RENDE, AND THAT IS ALREADY A RESULT. The indexes of parish marriages of Rende were read for THIRTY-NINE consecutive years —1733 to 1750 and 1752 to 1772— and in none of them does a Michele Volpintesta marry a Tenuta. ONE SINGLE GAP remains, 1751, which is the only year the blog does not have. The other explanation, simpler, is that they married IN HER PARISH, which is the usual thing, and then the record is in another village nearby.

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026, and doubly so. The death record of his daughter Teresa —Marano Marchesato, 6 June 1851, record 44, downloaded and transcribed in her own file— says «figlia del FU GIO: ANTONIO, di professione BRACCIALE, e della FU MARIANNA CONFORTI». And the death index of Marano for 1834, record 17, carries an Antonio Luchetta «di Antonio e Conforti Marianna», married to Caterina Cosentino: a brother of Teresa, who confirms the marriage by a second, independent route. His own record would have to be looked for in the Morti of Marano Marchesato earlier than June 1851. The series is on Antenati from 1822, so if he died after that date it is within reach; the sweep of indexes from 1822 to 1867 already carried out did not find him, which pushes his death back to before 1822 and, with it, into the parish registers.

CONFORTI is the surname of the mayors of Marano for half a century: Francesco Conforti signs Carmina's birth in 1814, Ferdinando Conforti Teresa's death in 1851 and Gioacchino Conforti that of Carmina Spizzirri in 1864. No kinship with any of them is proven

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY RECORD in August 2026. The death record of her daughter Teresa Luchetta —Marano Marchesato, 6 June 1851, record 44— names her in full: «e della FU MARIANNA CONFORTI». It is exactly what the blog index gave, now backed by the document. And there is a second, independent route: the death of 1834, record 17, of an Antonio Luchetta «di Antonio e Conforti Marianna», Teresa's brother. That a Marano record should name the mother is not something that can be taken for granted: the record of Vincenzo Volpentesta of 1823 left that box blank, and that of Carmina Spizzirri of 1864 left both blank. Here the clerk filled them in, and with that Teresa Luchetta went from having no paper at all to having father and mother in a single line.

Ancestor

Giuseppe Palermo

A shepherd and day labourer from Marano Marchesato, father of Francesco Giacomo Palermo. He was born around 1773 and died in his own house on 6 March 1863, aged NINETY. His death record, read in August 2026, at last names his parents: Lorenzo Palermo and Giulia Chiappetta.

Child of Lorenzo Palermo · Giulia Chiappetta

Married Carmina Spizzirri

Parent of Francesco Giacomo Palermo · Aquila Palermo

From four words to two generations

For months everything known about him fitted into four words. On 14 May 1867 two neighbours from Rende with village trades — Pasquale Sacco, a carter, and Costantino Perugini, a tailor — reported the death of his son Giacomo Palermo, a shepherd. The parentage of the deceased was settled like this: «figlio del fu Giuseppe». A name, and nothing else. No mother, no village, no age.

In August 2026 his own record turned up, and it is one of those that change a whole tree. He died on 6 March 1863 at eight in the morning, in his own house at Marano Marchesato, and record 24 puts him at NINETY YEARS OLD: the greatest age in this entire genealogy. And it names his parents — LORENZO PALERMO and GIULIA CHIAPPETTA, both already dead — so the line climbs at a stroke to people born around 1740-1750, thirty years before civil registration existed in Calabria.

The trade comes and goes with the years, like his son's: bracciale in June 1821, when he registers his daughter Aquila's birth; pastore in December 1838, when he marries her off; and bracciale again when he dies. The only known address of this family comes from that 1821 record: contrada Malvitani, at Marano.

One discrepancy remains unresolved, and it is better not to paper over it. The ninety years of 1863 place his birth around 1773; but in 1821, declaring for himself, he said he was forty, which would give 1781. Eight years apart. The 1821 figure comes from his own mouth and the 1863 one from two neighbours, so the first is probably the sound one.

What the manuscript says

…è morto nella propria sua casa GIUSEPPE PALERMO sposo di Carmina Spizzirri, nato in Marano medesimo, figlio di fu Lorenzo di professione ——— domiciliato ———, e di fu Giulia Chiappetta domiciliata ——— di anni novanta di professione Bracciale domiciliato ivi.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and sixty-three, on the SIXTH day of the month of MARCH, at sixteen hours, before Us, COSTANTINO BELMONTE, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared GIOVANNI CALDARAZZO, aged forty-three, by trade a PINTORE, of the kingdom, resident at Marano, and GAETANO ZICCARELLI, aged fifty, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident in the same place.

Who declared that on the SIXTH day of the month of MARCH of the current year, at EIGHT hours, there died IN HIS OWN HOUSE GIUSEPPE PALERMO, HUSBAND OF CARMINA SPIZZIRRI, born in the said Marano, SON OF THE LATE LORENZO — by trade (blank), resident (blank) — AND OF THE LATE GIULIA CHIAPPETTA, resident (blank), AGED NINETY, by trade a BRACCIALE, resident in the same place.

In compliance with the laws we proceeded together with the said witnesses to the deceased person and ascertained his actual death. We thereafter drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers, and having read it to the informants, it was signed by Us on the day, month and year stated above, while the witnesses declared that they COULD NOT WRITE.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE RECORD THAT ADDS A GENERATION ON THE PALERMO SIDE. The death of his son Francesco Giacomo (Rende, 1867, record 35) broke off at «figlio del fu Giuseppe» and did not name the mother. Here, by contrast, the Marano form fills in both boxes: LORENZO PALERMO and GIULIA CHIAPPETTA, both already dead in 1863. It confirms point by point what the blog index gave.
  • NINETY YEARS OLD. It is the highest age in the whole genealogy and places his birth c. 1773, in the full Bourbon Kingdom of Naples, thirty-six years before civil registration existed. His certificate of baptism, if it survives, is in the parish of S. Maria del Carmine of Marano.
  • THE TRADE CHANGES, and it is not an error. In 1838 his daughter Rosalia's marriage record calls him PASTORE; here, twenty-five years later, BRACCIALE. At ninety he would no longer be out with the flock, and «bracciale» is the default category of the Marano clerk. His son Giacomo was a pecoraio (shepherd) all his life, which is the same as pastore.
  • WHAT IDENTIFIES THIS MAN AND NO OTHER is «sposo di Carmina Spizzirri». In Marano at least eight distinct Palermo families live side by side; the forename alone is worth nothing.
  • The first informant, Giovanni Caldarazzo, is PINTORE — painter. It is an uncommon trade in these registers, where almost everybody is bracciale or contadino.
Ancestor

Carmina Spizzirri

A countrywoman of Marano Marchesato, wife of Giuseppe Palermo. She was born around 1789 and died a widow on 11 October 1864. Her death record was sought precisely to get her parents out of it, and it is the one document in the tree that leaves those two boxes deliberately blank; her age is fixed better by the birth of her daughter Aquila in 1821 than by that record.

Married Giuseppe Palermo

Parent of Francesco Giacomo Palermo · Aquila Palermo

The record that was sought and came back blank

Her death record was sought for a precise reason: it was the one place in the world where her parents' names could appear. The Calabrian blog's index did not give them, and above 1809 there is no civil registration in Calabria.

The record turned up — Marano Marchesato, 12 October 1864, record 74 — and the answer is negative. The printed form says «figlia del fu ———» and «della fu ———», with both names left blank. It is the only document in the whole tree that leaves those two boxes deliberately empty. All it proves is that by October 1864 her father and mother were both dead.

And it is not a gap in the reading. On 29 August 2026 it was checked by a second route: the Marano death index for 1864, transcribed by someone else, gives in the row for record 74 only «Spizzirri» in the parents column, with no names, exactly as the original does. The gaps are in the paper.

What the record does give, and it is not nothing, is her identity. The spouse column says «Giuseppe Palermo», and that pins her down beyond error — because in that same index there is ANOTHER Carmina Spizzirri, married to a Giuseppe Pellegrino. She died on 11 October 1864 at eleven in the morning, at home, after eighteen months of widowhood.

Her age, on the other hand, is better fixed by another document. The 1864 record puts her at seventy, which would give 1794; but in June 1821 her husband declared her thirty-two, which gives 1789. For her parents' names all that is left are the Marano parish books before 1809, or her marriage record.

How it was located

What the manuscript says

…è morta nella propria sua casa CARMINA SPIZZIRRI vedova di Giuseppe Palermo, nata in Marano medesimo, figlia del fu ————— di professione ——— domiciliato ———, e della fu ————— domiciliata ——— di anni settanta di professione Contadina domiciliata ivi.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and sixty-four, on the TWELFTH day of the month of OCTOBER, at fifteen hours, before Us, GIOACCHINO CONFORTI, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared GIULIO DE LUCA, aged forty, by trade an ARMIERE, of the kingdom, resident at Marano, and VINCENZO BELMONTE, aged thirty-three, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident in the same place.

Who declared that on the ELEVENTH day of the month of OCTOBER of the current year, at ELEVEN hours, there died IN HER OWN HOUSE CARMINA SPIZZIRRI, WIDOW OF GIUSEPPE PALERMO, born in the said Marano, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE (name blank) — by trade (blank), resident (blank) — AND OF THE LATE (name blank), resident (blank), AGED SEVENTY, by trade a CONTADINA, resident in the same place.

In compliance with the laws we proceeded together with the said witnesses to the deceased person and ascertained her actual death. We thereafter drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers, and having read it to the informants, it was signed by Us on the day, month and year stated above, while the witnesses declared that they could not write.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS RECORD WAS APPROACHED FOR THE PARENTS, AND THEY ARE NOT THERE. It was the only place where they could appear, because the blog index does not give them. The answer is negative, but it is a clean negative and not a gap in the reading: the printed form has both boxes, the clerk wrote both «fu» —«figlia DEL FU...», «e DELLA FU...»— and left both NAMES blank, with the printed rules unfilled. He knew they were dead and did not know what they were called, or the informants did not tell him.
  • THE ONLY THING TO BE GOT out of those two lines is that in October 1864 Carmina's father and mother were already dead. For the names there now remain only the parish books of Marano earlier than 1809 or her marriage record with Giuseppe Palermo, which is earlier than 1812 and therefore also parish.
  • THIS NOTE WAS RESOLVED ON 26 AUGUST 2026 — READ IT KNOWING HOW IT ENDS. The birth record of her daughter AQUILA (Marano, 22 June 1821, record 34) has her husband declare that the mother is THIRTY-TWO years old. That puts her in 1789, not in 1794, and with 1789 she would have been eighteen when Francesco Giacomo was born, not thirteen. The chronological objection that follows collapses. The age of 1821 is far more reliable than that of 1864: it is given by her husband within days of the birth; the seventy of 1864 were given by a gunsmith and a day labourer standing over a corpse.
  • THE AGE OPENS A SERIOUS PROBLEM [text of August 2026, before reading the 1821 record]. Seventy years old in October 1864 makes her born c. 1794. Her husband, ninety when he died in 1863, was born c. 1773: twenty-one years older. And above all, FRANCESCO GIACOMO PALERMO was born c. 1805-1812 according to the ages he himself kept declaring, which would make Carmina between eleven and eighteen. With the other children it fits well —Antonio Benedetto 1812, Francesca 1816, Vincenzo 1818, Aquila 1821, Francesca 1823: from eighteen to twenty-nine— but Francesco Giacomo falls outside or just on the edge.
  • CONSEQUENCE: that Carmina Spizzirri is the MOTHER of Francesco Giacomo can no longer be taken as good. What remains proven is that Giuseppe Palermo was his father («figlio del fu Giuseppe», Rende 1867) and that Giuseppe was married to Carmina. But it is perfectly possible that Francesco Giacomo is from an EARLIER marriage of Giuseppe, who by c. 1807 was already in his thirties. It would be settled by the birth of Francesco Giacomo (Marano, c. 1807, parish) or by Giuseppe's marriage to Carmina.
  • CLOSING OF THAT CONSEQUENCE: with the thirty-two years of 1821, Giuseppe's earlier marriage ceases to be needed. That Carmina is the mother of Francesco Giacomo is still not proven —there is no document naming them together— but it becomes once more the simplest reading, and it no longer clashes with any arithmetic.
  • The first informant, Giulio De Luca, is ARMIERE — gunsmith. Another rare trade in a register where almost all are bracciali.
Ancestor

Vincenzo Volpintesta

In the records: VOLPENTESTA in the Marano records; Volpintesta in those of Rende

A day labourer from Marano Marchesato, father of Carmina Volpintesta. He was born around 1773 and died at home on 19 March 1823, aged fifty, when his daughter Carmina was eight. His death record gives his father's name: Michele.

Child of Michele Volpintesta

Married Teresa Luchetta

Parent of Carmina Volpintesta

Fifty years old, and his father's name

He entered this tree sideways, in a record that was not his. The death record of his son-in-law Giacomo Palermo (Rende, 14 May 1867) said he was the «marito di Carmina Volpintesta fu Vincenzo»: four words written for something else that proved two, that Carmina's father was called Vincenzo and that he was already dead.

In August 2026 his own record turned up and the detour is no longer needed. He died on 19 March 1823 at midday, at his house in Marano Marchesato, aged fifty, when his daughter Carmina was eight. Record 8 gives his trade — bracciale, day labourer — and his father's name: «figlio del FU MICHELE di professione BRACCIALE». The trade was inherited: Michele bracciale, Vincenzo bracciale. The mother, though, is not named: that box was left blank, as in so many others across the district.

There is a detail that makes that record indispensable and no other. At Marano there live at the same time at least four or five Vincenzo Volpintestas — one married to Arcangela Bartelli, another to Rosa Mancino, another the son of Antonio and Francesca — and without a line to tell them apart any of them would do. The one that identifies him is «e sposo di TERESA LUCHETTA».

She outlived him by twenty-eight years, and her own record, of 1851, calls her «vedova di Vincenzo Volpentesta». The marriage is confirmed from both ends.

How it was located
this register carries no alphabetical index. One probe on page 5 gave records 6 and 7, from which comes the straight line record = 2 × page − 4 and record 8 on the following page

What the manuscript says

…è morto nella propria sua casa VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA, nato in Marano suddetto, figlio del fu Michele di professione Bracciale domiciliato ———, e di ————— domiciliata ————— di anni cinquanta di professione Bracciale domiciliato ivi, e sposo di Teresa Luchetta.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and twenty-three, on the NINETEENTH day of the month of MARCH, at eighteen hours, before Us, RAFFAELE RUFFOLO, mayor and registrar of the civil status of the Comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared NICOLA ROSSO, aged forty, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident at Marano, and ANTONIO CAIRO, aged thirty-eight, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident in the same place.

Who declared that on the NINETEENTH day of the month of MARCH of the current year, at TWELVE hours, there died IN HIS OWN HOUSE VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA, born in the said Marano, SON OF THE LATE MICHELE, by trade a BRACCIALE, resident (blank), AND OF (name blank), resident (blank), AGED FIFTY, by trade a BRACCIALE, resident in the same place, AND HUSBAND OF TERESA LUCHETTA.

In compliance with the laws we proceeded together with the said witnesses to the deceased person and ascertained his actual death. We thereafter drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers, and having read it to the informants, it was signed by Us on the day, month and year stated above, and signed with a CROSS by the witnesses, who are unlettered.

What you need to know about this record

  • «E SPOSO DI TERESA LUCHETTA» IS THE LINE THAT IDENTIFIES HIM. In Marano at least four or five Vincenzo Volpintesta live at the same time —one married to Arcangela Bartelli, another to Rosa Mancino, another the son of Antonio and Francesca Morrone, another the son of Ferdinando and Anna Maria Tenuta—. The name on its own is worth nothing; the spouse is. And it fits with Carmina's birth record of 1814, where he himself is the informant and names Teresa Luchetta.
  • THE FATHER, YES: «figlio del FU MICHELE di professione BRACCIALE». It confirms what the index gave and adds the trade, which is inherited: Michele bracciale, Vincenzo bracciale.
  • THE MOTHER, NO. The blog index gave Vincenzo as the son of Michele and of TERESA TENUTA. The record has the box —«e di ————— domiciliata —————»— and the clerk left it empty. Teresa Tenuta therefore remains without any documentary support, and that is why no page has been opened for her in this tree. Beware, moreover, that in the same village there is another Vincenzo Volpintesta, son of Ferdinando and of ANNA MARIA TENUTA: that mother's surname is the same, and the family is not.
  • FIFTY YEARS OLD at death, that is, born c. 1773. The birth record of his daughter Carmina, of May 1814, gave him thirty-eight, which would give 1776. Three years' difference is the normal thing in these registers; neither figure is a census.
  • HE DIED YOUNG AND LEFT CARMINA FATHERLESS AT EIGHT. She was born in May 1814 and he died in March 1823. Teresa Luchetta was left a widow in her thirties and so remained twenty-eight years more, until 1851: in her death record they still call her «vedova di Vincenzo Volpentesta».
  • The heading of the record carries the month CROSSED OUT —the clerk had been carrying «Febbrajo» over from the preceding records— and corrected. The body of the record says MARZO twice, beyond doubt.
  • 29 AUGUST 2026 — A BAPTISM HAS TURNED UP THAT COULD BE HIS, AND WHAT DOES NOT FIT MUST BE TOLD TOO. His father Michele died in RENDE in 1793, so his children had to be baptised there. The baptismal books of the parish of Rende begin in 1731 and are transcribed, year by year, in the Calabrian blog we already had downloaded on disc. They were swept. And in that of 1770 there is this: RENDE, BAPTISMS OF 1770, RECORD 48 — VINCENZO MERCURIO VOLPINTESTA, «Volpintesta, Michele — Tenuta, Teresa». It fits on four counts: the name, the father MICHELE —the only thing his death record says of his parents—, the mother TERESA TENUTA —which is precisely the box that record left blank— and the village. And he has two brothers in the same tables: PIETRO GAETANO (baptisms of 1767, record 13) and GREGORIO GIOACCHINO (baptisms of 1773, record 41), both children of the same couple. The family was in Rende in 1767, in 1770 and in 1773. BUT HIS OWN DEATH RECORD SAYS HE WAS BORN IN MARANO: «nato in Marano suddetto». And the ages do not tally: that of 1823 gives him fifty years (born c. 1773) and the record of his daughter Carmina, of 1814, gives him thirty-eight (born c. 1776). The baptism is of 1770. The two honest readings are: either it is he, and the ages come rounded —they were declared by two illiterate day labourers fifty-three years later, and the «born in Marano» could have been a supposition of the clerk—, or it is an elder brother who died as a child and whose name was repeated, which was a common custom. In favour of the first: in the Rende baptisms from 1774 to 1783, swept one by one, there is NO other child of Michele and Teresa Tenuta nor a second Vincenzo; and of Marano there exist no books earlier than 1809 in any consultable place. IT IS NOT TAKEN AS GOOD. It is a transcription made by someone else, not the book. And the book is not in Antenati —the Rende holding begins in 1809 and this is parish material of 1770—: it is on a FamilySearch film. To check it one must request there the BAPTISMS of the parish of RENDE (Cosenza), YEAR 1770, RECORD 48.
Ancestor

Teresa Luchetta

A countrywoman of Marano Marchesato, wife of Vincenzo Volpentesta and mother of Carmina. She was born around 1779 and died on 6 June 1851, aged seventy-two, after twenty-eight years of widowhood. Until August 2026 she was the only person in the tree without a single document; she now has a record of her own and both parents named.

Child of Gio: Antonio Luchetta · Marianna Conforti

Married Vincenzo Volpintesta

Parent of Carmina Volpintesta

From having nothing to having everything

Her name came from a single place, and that place turned out not to exist. She was credited to a marriage banns record at Marano Marchesato in 1837, for the wedding of her daughter Carmina to Francesco Giacomo Palermo. It was searched for and it is not there: the 1837 register carries an alphabetical index at the end, with its twenty-five weddings one by one, and there is not a single Palermo or Volpintesta; the dossier file for that year, two hundred and thirty-five pages long, corresponds exactly to those twenty-five and leaves no room for a twenty-sixth. The wedding was at Rende, not at Marano.

For a time she was therefore the only person in the tree without a single document. It was even written of her that she was «the only one of the four undocumented forebears still exactly where she was».

And then her record appeared, and it turned out to be the best of the whole batch. Marano Marchesato, 6 June 1851, record 44. She died at eight in the morning in her own house, aged seventy-two, after twenty-eight years of widowhood. In a single paragraph the form gives the spouse — «vedova di Vincenzo Volpentesta» — the age, the trade of contadina and BOTH PARENTS: GIO: ANTONIO LUCHETTA, bracciale, and MARIANNA CONFORTI, both already dead. It is rare for these records to name the mother; this one does.

With that, two more names of the 1740-1760 generation enter the tree, and the woman who had nothing became the one who gives most. Her own birth, around 1779, is now out of reach by the civil route: only the books of the parish of S. Maria del Carmine at Marano would hold it.

Death record

Marano Marchesato · record 44 · 6 June 1851, 15 h (the record); died at 8 h

Archive
Archivio di Stato di Cosenza
Collection

Register
container hash: 5Y46Aky
Page
24 of 51, LEFT-hand column (on the right, record 45)
Handwritten year
«mille ottocento cinquantuno» — agrees with the catalogue
Registrar
Ferdinando Conforti, sindaco — the same man who registered her granddaughter Teresa Palermo in January 1852
How it was located
a probe on page 23 = records 42 and 43, with the order number printed in the heading; 44 falls on page 24

What the manuscript says

…è morta nella propria sua casa TERESA LUCHETTA, nata in Marano suddetto, figlia del fu Gio: Antonio di professione Bracciale domiciliato ———, e della fu Marianna Conforti domiciliata ——— di anni settantadue di professione Contadina domiciliata ivi, vedova di Vincenzo Volpentesta.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and fifty-one, on the SIXTH day of the month of JUNE, at fifteen hours, before Us, FERDINANDO CONFORTI, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared IGNAZIO CONFORTI, aged fifty, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident there, and GIUSEPPE ZUPPO, aged twenty-eight, by trade a BRACCIALE, of the kingdom, resident in the same place.

Who declared that on the SIXTH day of the month of JUNE of the current year, at EIGHT hours, there died IN HER OWN HOUSE TERESA LUCHETTA, born in the said Marano, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE GIO: ANTONIO, by trade a BRACCIALE, resident (blank), AND OF THE LATE MARIANNA CONFORTI, resident (blank), AGED SEVENTY-TWO, by trade a CONTADINA, resident in the same place, WIDOW OF VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA.

In compliance with the laws we proceeded together with the said witnesses to the deceased person and ascertained her actual death. We thereafter drew up the present record, which we entered in both registers, and having read it to the informants, it was signed by Us on the day, month and year stated above. The witnesses declared that they COULD NOT WRITE.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE BEST OF THE FIVE RECORDS IN THIS BATCH. It gives the spouse, both parents, the age and the trade, all in the same paragraph, and confirms point by point what until now was a line in a blog index without any support.
  • FROM HAVING NOTHING TO HAVING EVERYTHING. In August 2026 it had been written of her that she was «the only one of the four ancestors without papers who remains exactly as she was» and that «of Teresa Luchetta there is today absolutely nothing, only a name inherited from a blog index». This record cancels all of that.
  • «FIGLIA DEL FU GIO: ANTONIO». The record writes the common abbreviation of GIOVANNI ANTONIO, not a plain «Antonio» as the index gave. And it gives him a trade: bracciale.
  • «E DELLA FU MARIANNA CONFORTI». The mother, with forename and surname. That a Marano record should name the mother is not a sure thing: that of her husband Vincenzo, of 1823, left that box blank, and that of Carmina Spizzirri, of 1864, left both blank.
  • IT TALLIES BY A SECOND ROUTE. The index of Marano deaths of 1834, record 17, brings an ANTONIO LUCHETTA «di Antonio e Conforti Marianna», married to Caterina Cosentino: a brother of hers. Two independent documents giving the same marriage of parents.
  • SEVENTY-TWO YEARS OLD, that is, born c. 1779. The birth record of her daughter Carmina, of May 1814, gave her thirty-one years, which would give 1783. Four years' difference, within the ordinary. She was widowed at about forty-four and lived as a widow twenty-eight years more.
  • She died seven months before the birth of her great-granddaughter Teresa Palermo, who was registered by the same mayor, Ferdinando Conforti, in January 1852.
Ancestor

Raffaele Chiappetta

A day labourer born in Castrolibero around 1805. Father of Giovanni Chiappetta and therefore great-grandfather of the Argentine Salernos. He died a widower at Rende, contrada Surdo, on 3 December 1880, aged seventy-five, one year less twelve days after his wife.

Child of Pietro Chiappetta

Married Teresa Molinaro

Parent of Giovanni Chiappetta

Eleven months a widower

He was born at Castrolibero around 1805 — his death record's seventy-five years say so — and he is the earliest-born ancestor this line reaches by document. A day labourer, like his father Pietro and like almost every man in the tree.

His wife, Teresa Molinaro, died on 19 December 1879 in the contrada Surdo at Rende. He died in the same contrada on 3 December 1880, at three in the afternoon, eleven and a half months later; the record calls him «vedovo di Molinaro Teresa». Two village day labourers went to report it, Bruno Salvatore, thirty, and Stellato Andrea, thirty-seven. The witnesses were a stonemason of sixty-six and a day labourer of thirty-seven. Nobody signed but the registrar, Vincenzo Martino.

The record names his father — «da fu Pietro, bracciante, domiciliato in detto Castrolibero» — and leaves the mother's box blank. Of his children, the documented one is Giovanni, born around 1848; probably also a Pasquale who in 1883 declares himself twenty-eight, and the Vincenzo and Pietro Chiappetta who appear as witnesses on family records. That last is inference from ages and surname, not proof.

What the manuscript says

…è morto Chiappetta Raffaele di anni settantacinque, di bracciante, residente in
Rende, nato in Castrolibero, da fu Pietro, bracciante, domiciliato in detto
Castrolibero, e da fu ————, domiciliata in ————, vedovo di Molinaro Teresa.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and eighty, on the fourth day of December, at ten in the morning, at the town hall.

Before me, MARTINO VINCENZO, senior assessor for the mayor, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared BRUNO SALVATORE, aged thirty, bracciante, resident at Rende, and STELLATO ANDREA, aged thirty-seven, bracciante, resident at Rende.

Who declared to me that at three in the afternoon YESTERDAY, in the house situated in CONTRADA SURDO, there died CHIAPPETTA RAFFAELE, AGED SEVENTY-FIVE, by trade a BRACCIANTE (day labourer), resident at Rende, BORN AT CASTROLIBERO, SON OF THE LATE PIETRO, bracciante, resident in the said Castrolibero, AND OF THE LATE ———— (the name is left blank), WIDOWER OF MOLINARO TERESA.

Present at this act as witnesses were ROSSA LUIGI (uncertain reading), aged sixty-six, MURATORE (stonemason), and ROSSA GIUSEPPE, aged thirty-seven, bracciante, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, I signed it alone, they being illiterate. — Vincenzo Martino.

What you need to know about this record

  • THE AGE, WHICH IS WHAT WAS BEING SOUGHT. Seventy-five years old on 3 December 1880: he was born c. 1805, most likely in 1805 and not later. He is the earliest-born ancestor reached by this line after Giuseppe Palermo.
  • HE WAS BORN IN CASTROLIBERO. It was suspected from the marriage of his son Giovanni, who declared he had been born there; the record confirms it directly for the father. The Chiappetta family is from Castrolibero and only afterwards comes down to Rende.
  • THE FATHER: PIETRO CHIAPPETTA, «fu Pietro, bracciante, domiciliato in detto Castrolibero». The index already gave him and the original record confirms it, and it adds besides his trade and that he lived in Castrolibero. The «fu» says he had died before December 1880.
  • THE MOTHER IS STILL WITHOUT A NAME. The form has her box —«e da ______ domiciliata in ______»— and the clerk wrote only «fu» and left the name blank. It is not a slip in the reading: on the father's line, in the same breath, he did put name and trade. The datum does not exist in the record. All that can be got out of it is that she too had already died.
  • «VEDOVO DI MOLINARO TERESA». He died a widower: his wife had gone ahead of him by eleven and a half months, on 19 December 1879. And the surname is written MOLINARO, not «Mulinaro».
  • The two of them lived in CONTRADA SURDO, the same in which Carmina Ricchio will die three years later. The two families joined by their children's marriage were neighbours.
Ancestor

Teresa Molinaro

A countrywoman born in Marano Principato around 1819, mother of Giovanni Chiappetta. She died at Rende, contrada Surdo, on 19 December 1879, aged sixty. Her death was reported by her son's father-in-law, Domenico Massei.

Child of Carmine Molinaro · Carmina Covello

Married Raffaele Chiappetta

Parent of Giovanni Chiappetta

Reported by her son's father-in-law

She died on 19 December 1879, at one in the afternoon, in the contrada Surdo at Rende, aged sixty. She had been born around 1819 at Marano Principato, a village that did not figure in this tree until that reading.

The man who went to the town hall to report it, at ten the following morning, was neither a son nor an ordinary neighbour: it was Massei Domenico, a day labourer, the father of Angela Massei — that is, the father-in-law of her son Giovanni. His age is written in ink so faint that all one can read is «fifty-something». With him was a sixty-eight-year-old day labourer whose surname probably reads De Rango: the same foundling surname Michele Salerno used at Castrolibero, though nothing proves any kinship.

The record spells MOLINARO, with an o, as do the marginal heading and the register's annual index. And it leaves both parents' boxes blank, so the names the indexes give — Carmine Molinaro and Carmina Covello — remain unsupported. Her husband outlived her by eleven and a half months and died in the same contrada.

What the manuscript says

…è morta Molinaro Teresa di anni sessanta, contadina, residente in Rende, nata in
Marano Principato, da fu ————, domiciliato in ————, e dalla fu ————,
domiciliata in ————, moglie di Chiappetta Raffaele.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and seventy-nine, on the twentieth day of December, at ten in the morning, at the town hall.

Before me, MARTINO VINCENZO, senior assessor for the mayor, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared MASSEI DOMENICO, aged some fifty-odd years (the figure is written in very faint ink), BRACCIALE, resident at Rende, and DE RANGO GASPARE (probable reading), aged sixty-eight, bracciale, resident at Rende.

Who declared to me that at one in the afternoon YESTERDAY, in the house situated in CONTRADA SURDO, there died MOLINARO TERESA, AGED SIXTY, CONTADINA (countrywoman), resident at Rende, BORN AT MARANO PRINCIPATO, daughter of the LATE ———— and of the LATE ———— (both names are left blank), WIFE OF CHIAPPETTA RAFFAELE.

Present at this act as witnesses were CHIANELLO GAETANO, aged fifty, bracciale, and BENA SERAFINO (uncertain reading), aged thirty, bracciale, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, I signed it alone, they being illiterate. — Vincenzo Martino.

What you need to know about this record

  • SIXTY YEARS OLD IN DECEMBER 1879: born c. 1819. She had Giovanni at about twenty-nine.
  • SHE WAS BORN IN MARANO PRINCIPATO, not in Castrolibero. It is a new village in this tree, and it should not be confused with Marano Marchesato, where the Palermo came from: they are two distinct neighbouring municipalities. Curiously Carmina Ricchio, the other grandmother, was also born in Marano Principato.
  • THE RECORD NAMES NEITHER OF HER PARENTS. The index gave Carmine Mulinaro and Carmina Covello; the original confirms neither the one nor the other. In both boxes the clerk wrote «fu» and left the name blank. So those two names still rest on the index alone.
  • WHAT IT DOES CONTRIBUTE: that double «fu» says that in December 1879 both her parents were already dead. If she was born c. 1819, her parents would be of c. 1790-1795.
  • THE SURNAME IS MOLINARO. So it is written by the body of the record, by the marginal heading («Molinaro Teresa») and by the annual index of the register itself. The «Mulinaro» of the modern index is a distortion. It agrees with the reading of her son's marriage in 1873.
  • THE FIRST INFORMANT IS THE DAUGHTER'S FATHER-IN-LAW ON THE OTHER SIDE. The man who goes to the town hall to report the death is MASSEI DOMENICO — the father of Angela Massei, whose daughter had married the deceased woman's son six years earlier. It is the proof that the two families lived side by side and that the kinship given by the index is real. He himself will die in 1891 and his record is also here.
  • The second informant reads DE RANGO GASPARE. De Rango is precisely the foundling surname under which the Salerno were registered in Castrolibero. It may be coincidence —it is an extremely common surname in the area— but it is noted.
Ancestor

Domenico Massei

In the records: Mazzei Domenico in his last two records

A day labourer BORN IN RENDE around 1821, father of Angela Massei. He was widowed in 1883 and died a widower on 8 November 1891, in contrada Saporito, aged seventy. One of the two witnesses on his record is his son-in-law, Giovanni Chiappetta.

Child of Filippo Massei

Married Carmina Ricchio

Parent of Angela Massei

The witness was his son-in-law

He was born at Rende around 1821 and died there on 8 November 1891, at two in the afternoon, in the contrada Saporito, aged seventy. He had been widowed eight years earlier of Carmina Ricchio, and his record calls him «vedovo di».

The two fathers-in-law buried one another, and the records set it down. In December 1879 it was Domenico who reported the death of Teresa Molinaro, his son-in-law's mother. In December 1883 it was the son-in-law, Giovanni Chiappetta, who reported that of Carmina Ricchio, his own mother-in-law. And in November 1891, on Domenico's own record, one of the two witnesses is «Giovanni Chiappetta, di anni quarantatre, bracciante»: the same man again.

Of the father, the record gives name and trade: «dal fu Filippo, bracciante, domiciliato in Rende». Of the mother, only the gap — but with a detail the other three records of that generation lack: the clerk left the name blank and still filled in «domiciliata in Rende».

The surname changes from clerk to clerk and not from man to man: MASSEI in 1873 and 1879, MAZZEI in 1883 and 1891. The tree keeps Massei, which is the form used in the records of the branch that lives on.
