What the manuscript says

…è morto Mazzei Domenico di anni settanta, bracciante, residente in Rende, nato in
Rende, dal fu Filippo, bracciante, domiciliato in Rende, e dalla fu ————,
domiciliata in Rende — Vedovo di Ricchio Carmina.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-one, on the ninth day of November, at eleven in the morning, at the town hall.

Before me, MERCAVALLO GABRIELE (uncertain reading), Secretary delegated by act of the Mayor of the nineteenth of September eighteen hundred and eighty-four, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared RICCHIO ANTONIO, aged fifty-six, bracciante, resident at Rende, and TURCO PIETRO, aged twenty-six, bracciante, resident at Rende.

Who declared to me that at two in the afternoon YESTERDAY, in the house situated in CONTRADA SAPORITO, there died MAZZEI DOMENICO, AGED SEVENTY, BRACCIANTE, resident at Rende, BORN AT RENDE, SON OF THE LATE FILIPPO, bracciante, resident at Rende, and of the LATE ———— (the name is left blank), who had been resident at Rende. WIDOWER OF RICCHIO CARMINA.

Present at this act as witnesses were TURCO PASQUALE, aged thirty-seven, bracciante, and GIOVANNI CHIAPPETTA, aged forty-three, bracciante, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, I signed it alone, the others being illiterate. — Gabriele Mercavallo.

What you need to know about this record

  • SEVENTY YEARS OLD IN NOVEMBER 1891: born c. 1821.
  • HE WAS BORN IN RENDE, and this corrects what had been supposed. He was taken for a man of Castrolibero because his daughter Angela was born there; but he is from Rende. The family moved: he is born in Rende, his daughter is born in Castrolibero c. 1858, and in 1873 they are all in Rende again.
  • THE FATHER: FILIPPO, bracciante, resident in Rende. The index gave him and the record confirms it, adding trade and village. The «fu» says he had already died by 1891.
  • THE MOTHER, ONCE AGAIN WITHOUT A NAME — but with a detail the other three records do not give: the clerk left the name blank AND YET filled in the following box, «domiciliata in Rende». He knew where she had lived and did not know what she was called. She too had already died.
  • THE WITNESS IS HIS SON-IN-LAW. «Giovanni Chiappetta, di anni quarantatre, bracciante»: he is the husband of his daughter Angela and the father of Filomena. Forty-three years old in November 1891 places him in 1848, exactly where his marriage of 1873 (25 years) and Filomena's birth in 1877 (29 years) put him. Three independent records, the same date.
  • THE FIRST INFORMANT IS A RICCHIO: Ricchio Antonio, aged fifty-six. Almost certainly family of his late wife.
  • THE SURNAME IS WRITTEN HERE MAZZEI, with a well-marked double z, just as in his wife's record of 1883. By contrast in his daughter's marriage of 1873 and in Teresa Molinaro's record of 1879 —both in the hand of Vincenzo Martino— it reads MASSEI. There is no «correct» spelling: it depends on the clerk. MASSEI is kept in the tree because it is the form used by the records of the branch that stays alive (1873, 1877, 1894).
  • HE DIED IN CONTRADA SAPORITO, not in Surdo, where he lived in 1883. It is the same contrada in which his great-grandson Giuseppe will be born in 1895 and his great-grandson Umberto in 1897.
Ancestor

Carmina Ricchio

A countrywoman born in Marano Principato around 1828, mother of Angela Massei. She died at Rende, contrada Surdo, on 11 December 1883, aged fifty-five. The man who went to report her death was her son-in-law, Giovanni Chiappetta.

Child of Giuseppe Ricchio · Maria Bosco

Married Domenico Massei

Parent of Angela Massei

Four Chiappettas on her record

She died on 11 December 1883, at noon, in the contrada Surdo at Rende, aged fifty-five. Of Filomena's four grandparents she is the one who died youngest. She had been born around 1828 at Marano Principato.

Her record is a gathering of Chiappettas: it is reported by Giovanni Chiappetta, thirty-three — her son-in-law — and Pasquale Chiappetta, twenty-eight; and the witnesses are Vincenzo Chiappetta, twenty-five, and Pietro Chiappetta, fifty. That Pasquale fits as a brother of Giovanni, another son of Raffaele and Teresa born around 1855. It is a lead to follow, not a proof.

Of her father the record says «da fu Giuseppe, bracciante, domiciliato in vita in Marano Principato»; of her mother, nothing. None of those present signed: only the registrar, Vincenzo Bruno, who also spells the husband's surname with a double z, Mazzei.

Three of Filomena's four grandparents died in the same contrada Surdo and in three Decembers: Teresa Molinaro on 19 December 1879, Raffaele Chiappetta on 3 December 1880 and she on 11 December 1883.

What the manuscript says

…è morta Ricchio Carmina di anni cinquantacinque, contadina, residente in Rende,
nata in Marano Principato, da fu Giuseppe, bracciante, domiciliato in vita in
Marano Principato, e dalla fu ————, domiciliata in ————, moglie di Mazzei Domenico.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and eighty-three, on the twelfth day of December, at eleven in the morning, at the town hall.

Before me, BRUNO VINCENZO, acting assessor for the mayor, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared CHIAPPETTA GIOVANNI, aged thirty-three, bracciante, resident at Rende, and CHIAPPETTA PASQUALE, aged twenty-eight, bracciante, resident at Rende.

Who declared to me that at noon YESTERDAY, in the house situated in CONTRADA SURDO, there died RICCHIO CARMINA, AGED FIFTY-FIVE, CONTADINA (countrywoman), resident at Rende, BORN AT MARANO PRINCIPATO, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE GIUSEPPE, bracciante, who was resident in life at Marano Principato, and of the LATE ———— (the name is left blank), WIFE OF MAZZEI DOMENICO.

Present at this act as witnesses were CHIAPPETTA VINCENZO, aged twenty-five, bracciante, and CHIAPPETTA PIETRO, aged fifty, bracciante, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, I signed it alone, the others being illiterate. — Vincenzo Bruno.

What you need to know about this record

  • FIFTY-FIVE YEARS OLD IN DECEMBER 1883: born c. 1828. She is the youngest of the four grandparents and the one who died earliest in age. She had Angela at about thirty.
  • SHE WAS BORN IN MARANO PRINCIPATO, just like Teresa Molinaro. Two of Filomena's four great-grandparents come out of the same village, which until now did not appear in this tree.
  • THE FATHER: GIUSEPPE RICCHIO, bracciante, «domiciliato in vita in Marano Principato». The index gave him and no more; the record gives him a trade and a village, and with the «fu» and the «in vita» makes clear that he had died and that he never moved from Marano.
  • THE MOTHER, WITHOUT A NAME. Third time out of four: the clerk writes «e dalla fu» and leaves the rest blank. There is no name to rescue. — RESOLVED ON 26 AUGUST 2026, and not here but in another file: the mother was MARIA BOSCO, and her own death record (Castrolibero, 11 August 1865, record 11) names her and identifies her as «vedova del fu Giuseppe Ricchio». That «dalla fu» of 1883 was true: she had been dead eighteen years.
  • THE RECORD IS A GATHERING OF CHIAPPETTAS. Both informants and both witnesses bear that surname. The first informant is CHIAPPETTA GIOVANNI, her son-in-law, the husband of Angela and father of Filomena. The second, CHIAPPETTA PASQUALE, aged twenty-eight, is almost certainly a brother of Giovanni: he fits as one more son of Raffaele and Teresa, born c. 1855. And among the witnesses, Chiappetta Vincenzo (25) and Chiappetta Pietro (50). The son-in-law's family buried the mother-in-law.
  • HERE THE HUSBAND'S SURNAME IS WRITTEN MAZZEI, with the unmistakable double z, whereas the marriage of 1873 wrote MASSEI. It is the same man.
  • Her husband survived her by eight years: he died on 8 November 1891, «vedovo di Ricchio Carmina», and his record is also in this archive.

Registrar
Francesco Falcucci (uncertain reading: «Falcuni»), municipal assessor delegated by the mayor by an act of 24 November 1889
How it was located
by the alphabetical index at the end of the register (page 168 of 170), where a single Pizzi appears: «Pizzi Domenico — 238». A probe on page 120 gave records 235-236, dated 28 September: 238 was on the following page

What the manuscript says

Numero 238 — Domenico Pizzi
L'anno mille ottocento novantuno, addì ventinove di Settembre, a ore pomeridiane
quattro e minuti dieci, nella Casa Comunale. Avanti di me Francesco Falcucci
assessore Municipale delegato dal Sindaco con atto del ventiquattro Novembre
mille ottocento ottantanove, debitamente approvato, Ufficiale dello Stato Civile
del Comune di Atessa, sono comparsi Donato Tiroli, di anni settanta, contadino,
domiciliato in Atessa, e Tommaso D'Amelio, di anni settantatre, contadino,
domiciliato in Atessa, i quali mi hanno dichiarato che a ore pomeridiane otto e
minuti cinque di ieri, nella casa posta in Via di Mezzo al numero ——, è morto
DOMENICO PIZZI, di anni sessantasette, muratore, residente in Atessa, nato in
Atessa, da fu GIUSEPPE, artigiano, domiciliato in vita in Atessa, e da fu ANTONIA
DI IORIO, filatrice, domiciliata in vita in Atessa, marito di TERESA FLOCCO.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-one, on the twenty-ninth day of September, at ten past four in the afternoon, at the town hall.

Before me, FRANCESCO FALCUCCI, municipal assessor delegated by the Mayor by act of the twenty-fourth of November eighteen hundred and eighty-nine, duly approved, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of ATESSA, there appeared DONATO TIROLI, aged seventy, farmhand, resident at Atessa, and TOMMASO D'AMELIO, aged seventy-three, farmhand, resident at Atessa.

Who declared to me that at FIVE PAST EIGHT YESTERDAY EVENING, in the house situated in VIA DI MEZZO, there died DOMENICO PIZZI, AGED SIXTY-SEVEN, STONEMASON, resident at Atessa, BORN AT ATESSA, SON OF THE LATE GIUSEPPE, artisan, who was resident in life at Atessa, and of the LATE ANTONIA DI IORIO, spinner, who was resident in life at Atessa. HUSBAND OF TERESA FLOCCO.

Present at this act as witnesses were FILIPPO FEDELIBUS, aged fifty-one, farmhand, and GIACOMO PASQUINI (uncertain reading), aged forty-eight, farmhand, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, I signed it alone, the informants and the witnesses having declared that they could not write. — Francesco Falcucci.

What you need to know about this record

  • SIXTY-SEVEN YEARS OLD IN SEPTEMBER 1891: born c. 1824. It is just what was expected of him, and it fits with a daughter born in 1869, when he would be forty-five.
  • THE RECORD DOES NAME HIS MOTHER, and that was the question this download brought with it. «E da FU ANTONIA DI IORIO, FILATRICE, domiciliata IN VITA in Atessa». Full name, trade and village. A new maternal branch —DI IORIO— enters the tree along this line.
  • THE FATHER: GIUSEPPE, artigiano, resident in his lifetime in Atessa. The «fu» and the «in vita» say that he had died and that he never moved from the village. He is exactly the same Giuseppe whom the 1869 record names when presenting the informant as «Domenico Pizzi FU Giuseppe»: two records twenty-two years apart giving the same father.
  • «MARITO DI TERESA FLOCCO», not «vedovo». She survived him. Her death would have to be sought in the Morti of Atessa after 1891.
  • HE DIED IN VIA DI MEZZO. In 1869 the family lived in the rione del CASTELLO; twenty-two years later it is in Via di Mezzo, within the same old town.
  • THE DISCREPANCY OF AGE. The 1869 record gives him as thirty-nine years old, which would put him in 1830; this one gives him as sixty-seven, which puts him in 1824. They are his only two records and they do not agree. The usual thing in these registers is for the age declared at a death to be the more rounded, so his birth falls in the window 1824-1830 and it must be sought in the Nati of Atessa of those years.
  • THE INFORMANT IS A TIROLI, just as in the death of Leone Pizzi in 1841 (Luigi Tiroli, aged thirty). Fifty years later, a Donato Tiroli aged seventy is once again the man who goes to the town hall to report the death of a Pizzi. Neighbours from time immemorial.

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY TWO RECORDS, even though she has no folder of her own. In the birth of her daughter (Atessa, 12 February 1869, record 65) the husband declares her: «da sua moglie TERESA FLOCCO FU PASQUALE, d'anni trentacinque, FILATRICE, seco lui domiciliata». And in his own death (Atessa, 28 September 1891, record 238) she figures as his living wife. Both records are downloaded and transcribed in the files of Angela and of Domenico. THE FACT TO BE NOTED is that «fu Pasquale»: her father was called PASQUALE FLOCCO and had already died by February 1869. No file has been opened for him because nothing more is known of him —neither trade nor age nor town, although Flocco is an Atessa surname: in the same register of 1869 there appears a «Signor Filippo Flocco», owner of a farmstead— but it is a new and verified name, of the generation of 1800, and it gives a direction in which to continue: the Morti of Atessa earlier than 1869.

Ancestor

Francesco Giacomo Palermo

In the records: Giacomo Palermo

A shepherd of Marano Marchesato. The records almost always call him plain «Giacomo Palermo». He had at least four children at Marano between 1839 and 1852, moved with the family to Rende and died there on 13 May 1867, aged sixty.

Child of Giuseppe Palermo · Carmina Spizzirri

Married Carmina Volpintesta

Parent of Maria Antonia Palermo · Antonio Palermo · Annunziata Palermo · Teresa Palermo

He began and ended as a shepherd

The records almost always call him plain Giacomo Palermo; the full «Francesco Giacomo» appears only at his daughter's marriage in 1858. He was born at Marano Marchesato around 1807, and that date is an average of figures that do not agree with one another: he declared twenty-seven in 1839, thirty-six in October 1841, thirty-eight in February 1845, forty in January 1852, and his death record puts him at sixty in May 1867. Four of the five fall between 1805 and 1812.

The trade comes and goes with the years: pastore when he registers Antonio in 1841, bracciale in 1845 and 1852, pecoraio — sheep-herder — when he dies. He begins and ends with livestock. The contradas move too: Palazzo in 1841, Langhi in 1845, Cerzi in 1852, all within Marano; and then the whole village, because the family ended up settled at Rende.

He died there on 13 May 1867 at four in the afternoon, at home, in a contrada the record writes as «Nocolitani» and which is at the resolution limit of the scan. The record says «marito di Carmina Volpintesta»: he did not die a widower, she outlived him. And it adds that he was «figlio del fu Giuseppe», without naming the mother.

The man signing the record as registrar was Luigi Perugini, acting assessor. Nine years earlier, as mayor of Rende, it was he who had married his daughter Maria Antonia to Michele Salerno.

Parish extract of baptism

Marano Marchesato · parish of the Carmine · 14 February 1809

Archive
Archivio di Stato di Cosenza
Collection
Stato civile della Restaurazione > Rende > Registro 29 «Matrimoni, processetti» 1837
Register
ark an_ua35976841 · container hash: LPAZyGB (269 images)
Page
225 of 269 — dossier 20, that of his own wedding
Original
book of the baptised of the church of SANTA MARIA DEL MONTE CARMELO of Marano Marchesato. Extract issued by the rector Dominicus Laura on 27 September 1837
Legalisation
F. Spizzirri, sindaco of Marano, and the chancellor Benedetto Conforti
How it was located
inside the dossier of his marriage of 1837, among the documents he had to produce in order to marry

What the manuscript says

Anno Domini Mille Octingentesimo NONO die 14 mensis FEBRUARII Marani, R.D. MATTHAEUS SANTANNA baptizavit infantem natum ex JOSEPHO PALERMO et CARMELA SPIZZIRRI legitimis conjugibus, cui impositum est nomen FRANCISCUS JACOBUS; compater BARBARA SANTANNA.

Translation

I, the undersigned rector of the church of SANTA MARIA DEL MONTE CARMELO of the town of Marano, having consulted the book of the baptised, certify that I have found the following entry:

«IN THE YEAR OF OUR LORD EIGHTEEN HUNDRED AND NINE, ON THE 14th DAY OF THE MONTH OF FEBRUARY, AT MARANO, the reverend don MATEO SANTANNA baptised a boy born of GIUSEPPE PALERMO AND CARMELA SPIZZIRRI, LAWFULLY MARRIED, upon whom was bestowed the name FRANCESCO GIACOMO; godmother, BARBARA SANTANNA.»

Given at Marano, on the fifth of the calends of October 1837. Domingo Laura, rector.

What you need to know about this record

  • THE PIECE THAT HAD BEEN MISSING FROM THE START. His birth is NOT in the civil registration of Marano for 1809: that year was read in full, eighty-four records, and the only two Palermo are the sons of other men. This extract, kept inside his marriage dossier, is probably the only copy that survives outside the parish book.
  • «LEGITIMIS CONJUGIBUS» — lawfully wedded. Giuseppe Palermo and Carmela Spizzirri WERE ALREADY MARRIED in February 1809. Until now it was known only that they were married before 1812, from the first known child. Their marriage must be sought BEFORE 1809, and it is the document that would at last give the parents of Carmina Spizzirri.
  • The twenty-eight years he declares on marrying in October 1837 give 1809. It coincides exactly with this baptism, and with the year the dossier's index attributed to him.
  • The mother appears here as CARMELA, just as in the marriage record of 1837 and contrary to everything else, where she is CARMINA. Two different documents, the same variant: it may be that Carmela was her real baptismal name and Carmina the one in use.

Death record

Rende · record 35 · 13 May 1867, 16 h

Collection
Stato civile italiano > Rende > Registro 59 «Morti» 1867
Register
an_ua35985546 · hash LD1KnvN
Page
11 of 36
Registrar
Luigi Perugini, assessor for the mayor being indisposed
Handwritten year
«milleottocento sessantasette» — agrees with the catalogue

What the manuscript says

…è morto Giacomo Palermo, di anni sessanta, di professione pecoraio, domiciliato a Rende, marito di Carmina Volpintesta fu Vincenzo, nato a Marano Marchesato, figlio del fu Giuseppe.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and sixty-seven, on the fourteenth day of the month of May, at seventeen hours, at the town hall of Rende, circondario of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citeriore.

Before me, LUIGI PERUGINI, acting assessor for the mayor being indisposed, Registrar of the Civil Status of the said comune, there appeared PASQUALE SACCO, son of the late Antonio, aged fifty, by trade a VETTURALE (carter), and COSTANTINO PERUGINI, son of the late Domenico, aged forty-two, by trade a SARTORE (tailor), both resident at Rende.

Who declared to me that on the thirteenth day of the month aforesaid, at sixteen hours, in this comune of Rende, in the house of his dwelling situated in the contrada Nocolitani (uncertain reading), there died GIACOMO PALERMO, AGED SIXTY, by trade a PECORAIO (shepherd), resident at Rende, HUSBAND OF CARMINA VOLPINTESTA, daughter of the late Vincenzo, BORN AT MARANO MARCHESATO, SON OF THE LATE GIUSEPPE.

The record having been read to the informants, who declared that they could not write, I signed it alone. — The Assessor, Luigi Perugini.

What you need to know about this record

  • The record DOES NOT NAME THE MOTHER. It breaks off at «figlio del fu Giuseppe». It was expected to confirm Carmina Spizzirri and it does not. — UPDATE, August 2026: the mother's name no longer depends on this record. It appeared in another, the marriage of Rosalia Palermo (Marano Marchesato, 8 December 1838, record 19), which names the whole couple: Giuseppe Palermo, pastore, and Carmina Spizzirri. What is missing now is not the name, but the proof that Rosalia and Giacomo were brother and sister.
  • 26 AUGUST 2026: THE LAST OBJECTION AGAINST CARMINA SPIZZIRRI FALLS. Her death record (1864) gave her seventy years, that is, born c. 1794, and on that reckoning she would have been THIRTEEN when he was born, c. 1807 — reason enough to stop taking her as his mother and to suspect a first marriage of Giuseppe Palermo. The birth of his sister AQUILA (Marano, 22 June 1821, record 34) has Giuseppe declare that Carmina is THIRTY-TWO years old: born c. 1789, eighteen when Francesco Giacomo was born. The reckoning fits and the earlier marriage ceases to be needed. There is still no direct proof —no document names Carmina and Francesco Giacomo together— but there is nothing against it any more.
  • The two «fu» of the record are worth as much as the rest: Giuseppe Palermo was dead, and Vincenzo Volpintesta, the widow's father, was too.
  • «Marito di», not «vedovo»: Carmina Volpintesta was still alive in May 1867. She was widowed in her early fifties.
  • THE AGE FORCES A CORRECTION OF THE PAGE. Sixty years old in May 1867 places his birth c. 1806-1807, not c. 1812 as had been said on the strength of the 27 years he declared in 1839. The records of his other three children give 36 years in 1841, 38 in 1845 and 40 in 1852: four of the five figures fall between 1805 and 1812, with the median in 1807.
  • The officer who draws up the record is LUIGI PERUGINI, the same man who as mayor of Rende had married his daughter Maria Antonia to Michele Salerno nine years earlier, in August 1858.
  • He died in Rende having been born in Marano Marchesato: the family had already moved, which explains why his daughter's wedding in 1858 was celebrated in Rende.
Ancestor

Carmina Volpintesta

In the records: VOLPENTESTA in the Marano records; Volpintesta in those of Rende

Wife of Giacomo Palermo and mother of Maria Antonia. Born at Marano Marchesato in May 1814, daughter of Vincenzo Volpentesta and Teresa Luchetta. She lost her father at eight and was widowed at Rende in May 1867.

Child of Vincenzo Volpintesta · Teresa Luchetta

Married Francesco Giacomo Palermo

Parent of Maria Antonia Palermo · Antonio Palermo · Annunziata Palermo · Teresa Palermo

Thirty years old, twice

She has no record of her own and yet appears in five: the births of her four children — Maria Antonia in 1839, Antonio in 1841, Annunciata in 1845 and Teresa in 1852 — and her husband's death in 1867.

The ages he declares for her are a good illustration of how little these figures are worth: thirty in October 1841, thirty again in February 1845, and thirty-five in January 1852. They place her somewhere between 1812 and 1817, and no closer.

In May 1867 she was alive: her husband's death record calls him «marito di Carmina Volpintesta», not a widower. And in passing it says «fu Vincenzo», so her own father was by then dead. The surname is written Volpintesta in the Rende records and Volpentesta in the Marano ones. Her birth would have to be sought around 1815; the «of Rende» the starting index gives her is confirmed by no record read.

Birth record

Marano Marchesato · record 41 · May 1814, 14 h — born on the mismo día dthe record

container hash: w1yyOoo
Page
24 of 64, RIGHT-hand column (on the left, record 40)
Handwritten year
the printed form says «L'anno milleottocento TREDICI»; it is struck through and corrected by hand to QUATTORDECI, 1814. It agrees with the catalogue and with record 63 of the same register, of August 1814
Registrar
Francesco Conforti, sindaco
How it was located
this register does NOT follow the straight line of the death registers. Page 41 has a single record, 63; page 27 has two, 46 and 47. In that stretch it runs two to the image and record 41 falls on page 24

What the manuscript says

…è comparso Vincenzo Volpentesta di anni trenta otto di professione Bracciale domiciliato nel detto comune… ed ha dichiarato che il giorno soprain[dicato]… è nato in sua casa da esso dichiarante e da Teresa Luchetta sua moglie legittima di anni trenta una, una femina che ci ha presentato… a cui si è dato il nome di CARMINA.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and FOURTEEN, on the (day) of the month of MAY, at FOURTEEN hours, before Us, FRANCESCO CONFORTI, mayor and registrar of the civil status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA, AGED THIRTY-EIGHT, by trade a BRACCIALE, resident in the said comune.

And he declared that on the DAY STATED ABOVE there was born IN HIS HOUSE, of himself, the informant, and of TERESA LUCHETTA, HIS LAWFUL WIFE, AGED THIRTY-ONE, A GIRL whom he has presented to us, and TO WHOM HAS BEEN GIVEN THE NAME CARMINA.

The presentation and declaration were made in the presence of GIOVANNI VINCIULLO, aged twenty-six, by trade a CONTADINO, resident in the aforesaid place, and of GIOVANNI ROSSO, aged twenty-eight, likewise by trade a CONTADINO, resident in the same contrada, witnesses.

The present record was read both to the informant and to the witnesses, and thereafter signed by Us and by the informant: VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA.

What you need to know about this record

  • HER PARENTAGE NO LONGER DEPENDS ON HER HUSBAND'S RECORD. Until now Carmina was the daughter of Vincenzo only because the death record of Giacomo Palermo (Rende, 1867) called her «Carmina Volpintesta FU VINCENZO», and Teresa Luchetta appeared on no paper at all. Here Vincenzo himself appears before the mayor, declares his age and his trade and names his wife. It is the first-hand source.
  • THE DAY CANNOT BE READ WITH CERTAINTY, and it is best not to pretend otherwise. The number of the day is split by the line break —it ends in «...ce» at the start of the following line— and the piece left on the first line is overlaid by a much later marginal annotation. It reads «D...ce» and by far the most probable is DODECE, twelve. What is certain: it is MAY 1814 and it is LATER THAN THE 10th, because record 40, on the same opening, is of the day «Diece» at sixteen hours, and this one was drawn up at fourteen. The files are dated 1814-05-12 with that reservation.
  • IT GIVES NO CONTRADA. The record says only that the father is «domiciliato nel detto comune» and that the girl was born «in sua casa». This Napoleonic form has no contrada box; those of the Restoration do, and that is why for her own children we know the contrade Langhi and Cerzi.
  • THE PARENTS' AGES. He thirty-eight —born c. 1776— and she thirty-one —c. 1783—. Their death records give fifty years in 1823 (c. 1773) and seventy-two in 1851 (c. 1779). The two pairs of figures differ by three and four years, which is the normal thing: in these registers nobody knew their exact age.
  • VINCENZO SIGNS. At the foot of the record is his signature, «Vincenzo Volpentesta», while the two witnesses are illiterate. A bracciale who can write his name in 1814 is not the usual thing.
  • THE YEAR WAS MISPRINTED. The form came preprinted «L'anno milleottocento TREDICI» —1813— and the clerk struck it out and wrote QUATTORDECI over it. It is the same notebook reused from one year to the next.
  • She was born in May 1814 and her father died in March 1823: she was eight years and ten months old. Her mother, Teresa Luchetta, raised her as a widow and lived until 1851.
Sibling

cover sheet; the groom's certificate (Marano 1810, record 10); THE BRIDE'S CERTIFICATE, which is record 34 of 1821; and the death record of the groom's mother (Marano 1816, record 42)

What the manuscript says

Comune di Marano Marchesato — Atto di solenne promessa di matrimonio tra VINCENZO GAETANO SANTELLI ed AQUILA PALERMO, di detta Comune.

Translation

COVER OF THE DOSSIER: «Comune of Marano Marchesato. Act of solemn promise of marriage between VINCENZO GAETANO SANTELLI and AQUILA PALERMO, of the said comune.» Seen by the mayor Luigi De Filippis, 16 November 1838.

IT CONTAINS FOUR DOCUMENTS:

1. The groom's birth record — Marano, record 10 of 1810: Gennaro Santelli, a bracciale aged 24, of the place called Pascalone, presents a boy born on 5 February 1810 of his lawful wife ANNA MARIA DI BARTOLO, and bestows on him the names VINCENZO GAETANO.

2. THE BRIDE'S BIRTH RECORD — and it is RECORD 34 OF 1821, that of AQUILA: Giuseppe Palermo, aged forty, bracciale, of the contrada Malvitani, presents a girl born of Carmina Spizzirri, aged thirty-two, and gives her the name AQUILA.

3. The death record of the groom's mother — Marano, record 42 of 1816: ANNA MARIA DE BARTOLO, daughter of the late Francesco and wife of Gennaro Santelli, aged twenty-four, contadina, of the street Pascalone, died on 21 August 1816 in her own house. Her death was reported by two neighbours and kinsmen, Domenico and Raffaele Santelli.

4. The notices, posted without opposition.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS DOSSIER IS THE ONE THAT PROVES THAT AQUILA AND ROSALIA ARE THE SAME WOMAN. The cover calls her Aquila. Her birth certificate calls her Aquila. And the marriage record that comes out of this very dossier calls her Rosalia. There are not two sisters: there is one woman with two names, the register's and the household's.
  • And it explains the puzzle that had been open for months: ROSALIA PALERMO does not appear in any register of births of Marano between 1809 and 1826, swept year by year, BECAUSE SHE IS REGISTERED AS AQUILA.
  • The lesson in method, and it is the second time in two days: THE DOSSIER IS WORTH MORE THAN THE RECORD. First it was the Rende one of 1837, which yielded a baptism of 1809 non-existent in the civil registration and a death of 1793. Now this one, which undoes a mistaken identity. Always ask for the processetto.
  • From the bridegroom one gets his whole branch into the bargain: Vincenzo Gaetano Santelli, born 5 February 1810, son of Gennaro Santelli, bracciale, and of Anna Maria de Bartolo, who died aged twenty-four when he was six. They lived at the place called Pascalone.

What the manuscript says

…sono comparsi nella casa comunale VINCENZO GAETANO SANTELLI di anni ventotto, nato in Marano suddetto, di professione bracciale, domiciliato ivi, figlio di GENNARO di professione bracciale domiciliato ivi, e di FU ALMA MARIA DI BARTOLO; e ROSALIA PALERMO di anni diciassette, nata in Marano medesimo, domiciliata ivi, figlia di GIUSEPPE di professione PASTORE domiciliato ivi, e di CARMINA SPIZZIRRI domiciliata col di lei marito…

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and thirty-eight, on the eighth day of the month of December, at twenty-one hours, before Us, LUIGI DE FILIPPIS, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared at the town hall:

VINCENZO GAETANO SANTELLI, AGED TWENTY-EIGHT, born at Marano, by trade a BRACCIALE (day labourer), resident here, son of GENNARO, by trade a bracciale, resident here, and of the LATE ALMA MARIA DI BARTOLO;

and ROSALIA PALERMO, AGED SEVENTEEN, born at Marano itself, resident here, DAUGHTER OF GIUSEPPE, BY TRADE A PASTORE, RESIDENT HERE, AND OF CARMINA SPIZZIRRI, RESIDENT WITH HER HUSBAND.

Who, in the presence of the witnesses named below, requested us to receive their solemn promise to celebrate before the Church, in the forms prescribed by the Holy Council of Trent, the marriage proposed between them. Notice of this promise was posted on the door of the town hall of Marano Marchesato on Sunday the eighteenth of November of the current year.

Documents presented: 1st the birth records of the husband and of the wife; 2nd the death record of ALMA MARIA DI BARTOLO, the husband's mother. It is further established that the husband is under no military obligation, and that husband and wife are unmarried. At the posting of the notices there was no impediment whatever. Finally, THE HUSBAND'S FATHER AND THE WIFE'S PARENTS gave their consent orally.

Four witnesses took part in the solemn promise: ANTONIO CAIRA, aged fifty, bracciale, resident at Marano; GIUSEPPE GENUITA, aged thirty-six, MOLINARO (miller); MICHELE SCOLA, aged fifty, bracciale; and SAVERIO VITIELI, aged twenty-six, bracciale. The contracting parties and the witnesses declared that they COULD NOT WRITE.

[Right-hand column] The parish priest of Marano returned to us one of the copies of the aforesaid promise; at the foot of which he certified that the celebration of the marriage followed on the EIGHTH day of the month of DECEMBER of the current year, in the presence of the witnesses GIUSEPPE MARRONE and GASPARE BARTUCCI.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS IS THE RECORD THAT RESCUES CARMINA SPIZZIRRI. Marano of 1838 was searched for the wedding of Giacomo Palermo to Carmina Volpintesta and it is not there —the index of the year, with its twenty-one weddings, brings no male Palermo nor any Volpentesta—. But in row 19 there was a Palermo bride «di Giuseppe», and the record develops it by naming both parents.
  • Giuseppe Palermo appears ALIVE and with a trade: PASTORE. It is the same trade as his presumed son Giacomo, who was a pastore in 1841 and died a pecoraio in 1867. The coincidence of village, name and trade is what sustains the kinship.
  • Carmina Spizzirri appears ALIVE, «domiciliata col di lei marito». It is the first time her name comes out of an original document.
  • The two «fu» of the record are on the bridegroom's side, not on ours: the dead person is Alma Maria di Bartolo, mother of Vincenzo Santelli. On the Palermo side there is no «fu» at all, and that is how we know that both of Rosalia's parents were alive.
  • WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY, and one must be honest: it does not say that Rosalia is the sister of Francesco Giacomo. Nobody has seen her named in any record of the Rende family. The kinship is an inference by convergence, a very strong one but an inference. It would be closed by the birth of Francesco Giacomo (Marano, c. 1807) or by that of Rosalia herself (Marano, c. 1821): both would name the same couple.
  • Note on resolution: the Marano scans are 2164 × 1900 pixels, which is their original size; the archived crops were requested from the server at those same coordinates and are not rescaled.
  • RECTIFICATION OF 26 AUGUST 2026, IN THE AFTERNOON: AQUILA AND ROSALIA ARE THE SAME WOMAN. In the morning the opposite had been concluded, and with reasoning that seemed sound: record 34 of 1821 imposes ONE SINGLE name, AQUILA, and the parish priest's note and the register's annual table repeat it. From that it was deduced that Rosalia had to be another sister. It was a deduction, not proof, and it was wrong. It was settled by THE DOSSIER OF HER OWN WEDDING —Marano Marchesato, processetti 1838, dossier 19, pages 157 to 162—. The cover says, in so many words: «Atto di solenne promessa di matrimonio tra VINCENZO GAETANO SANTELLI ed AQUILA PALERMO». And the birth certificate the bride presented, sewn inside the bundle, IS RECORD 34 OF 1821, that of Aquila. That is to say: the dossier calls her Aquila, her birth certificate calls her Aquila, and the marriage record that comes out of that same dossier calls her Rosalia. The clerk used the name by which she was known in the village. And that explains once and for all the puzzle that had been open for months: ROSALIA PALERMO DOES NOT APPEAR IN ANY REGISTER OF BIRTHS OF MARANO BETWEEN 1809 AND 1826 —swept year by year— BECAUSE SHE IS REGISTERED AS AQUILA.

What the manuscript says

…è comparso [il Signor, tachado] GIUSEPPE PALERMO di anni QUARANTA di professione BRACCIALE domiciliato in Marano, Contrada Malvitani, e ci ha presentato una bambina; secondo che abbiamo ocularmente riconosciuto, e ci ha dichiarato che la stessa è nata da CARMINA SPIZZIRRI d'anni TRENTADUE domiciliata con esso, e da lui dichiarante di professione come sopra domiciliato come sopra, nel giorno VENTIDUE del mese di sopra detto anno corrente all'ore UNDICI nella Casa di sua abitazione. — Ha in oltre dichiarato di dare alla Bambina il Nome di AQUILA ———

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and twenty-one, on the TWENTY-SECOND day of the month of JUNE, at NINETEEN hours, before Us, FEDELE TURCO, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared GIUSEPPE PALERMO, AGED FORTY, by trade a BRACCIALE (day labourer), resident at Marano, CONTRADA MALVITANI.

And he presented to us a girl, as we ascertained with our own eyes, and declared to us that she was born OF CARMINA SPIZZIRRI, AGED THIRTY-TWO, resident with him, and of himself, the informant, of the trade stated above and resident as above, on the TWENTY-SECOND day of the month aforesaid of the current year, AT ELEVEN HOURS, IN THE HOUSE OF THEIR DWELLING.

HE FURTHER DECLARED THAT HE GAVE THE GIRL THE NAME AQUILA. ———

The aforesaid presentation and declaration were made in the presence of GABRIELE DE SPOSITO (surname reading uncertain), aged forty, by trade a MURATORE (stonemason), resident at Marano, contrada Langhi; and of GIUSEPPE SCOLA, aged sixty, by trade a bracciale, resident at Marano, contrada Palazzo, witnesses who took part in the present act and were chosen by the informant himself.

[Right-hand column] The parish priest of the church of Marano returned to us the notice we had sent him, certifying at the foot that the Sacrament of Baptism was administered to the said girl, CALLED AQUILA PALERMO, on the TWENTY-SECOND day of the month indicated.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS RECORD WAS APPROACHED TO ASK IT WHETHER SHE WAS ROSALIA, AND THE ANSWER IS NO. The hypothesis was reasonable: in these registers two or three forenames are often imposed and the index picks up only one, so «Aquila Rosalia» would have closed at a stroke the birth of Rosalia Palermo, the bride of 1838. The record says AQUILA and nothing more: the rule on the printed form is filled with a long stroke that leaves no room for a second name. RECTIFICATION OF 26 AUGUST 2026, IN THE AFTERNOON: AQUILA AND ROSALIA ARE THE SAME WOMAN. In the morning the opposite had been concluded, and with reasoning that seemed sound: record 34 of 1821 imposes ONE SINGLE name, AQUILA, and the parish priest's note and the register's annual table repeat it. From that it was deduced that Rosalia had to be another sister. It was a deduction, not proof, and it was wrong. It was settled by THE DOSSIER OF HER OWN WEDDING —Marano Marchesato, processetti 1838, dossier 19, pages 157 to 162—. The cover says, in so many words: «Atto di solenne promessa di matrimonio tra VINCENZO GAETANO SANTELLI ed AQUILA PALERMO». And the birth certificate the bride presented, sewn inside the bundle, IS RECORD 34 OF 1821, that of Aquila. That is to say: the dossier calls her Aquila, her birth certificate calls her Aquila, and the marriage record that comes out of that same dossier calls her Rosalia. The clerk used the name by which she was known in the village. And that explains once and for all the puzzle that had been open for months: ROSALIA PALERMO DOES NOT APPEAR IN ANY REGISTER OF BIRTHS OF MARANO BETWEEN 1809 AND 1826 —swept year by year— BECAUSE SHE IS REGISTERED AS AQUILA.
  • AND THEY CANNOT BOTH BE OF 1821. Aquila was born on 22 June; a whole sister fits neither before nor after within the same calendar year. Rosalia, who declared seventeen years in December 1838, must be of 1820 — her seventeen fit just as well with a birth at the end of that year. The register of NATI 1820 of Marano Marchesato is the immediate objective.
  • THE AGE OF CARMINA SPIZZIRRI, WHICH WAS WHAT WAS MOST NEEDED. «nata da CARMINA SPIZZIRRI D'ANNI TRENTADUE»: thirty-two years old in June 1821, that is, born c. 1789. Her death record of 1864 gave her seventy, that is, 1794, and on that reckoning she would have been thirteen when Francesco Giacomo was born (c. 1807) — the son through whom this family descends. With 1789 she would be eighteen, and the objection collapses. The five years' difference between the two declarations is entirely ordinary, but that of 1821 is far more reliable: it is given by her own husband within days of the birth, and not by a few neighbours over a corpse forty-three years later.
  • NOTE: this does not PROVE that Francesco Giacomo is Carmina's son. There is still no document naming them together. What it does is remove the only serious objection there was —the chronological one— and with it the need to suppose an earlier marriage for Giuseppe Palermo.
  • THE FATHER'S AGE MOVES IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION. Here he is FORTY, that is, born c. 1781; his death record of 1863 gave him ninety, that is, 1773. Eight years' difference. The ninety of a death record is almost always a round figure from neighbours; the forty he says himself. Giuseppe Palermo is in all probability of c. 1781, and not the ninety-year-old patriarch he seemed.
  • THE TRADE CHANGES THREE TIMES: BRACCIALE in 1821, PASTORE in Rosalia's marriage of 1838, and bracciale again on his death in 1863. They are not three men: in 1821 he is already married to Carmina Spizzirri, and that fixes it. In Marano a day labourer could spend years minding sheep and go back to day labour.
  • THE CONTRADA IS MALVITANI, which is a new datum. In 1838 Rosalia's record gives no contrada, and in 1863 and 1864 neither do they. It is the only known address of this family in Marano.
  • A DETAIL OF CLASS: the printed form says «è comparso IL SIGNOR ———» and the clerk STRIKES OUT «il Signor» before writing Giuseppe Palermo. In the same register he leaves it standing for others. He was no gentleman, he was a bracciale, and the clerk corrected it.
  • THE ANNUAL TABLE OF THE REGISTER is a separate find and is archived: «TAVOLA annuale alfabetica de' nati in questo comune dell'anno 1821», page 44, with records 19 to 54 — order number, name of the child, name and surname of the FATHER and day. Between 19 and 54 there is no other Palermo. WARNING FOR WHOEVER RETURNS TO THIS CONTAINER: pages 45 to 53 are not from Marano nor from 1821, but a loose bundle from APRIGLIANO of 1819 bound in at the end; the index is on pages 43 and 44.
Ancestor

Giovanni Chiappetta

A day labourer born in Castrolibero around 1848. He married at Rende in 1873 and had Filomena there. Still alive, and still able to sign, in 1910.

Child of Raffaele Chiappetta · Teresa Molinaro

Married Angela Massei

Parent of Filomena Chiappetta

He could sign his name

He was born at Castrolibero around 1848 and married at Rende on 19 October 1873, at three in the afternoon, to Angela Massei. He declared twenty-five years completed; she, fifteen. That day nobody signed: neither the couple nor the witnesses could write, and the record is initialled only by the sindaco Vincenzo Martino.

In May 1877 he came back to the town hall to register his daughter Filomena: twenty-nine years old, day labourer, the child born the previous day at nine in the morning in the contrada Soprano. Three separate records — the marriage of 1873, that birth of 1877 and his appearance as a witness in 1891 aged forty-three — point to the same year of birth. The fourth, that of 1910, is two years out.

And here is what sets him apart. On 30 August 1910 he went to report the death of his granddaughter Angelina, aged eight, together with the child's uncle. At the foot of the record it is stated that the informant Chiappetta Giovanni and the witness Vercillo Ferdinando sign; the others declared themselves illiterate. In a family where almost nobody signs — not the couple of 1873, nor those of 1894, nor Francesco on any of the nine records of his children — Giovanni Chiappetta could write his own name.

His birth record has not been located, but since August 2026 it is known where to look for it: his marriage record (Rende, 1873, record 32, in the file of that union) says that he was born in CASTROLIBERO —not in Rende— and that he had turned twenty-five, that is, that he was born c. 1848. He appears besides in the birth of Filomena (1877, aged 29) and in the death of his granddaughter Angelina (1910, aged 60, and signing in his own hand: he could write, a rare thing in this family).

Ancestor

Angela Massei

In the records: Angela Massei (the index gives her as «Mazzei»)

Born at Castrolibero around 1858. She married at barely fifteen and was Filomena's mother at nineteen.

Child of Domenico Massei · Carmina Ricchio

Married Giovanni Chiappetta

Parent of Filomena Chiappetta

Fifteen years completed

Her marriage record leaves no room for doubt: «di anni quindici compiti», fifteen years completed. She married on 19 October 1873, at the town hall of Rende, to Giovanni Chiappetta, a twenty-five-year-old day labourer. Both had been born at Castrolibero and both were already living at Rende; all four parents were alive and the record gives them as resident in the village.

She had Filomena at nineteen, on 11 May 1877. And twenty-one years later, on 1 April 1894, she went back to the same town hall to give her consent for her daughter to marry at sixteen. The same scene, one year apart.

She could not write, and her birth record — around 1858, at Castrolibero — has not been located. The surname is written MASSEI on her marriage, on Filomena's birth and at the 1894 wedding; the index this began from gives it as «Mazzei» and also as «Mazza».

What the manuscript says

…sono comparsi Giovanni Chiappetta, di anni venticinque compiti, bracciale, nato a Castrolibero, qui residente, figlio di Raffaele bracciale, e di Teresa Molinaro, contadina, quivi residenti;

ed Angela Massei, di anni quindici compiti, contadina, nata a Castrolibero, qui residente, figlia di Domenico, bracciale, e di Carmina Ricchio, contadina, qui residenti;

non che i testimoni Antonio Loizzo o Guallatta, di anni quaranta, bracciale, e Santo Palermo fu Luigi, di anni ventisei, bracciale, qui domiciliati.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and seventy-three, on the nineteenth day of October, at the town hall of Rende, circondario of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citeriore, at fifteen hours.

Before Us, VINCENZO MARTINO, mayor and Registrar of the Civil Status of the said comune, there appeared:

GIOVANNI CHIAPPETTA, aged twenty-five years completed, day labourer, BORN AT CASTROLIBERO, resident here, son of RAFFAELE, day labourer, and of TERESA MOLINARO, countrywoman, resident here;

and ANGELA MASSEI, aged fifteen years completed, countrywoman, BORN AT CASTROLIBERO, resident here, daughter of DOMENICO, day labourer, and of CARMINA RICCHIO, countrywoman, resident here;

together with the witnesses Antonio Loizzo or Guallatta, aged forty, day labourer, and Santo Palermo, son of the late Luigi, aged twenty-six, day labourer, resident here.

The said Chiappetta and Massei, in the presence of the said witnesses, formally requested me to proceed to the celebration of the marriage proposed between them, the two publications having already been made on the twenty-eighth of last September and the fifth of the current October, without any opposition arising, as appears from the certificate issued by this Registrar of the Civil Status on the ninth of the said October.

Granting that request, I read to them in the presence of the witnesses articles one hundred and thirty, one hundred and thirty-one and one hundred and thirty-two of the Civil Code, and having received from the spouses themselves, one after the other, the declaration that they wished to take each other as husband and wife, I pronounced in the name of the law that GIOVANNI CHIAPPETTA and ANGELA MASSEI are lawfully united in marriage.

The record is signed by me alone: the spouses and the witnesses declared that they could not write. — The Mayor, Vincenzo Martino.

What you need to know about this record

  • This record is the one that raises the tree by a whole storey on the Chiappetta side: it gives four new people at a stroke —Raffaele Chiappetta, Teresa Molinaro, Domenico Massei and Carmina Ricchio— and says that all four were alive.
  • That they were alive is not a supposition. The 1873 form distinguishes rigorously: in the same record it calls a witness «Santo Palermo FU Luigi», and it gives the four parents without «fu» and as «residenti».
  • BOTH BRIDE AND GROOM WERE BORN IN CASTROLIBERO. It is the collateral find: the Chiappetta branch comes from the same village that Michele Salerno (De Rango) and Maria Palermo came from. Filomena is already born in Rende, in 1877. Two families making the same journey in the same years.
  • THE SURNAME IS MASSEI, NOT MAZZEI. The index that was the starting point transcribes it «Mazzei» and «Mazza»; the original record, Filomena's birth (1877) and the marriage of 1894 all three write MASSEI. The same happens with «Mulinaro», which in the record is MOLINARO.
  • The bride's father is called only DOMENICO in the record; the «Antonio» the index added to him is not in the original.
  • She had turned fifteen. Four years later Filomena was born, and twenty-one years later Filomena married at sixteen: two consecutive generations of adolescent brides.
  • The first witness's surname is of doubtful reading. It is written with the alias formula «X o Y»: the most probable is «Antonio Loizzo o Guallatta».
Direct line

Michele Salerno

In the records: Michele Salerno di Rende

A foundling. Found at the wheel of the Cosenza hospice on 3 May 1825. A day labourer. He used three surnames over his lifetime and had none of his own.

Married Maria Antonia Palermo

Parent of Domenico Salerno · Arcangela Salerno · Francesco Salerno · Giovanni Salerno

The child at the wheel

On 3 May 1825, at one in the afternoon, a child appeared at the foundling wheel of the Cosenza hospice, wrapped in a white cloth and «senza alcun segno al di lui corpo» — with no mark on his body. Two hours later, at three, Emiddio Carli, a servant of the hospice, forty-one years old, resident in the contrada del Ospedale, carried him before the mayor Francesco Barone Ferreri and presented him. The chaplain don Gaetano Barra had already baptised him that same morning.

The business of the mark is not a piece of boilerplate. The usual thing was to leave a foundling with a garment torn in two, half a coin or a ribbon, and to keep the other half in order to be able to claim him one day. That he carried nothing means nobody intended to come back for him. On the record, the section for witnesses was left blank, and so was the column reserved for the parish to notify the baptism.

The name was given by the servant, not by the priest or the mayor: the form says «lo stesso ha inoltre dichiarato di dare al bambino il nome di Michele Salerno di Rende», and «the same» is Emiddio Carli. Foundlings had surnames invented for them and a place name was often pressed into service. That «di Rende» stuck to the name ended up being read as a birthplace: in 1858, in order to marry, the mayor of Castelfranco certified him as «esposito nativo di Rende». Michele could not possibly have known where he was born.

He had three documentary identities and no surname of his own: Salerno, imposed on him that day; Esposito, which was his condition and stayed with him always; and De Rango at Castrolibero, presumably from the household that raised him — his three children are registered there as De Rango. He was a day labourer and illiterate, and he never knew his age: he declared forty-two in 1868, forty-eight in 1870 and forty in 1873. He married at thirty-three to Maria Antonia Palermo, and he was still alive on 5 November 1910, aged eighty-five: the record of his son Domenico's second marriage names him without the «fu» with which that same clerk marks, on the same page, the bride's dead parents. His death has not been located, and since the digitised Rende collections stop precisely in 1910, it will probably have to be sought at the town hall.

Birth

Birth record

Cosenza · record 254, folio 122 verso · 3 May 1825, 15 h

Archive
Archivio di Stato di Cosenza
Found in
Rende, Reg. 50 «Matrimoni, processetti» 1858, p. 209 of 569
Register
an_ua35977232 · hash LyxMQVl
Copy issued
10 August 1858, Archivio Provinciale di Cosenza

What the manuscript says

…un fanciullo involto in fascia bianca, e senza alcun segno al di lui corpo.

Lo stesso ha inoltre dichiarato di dare al bambino il nome
di  Michele Salerno di Rende

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and twenty-five, on the third day of the month of May, at fifteen hours, before Us, FRANCESCO BARONE FERRERI, mayor and registrar of the civil status of the comune of Cosenza, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citeriore, there appeared signor EMIDDIO CARLI, aged forty-one, by trade a SERVANT, resident at Cosenza, contrada del Ospedale, and he presented to us a child whom we ascertained with our own eyes, declaring that the same was born according to the report of the chaplain of the hospice, signor don Gaetano Barra.

From the said report it appears that today, at thirteen hours, there was found at the wheel of the aforesaid house a child wrapped in a white cloth, and with no mark whatever on his body.

And We, Registrar of the Civil Status, in compliance with article fifty-two of the civil laws, having transcribed the aforesaid report, have initialled it and entered it in the volume of documents at folio two hundred and fifty-four, and it shall finally be deposited within the term fixed by law in the Chancery of the Civil Court.

The chaplain declared that he had administered Holy Baptism, and that it was conferred that same day.

The same further declared that he gave the child the name MICHELE SALERNO DI RENDE.

[The section for witnesses is left BLANK. The right-hand column, reserved for notification of the baptism by the parish, is also left BLANK.]

What you need to know about this record

  • «Senza alcun segno al di lui corpo» — without any mark on his body. It is not a formality: the usual thing was to leave the foundling with a garment torn in two, half a coin or a ribbon, keeping the other half in order to be able to reclaim him. The absence of a token means that nobody intended to come back.
  • «Salerno» was not adopted by him in later life: it was imposed on him on the very day he was found. Foundlings had surnames invented for them, and frequently a place name was pressed into service.
  • Who gave him his name: not the priest nor the mayor, but the informant. The form says «Lo stesso ha inoltre dichiarato di dare al bambino il nome di…» — «the same man», that is Emiddio Carli, the servant of the foundling hospital who took him to the town hall. The chaplain Gaetano Barra had baptised him that same morning; the mayor merely wrote it down.
  • The record says Cosenza. The appendix «di Rende» in his name ended up being read as his place of birth: in 1858 the mayor of Castelfranco certifies him as «esposito nativo di Rende». Michele could not know where he had been born.
Certificate

Certificate from the mayor of Castelfranco

Castelfranco (today Castrolibero) · document from the marriage dossier · August 1858

What the manuscript says

…esposito nativo di Rende…

Translation

Certificate issued by the mayor of Castelfranco for the marriage dossier, in which Michele is described as a «foundling native of Rende».

What you need to know about this record

  • It contradicts the 1825 record, which places him in the foundling wheel of the Cosenza hospital. It is the proof of how the «di Rende» of his name turned into a place of birth.
Direct line

Maria Antonia Palermo

Born at Marano Marchesato in March 1839. She married at nineteen a man fourteen years her senior. A countrywoman, and by 1873 a spinner.

Child of Francesco Giacomo Palermo · Carmina Volpintesta

Married Michele Salerno

Parent of Domenico Salerno · Arcangela Salerno · Francesco Salerno · Giovanni Salerno

The index changed her name

She was born at Marano Marchesato in March 1839. Her record was drawn up by the sindaco Giuseppe Filippelli at four in the afternoon, with her father Giacomo Palermo appearing and declaring twenty-seven years, and the name given was written down as «Maria Antonia».

The index this investigation started from transcribes that same record 18 of 1839 as «Maria Francesca Palermo». It is an index error, and an expensive kind: searching for a Maria Francesca finds nobody. The rule that came out of this holds for everything else: if the record number, the year and the parents match and only the name is wrong, the original record wins.

She married on 22 August 1858, at two in the afternoon, at the town hall of Rende, aged nineteen; he was thirty-three, fourteen years older. The banns were read at Castrolibero, where he lived, and they were married by the mayor of Rende, Luigi Perugini — the same official who nine years later would sign her father's death record.

On her children's records she appears as contadina, a countrywoman, and in 1873 as filatrice, a spinner. She is almost always called «Palermo Maria», without the Antonia: the full name appears only on her own birth record. She was still alive in November 1910, aged seventy-one, when her eldest son married.

What the manuscript says

…il nome di Maria Antonia.

Translation

Before Giuseppe Filippelli, mayor and Registrar of the Civil Status, there appeared Giacomo Palermo, aged twenty-seven, who declared that of Carmina Volpentesta, his lawful wife, a girl had been born, upon whom he bestowed the name MARIA ANTONIA.

What you need to know about this record

  • The blog index transcribes this same record 18 of 1839 as «Maria Francesca Palermo». The original record says Maria Antonia. Because of that error her birth appeared not to exist for years.
  • As a side effect a sister who never existed disappears: the «Maria Francesca of 1839» and Maria Antonia are the same person.
Sibling

Antonio Palermo

Brother of Maria Antonia. Born at Marano Marchesato on 10 October 1841, in the contrada Palazzo, when his father was a shepherd.

Child of Francesco Giacomo Palermo · Carmina Volpintesta

Baptised the same day

He was born on 10 October 1841 at ten in the morning, in his parents' house, in the contrada Palazzo of Marano Marchesato. That same afternoon, at four, his father took him before Francesco Spizzirri, sindaco and registrar. Giacomo Palermo declared thirty-six years and the trade of shepherd.

The right-hand column of the record certifies that the parish priest of Marano baptised him that same 10th. The witnesses were two day labourers from his own contrada, Bruno Parro — an uncertain reading — aged thirty, and Domenico Belmonte, forty. None of the three could write: only the mayor signs.

He is the second of the four documented children of Giacomo and Carmina, and Maria Antonia's brother. After that day he never appears in any paper again: no marriage, no death, no mention on anyone else's record.

Registrar
Francesco Spizzirri, sindaco
Handwritten year
«mille ottocento quarantuno» — agrees with the catalogue

What the manuscript says

…è comparso il Signor Giacomo Palermo di anni trentasei di professione pastore domiciliato in Marano contrada Palazzo… da Carmina Volpentesta di anni trenta domiciliata con esso… il nome di Antonio.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and forty-one, on the tenth day of the month of October, at sixteen hours, before Us, FRANCESCO SPIZZIRRI, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared signor GIACOMO PALERMO, AGED THIRTY-SIX, by trade a PASTORE, resident at Marano, CONTRADA PALAZZO.

And he presented to us a boy, as we ascertained with our own eyes, and declared to us that the same was born of CARMINA VOLPENTESTA, aged thirty, resident with him, on the tenth day of the month aforesaid of the current year, at ten in the morning, in the house of their dwelling.

The same further declared that he gave the boy the name ANTONIO.

The presentation and declaration were made in the presence of BRUNO PARRO, aged thirty, day labourer, resident at Marano, contrada Palazzo, and of DOMENICO BELMONTE, aged forty, day labourer, resident at Marano, contrada Palazzo, witnesses.

[In the right-hand column] The parish priest of the church of Marano returned to us the notice stating that the Sacrament of baptism was administered to the said boy, called ANTONIO PALERMO, on the tenth day of the month indicated.

What you need to know about this record

  • It is the only one of this man's five records in which he declares himself PASTORE. In 1845 and 1852 he will say «bracciale», and on his death in 1867 the record calls him «pecoraio». He begins and ends as a shepherd.
  • Thirty-six years old in October 1841 places him c. 1805. It is the earliest of the five figures known for him.
  • He was born and registered on the same day, and he was baptised on the same day too. That was normal: infant mortality left no room for waiting.
  • The first witness's surname is of doubtful reading: «Bruno Parro» or «Bruno Perri».
Sibling

Annunziata Palermo

In the records: Annunciata Palermo

Sister of Maria Antonia. Born at Marano Marchesato on 3 February 1845, in the contrada Langhi.

Child of Francesco Giacomo Palermo · Carmina Volpintesta

Annunciata, with a c

She was born on 3 February 1845 at nine in the morning, in the contrada Langhi of Marano Marchesato, and her father registered her at two that same afternoon. Giacomo Palermo then declared thirty-eight years and the trade of day labourer; four years earlier he had declared shepherd.

The modern index calls her Annunziata, with a z. The original record and the baptismal entry write ANNUNCIATA, with a c. The spelling of the document is what governs.

One of her two witnesses, Gaetano De Luca, a forty-two-year-old day labourer from the contrada Conchiali, will sign again seven years later at her sister Teresa's birth. After 1845 nothing more is known of her.

What the manuscript says

…è comparso il Signor Giacomo Palermo di anni trentotto di professione bracciale domiciliato in Marano contrada Langhi… da Carmina Volpentesta sua moglie legittima di anni trenta… il nome di Annunciata.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and forty-five, on the third day of the month of February, at fourteen hours, before Us, FRANCESCO SPIZZIRRI, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared signor GIACOMO PALERMO, AGED THIRTY-EIGHT, by trade a BRACCIALE (day labourer), resident at Marano, CONTRADA LANGHI.

And he presented to us a girl, as we ascertained with our own eyes, and declared to us that the same was born of CARMINA VOLPENTESTA, his lawful wife, AGED THIRTY, resident with him, on the third day of the month aforesaid of the current year, at nine in the morning, in the house of their dwelling.

The same further declared that he gave the girl the name ANNUNCIATA.

The presentation and declaration were made in the presence of FRANCESCO CONFORTI, aged thirty, day labourer, resident at Marano, contrada Conchiali, and of GAETANO DE LUCA, aged forty-two, day labourer, resident at Marano, contrada Conchiali, witnesses.

[In the right-hand column] The parish priest of Marano certifies that baptism was administered to the girl, called ANNUNCIATA PALERMO, on the third day of the month indicated.

What you need to know about this record

  • The index that was the starting point calls her «Annunziata»; the original record and the certificate of baptism write ANNUNCIATA, with a c. The folder was left with the agreed name and the file carries the spelling of the record.
  • The family has moved: in 1841 they lived in contrada Palazzo and here they are in contrada Langhi. In 1852 they will be in Cerzi.
  • The father declares thirty-eight years, which places him c. 1807 — the same reckoning that will come out of his death record of 1867.
  • The mother declares thirty years, the same she declared in 1841. Women's ages in these registers are repeated and rounded quite naturally.
  • Gaetano De Luca is a witness again seven years later, at the birth of Teresa.
Sibling

Teresa Palermo

The youngest of the documented siblings. Born at Marano Marchesato on 16 January 1852, in the contrada Cerzi.

Child of Francesco Giacomo Palermo · Carmina Volpintesta

The last one from Marano

She was born on 16 January 1852 at ten in the morning, in the contrada Cerzi — an uncertain reading — and was registered at five that same afternoon. She was baptised that day in the parish of S. Maria del Carmine at Marano.

Her father declared forty years. The mayor was no longer Francesco Spizzirri, who signed in 1841 and 1845, but Ferdinando Conforti. And among the witnesses is Gaetano De Luca again, now aged forty-eight.

She is the youngest of the four documented children, and eleven years separate her from the eldest. Between 1839 and 1852 nobody has yet swept the Marano births: it is very likely there are more siblings to find. Of Teresa's life after that 16 January nothing is known.

What the manuscript says

…è comparso il signor Giacomo Palermo di anni quaranta di professione bracciale domiciliato in Marano contrada Cerzi… da Carmina Volpentesta sua moglie legittima di anni trentacinque… il nome di Teresa.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and fifty-two, on the sixteenth day of the month of January, at seventeen hours, before Us, FERDINANDO CONFORTI, mayor and registrar of the civil status of the comune of MARANO MARCHESATO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared signor GIACOMO PALERMO, AGED FORTY, by trade a BRACCIALE, resident at Marano, CONTRADA CERZI.

And he presented to us a girl, as we ascertained with our own eyes, and declared to us that the same was born of CARMINA VOLPENTESTA, his lawful wife, AGED THIRTY-FIVE, resident with him, on the sixteenth day of the month aforesaid of the current year, at ten in the morning, in the house of their dwelling.

The same further declared that he gave the girl the name TERESA.

The presentation and declaration were made in the presence of LUIGI SICILIA, aged fifty, day labourer, resident at Marano, contrada Langhi, and of GAETANO DE LUCA, aged forty-eight, day labourer, resident at Marano, contrada Langhi, witnesses.

[In the right-hand column] The parish priest of S. Maria del Carmine certifies that baptism was administered to the girl, called TERESA PALERMO, on the sixteenth day of the month indicated.

What you need to know about this record

  • The mayor has changed: until 1845 Francesco Spizzirri signed and now Ferdinando Conforti signs.
  • It is the only one of the three records in which the name of the parish appears: SANTA MARIA DEL CARMINE of Marano.
  • The father declares forty years, which would give a birth c. 1812. It is the figure that fits worst with the others (36 in 1841, 38 in 1845, 60 at his death in 1867); the ages declared in these registers are approximate.
  • The name of the contrada is of doubtful reading: «Cerzi» is the most probable. The Restoration volumes are scanned at a lower resolution than the later ones and some words will not be read however much they are enlarged.
  • Thirteen years separate Teresa from her elder sister Maria Antonia. Between 1839 and 1852 there are almost certainly more siblings not yet looked for.

What the manuscript says

acta 19 | Michele Esposito Salerno | N/A
acta 19 | Maria Antonia Palermo    | Palermo, Francesco Giacomo  Volpintesta, Carmina

Translation

Marriage celebrated before Luigi Perugini, mayor of Rende. The groom appears as «Michele Esposito Salerno», aged 33, with the parents' box left blank on account of his being a foundling. The bride, Maria Antonia Palermo, aged 19, daughter of Francesco Giacomo Palermo and of Carmina Volpintesta. The dossier preserves the birth records of both and the certificate from the mayor of Castelfranco.

What you need to know about this record

  • In that same year TWO men called Michele Salerno married in Rende. The other is record 3: Michele Salerno, son of Gaetano Salerno and Isabella Iorio, with Rosa Cundari. Any search engine crossing «Michele Salerno + Rende + 1858» returns the wrong one.
  • Accepting the namesake would give parents to a man who documentarily has none, and would divert the whole branch.
Sibling

Domenico Salerno

The eldest of the four brothers. He posted banns at Rende in 1888 and married Giuseppina Cairo in 1910. He was widowed at least once.

Child of Michele Salerno · Maria Antonia Palermo

A widower at fifty-one

He is the eldest of the four brothers and the only one whose birth record has not turned up. What there is are two declarations of his own, ten weeks apart and both from 1910: on 30 August, reporting the death of his niece Angelina, he said he was fifty; on 5 November, marrying, he said fifty-one. The two figures cross at a birth around 1859, some ten months after his parents' wedding. He would therefore be the first-born.

His marriage record states unambiguously that he was born at Castrolibero. That clashes with an earlier check which had ruled Castrolibero out after sweeping fourteen years of registers. The likeliest explanation is that the wrong surname was searched: there Michele registered his children as DE RANGO. 1858 and 1859 must be looked at again for a «De Rango Domenico», son of Michele and Maria Palermo.

He married on 5 November 1910, at half past nine in the morning, to Giuseppina Cairo, a spinster of thirty-two, orphaned of both parents. The record calls him «vedovo»: it was not his first wedding, and of the first wife neither the name nor the date of death is known. Nobody appears to give consent — he was fifty-one, she thirty-two — and neither of them signs; the witnesses do, a forty-eight-year-old tailor and a twenty-seven-year-old «civile». Two children by a Maria Russo are attributed to him, Salvatore in 1906 and Michele in 1909, but no record confirms it.

His wedding is, incidentally, the single most productive document of the whole investigation. By naming his parents without the «fu» — when on the same page the same clerk writes «figlia di fu Giovanni» of the bride — it proves that Michele and Maria Antonia were still alive in November 1910.

No record obtained yet

His birth record has still not turned up, but since 2026 it is known where to look for it. At his wedding in 1910 (record 44, already downloaded and transcribed in the file of the marriage) he declares FIFTY-ONE YEARS and to have been BORN IN CASTROLIBERO; and ten weeks earlier, when declaring the death of his niece Angelina, he had said fifty. Both figures point to a birth c. 1859, some ten months after his parents' wedding: he would be the eldest son. The earlier sweep of Castrolibero, which started in 1859 and searched for «Salerno», did not find him; it must be repeated over 1858 and 1859 searching for DE RANGO, which is the surname under which Michele registered his children there.

Sibling

Arcangela Salerno

In the records: Arcangela De Rango

The only daughter. Born at Castrolibero in 1868 and registered under the surname De Rango.

Child of Michele Salerno · Maria Antonia Palermo

The only girl in the house

She was born on 23 June 1868 at two in the morning, in her parents' house, on the strada Feudo at Castrolibero. Her father went to register her at eight the following morning, before Giovanni Morelli, senior assessor acting as mayor in the absence of the incumbent.

The record calls him «MICHELE DE RANGO, figlio di Esposito», forty-two years old, by trade bracciale; and her mother «MARIA PALERMO, contadina». It is the first of the three Castrolibero records that write De Rango where Rende will put Salerno. The two witnesses were day labourers like him, Pasquale Sarentino, thirty-eight, and Ippolito Stellato, thirty-six, and none of them signed: the record notes that father, informant and witnesses are illiterate.

She is the only daughter of Michele and Maria Antonia for whom there is documentary evidence. After that record she never appears again: no marriage, no death, not a mention on her brothers' records.

What the manuscript says

Michele De Rango, figlio di Esposito, di anni quarantadue, di professione bracciale…

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and sixty-eight, on the twenty-fourth day of June, at eight in the morning, at the town hall, before Giovanni Morelli, senior assessor acting as mayor in the absence of the incumbent, there appeared MICHELE DE RANGO, son of Esposito, aged forty-two, by trade a day labourer, resident in this comune.

He declares that of MARIA PALERMO, countrywoman, resident in this comune, there was born on the twenty-third of the current month, at two in the morning, in the house of their dwelling situated in this comune, strada Feudo, a girl, upon whom he bestows the name ARCANGELA.

Witnesses: Pasquale Sarentino, son of the late Michele, aged thirty-eight, day labourer; and Ippolito Stellato, son of the late Domenico, aged thirty-six, day labourer. Neither the informant nor the witnesses sign, being illiterate.

What you need to know about this record

  • First appearance of the surname DE RANGO. In Castrolibero the whole family is registered thus; in Rende, the same people are Salerno.
  • The father's age (42 in 1868) is the one that best tallies with the foundling of 1825, who would be 43.
Direct line

Francesco Salerno

In the records: Francesco De Rango

Born at Castrolibero in 1870 and registered as De Rango. He married at Rende in 1894, by then as Salerno. A day labourer and farmhand; the family moved from one contrada to another almost every year.

Child of Michele Salerno · Maria Antonia Palermo

Married Filomena Chiappetta

Parent of Giuseppe Salerno · Antonio Salerno · Umberto Salerno · Settimo Salerno · Saverio Salerno · Angelina Salerno · Giovanni Salerno · Arturo Salerno · Maria Salerno

He never knew his age

He was born on 7 December 1870 at six in the morning at Castrolibero, and his father registered him the next day at two in the afternoon, presenting himself as «Michele De Rango Esposito, forty-eight years old, day labourer». The contrada reads Feudo in some transcriptions and Sciolle in others; it is the same field where Arcangela had been born two years earlier and where Giovanni would be born in 1873.

The ages he declared over his lifetime almost never add up. In February 1895, registering his first child, he said thirty: he was twenty-four. On marrying in April 1894 he had said twenty-four, when he was twenty-three. In November 1897 he said twenty-four again and was twenty-six. In May 1902 he said thirty and was thirty-one; in December 1907 he said thirty-eight on the very day he turned thirty-seven. He got it right twice: twenty-five in June 1896 and twenty-eight in October 1899. He was illiterate and signed none of those records.

He married on 1 April 1894, at half past ten in the morning, to Filomena Chiappetta, aged sixteen. Both declared they could not write. They had nine documented children between 1895 and 1909, and the household moved with almost every one: contrada Saporito in 1895, S. Agostino in 1896, Saporito again in 1897, Campagnano in 1899 and 1902, S. Agostino from 1904. The trade wobbles on paper too — agricoltore, contadino, bracciante — though the work was always the same.

On 24 September 1909 he went to register his daughter Maria and stayed on as a witness to the next record, that of his neighbour Benincasa: there appears a «Salerno Francesco, di anni trentotto, contadino», the same age and the same trade. That record was reported by a midwife because the husband «è in America». Ten years later his eldest sons would emigrate; he and Filomena stayed at Rende.

What the manuscript says

…è comparso Michele De Rango Esposito, di anni quarantotto, di professione bracciale, domiciliato in questo comune, contrada Feudo…

…è nato dalla sua legittima moglie Maria Palermo, contadina, domiciliata con suo marito, che il giorno sette del corrente mese ed anno ad ore sei antimeridiane…

Lo stesso dichiarante ha imposto al bambino il nome di Francesco.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and seventy, on the eighth day of the month of December, at the town hall of Castrolibero, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citeriore, at two in the afternoon. Before me, Giovanni Morelli, mayor and Registrar of the Civil Status of the said comune, there appeared MICHELE DE RANGO ESPOSITO, aged forty-eight, by trade a day labourer, resident in this comune, contrada Feudo.

Who, in the presence of the witnesses chosen by him — Alessandro Calabrese, son of the late Vincenzo, aged twenty-eight, and Michele Cosentino, son of the late Antonio, aged thirty-seven, both day labourers resident in this comune — presented to me a child of the male sex whom I ascertained with my own eyes, and declared that the same was born of his lawful wife MARIA PALERMO, countrywoman, resident with her husband, on the seventh of the current month and year, at six in the morning, in the house of their dwelling situated in this comune, contrada as above.

The same informant bestowed on the child the name FRANCESCO.

What you need to know about this record

  • It agrees with the marriage of 1894, where Francesco declares 23 years and that he was born in Castrolibero, son of Michele and of Palermo Maria. And with record 117 of 1896, where he appears aged 25.
  • The father's age wavers from record to record: 42 in 1868, 48 in 1870, 40 in 1873. Michele was a foundling and illiterate: he never knew the day of his birth and reckoned by eye.
Sibling

Giovanni Salerno

In the records: Giovanni De Rango

The youngest. Born at Castrolibero in 1873, married at Rende in 1897 and dead at twenty-six, leaving a thirteen-month-old son.

Child of Michele Salerno · Maria Antonia Palermo

Married Vincenzina Morelli

Parent of Salvatore Salerno

The clerk wrote «girl»

He was born on 24 January 1873 at two in the afternoon, on the strada Sciolle at Castrolibero, and was registered the following day at eleven in the morning. On the record the clerk wrote «un bambino di sesso femminile» — a girl — and then immediately gave the child the name GIOVANNI; the marginal heading says «De Rango Giovanni di Michele». It is a slip of the pen: he copied the wording from the neighbouring record, which really was a girl's. He was male, and everything that followed confirms it.

He married on 17 October 1897, at four in the afternoon, to Vincenzina Morelli, aged twenty-three. Neither signed. That record is the hinge of the whole tree: the same man registered as DE RANGO at birth marries as SALERNO, with the same parents, Michele and Palermo Maria. Without it there would be no way to stitch the Castrolibero records to those of Rende.

His son Salvatore was born on 12 January 1899. Giovanni died on 23 February 1900, at ten past ten in the morning, in the contrada S. Agostino, aged twenty-six: two years and four months after marrying, leaving a thirteen-month-old child. Two village farmhands went to report it, Cacciaro Raffaele, twenty-nine, and Stellato Andrea, forty-one.

The record says he had been born at Rende. That is an error by the informants, who were neighbours and not family: his birth record is from Castrolibero, and he had said so himself on marrying three years earlier. One further detail from that record: one of the witnesses was called De Rango Salvatore. The family's foundling surname was still alive at Rende in 1900.
