Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and seventy-three, on the twenty-fifth day of January, at eleven in the morning, at the town hall, before the mayor and Registrar of the Civil Status, there appeared DE RANGO MICHELE ESPOSITO, aged forty, by trade a day labourer, resident at Castrolibero.

He declares that of PALERMO MARIA, by trade a spinner, resident in the said comune, there was born on the twenty-fourth of January of the current year, at two in the afternoon, in his own dwelling, strada Sciolle, a child upon whom he bestows the name GIOVANNI.

Witnesses: Calabria Alessandro, son of the late Vincenzo, aged thirty-nine; and Sollato Spoglia, son of the late Domenico, aged forty-six, both day labourers of Castrolibero.

What you need to know about this record

  • The clerk wrote «un bambino di sesso femminile» but imposed the name GIOVANNI, and the marginal index says «De Rango Giovanni di Michele». It is a slip of the pen: he copied the formula from the neighbouring record, which was indeed for a girl. He is male — he marries in 1897 and dies in 1900 as a male.
  • The mother appears here as a spinner; in 1868 and 1870 she was a countrywoman.

What the manuscript says

Salerno Giovanni, di anni ventisei, contadino… figlio di Michele… e di Palermo Maria… marito di Morelli Vincenzina.

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred, on the twenty-fourth day of February, at twenty past ten in the morning, at the town hall. Before the Registrar of the Civil Status there appeared Cacciaro Raffaele, aged twenty-nine, farmhand, and Stellato Andrea, aged forty-one, farmhand, both of Rende, who declared that yesterday, at ten past ten in the morning, in the house situated in contrada S. Agostino, there died SALERNO GIOVANNI, aged twenty-six, farmhand, resident at Rende, born at Rende, son of Michele, farmhand resident at Rende, and of PALERMO MARIA, countrywoman resident at Rende, husband of MORELLI VINCENZINA.

Witnesses: De Rango Salvatore, aged thirty, farmhand; and Spizzirri Giovanni, aged forty-seven, farmhand.

What you need to know about this record

  • The record says he was born in Rende, but his birth certificate is from Castrolibero and so he himself declared on marrying in 1897. The error belongs to the two neighbours who went to report the death.
  • One of the witnesses is DE RANGO SALVATORE: the family's foundling surname was still alive in Rende in 1900.
  • He died two years and four months after marrying, leaving a thirteen-month-old son.
Spouse

Filomena Chiappetta

Born at Rende in 1877. She married at sixteen. Mother of the nine documented children.

Child of Giovanni Chiappetta · Angela Massei

Married Francesco Salerno

Parent of Giuseppe Salerno · Antonio Salerno · Umberto Salerno · Settimo Salerno · Saverio Salerno · Angelina Salerno · Giovanni Salerno · Arturo Salerno · Maria Salerno

Married at sixteen

She was born on 11 May 1877 at nine in the morning, in the contrada Soprano — an uncertain reading — at Rende. Her father, Giovanni Chiappetta, a twenty-nine-year-old day labourer, registered her at the same hour the next day, before the senior assessor Vincenzo Martino.

She married on 1 April 1894 at sixteen. Her mother, Angela Massei, had married at fifteen. On the record all four parents appear to give consent, and neither the couple nor they sign: «per essere analfabeti come hanno dichiarato». The two witnesses signed — Rendano Alfonso, a thirty-five-year-old landholder, and Vercillo Salvatore, a forty-five-year-old farmer — and the registrar.

Between 1895 and 1909 she had nine documented children: Giuseppe, Antonio, Umberto, the twins Settimo and Saverio, Angelina, Giovanni, Arturo and Maria. On the records she appears as «donna di casa» until 1897 and as contadina from 1899. In August 1910 she lost Angelina, aged eight; the death was reported not by her or her husband but by her father and her brother-in-law.

Family memory speaks of fourteen children. The sweep of the Rende births from 1893 to 1910, complete year by year and with no gaps, gives nine to Filomena; the other Salernos with a father named Francesco who turn up in those years belong to three other women in the village. There may have been more children between 1911 and 1918, which is the stretch that is not digitised — but that, as things stand, is a hypothesis.

In the year eighteen hundred and seventy-seven, on the twelfth day of May, at nine in the morning, at the town hall, before Martino Vincenzo, senior assessor for the mayor, there appeared CHIAPPETTA GIOVANNI, aged twenty-nine, by trade a day labourer, resident at Rende.

  • Her age of 16 at the wedding of April 1894 confirms that this register does indeed contain records of 1877 and not of 1878 — a necessary check, because the dating of the Antenati catalogue does not always agree with the contents.
Spouse

Vincenzina Morelli

Born at Castrolibero on 4 April 1874, on the Strada Marchesato, daughter of a day labourer and a spinner. She married Giovanni in 1897 and was widowed two and a half years later, with a thirteen-month-old son.

Married Giovanni Salerno

Parent of Salvatore Salerno

Two and a half years of marriage

She was born at Castrolibero around 1874, daughter of Morelli Antonio and Vina Maria. Her birth record has not been located: everything known about her comes from two records belonging to other people.

She married on 17 October 1897, at four in the afternoon, at the town hall of Rende, to Giovanni Salerno. She declared twenty-three years; he, twenty-four. The witnesses were not day labourers, as at almost every wedding in this family, but Pellicori Antonio, a thirty-two-year-old café keeper, and Cianci Costantino, a merchant. The couple did not sign.

Her son Salvatore was born on 12 January 1899 at three in the morning. Thirteen months later, on 23 February 1900, her husband died aged twenty-six. She was widowed at about twenty-six, with a thirteen-month-old child. Of her life from then on nothing is recorded — except that the boy grew up: in October 1921 he married at Rende.

Birth record

Castrolibero · record 17 · 5 April 1874, 9 h (the record); born on 4 April at 17 h

9 of 20 — folio 8 of the book
Registrar
Silvestro Vina, sindaco
Handwritten year
«mille ottocento settantaquattro» — agrees with the catalogue
How it was located
the «Indice annuale» at the end of the register, page 20 of 20: the row «Morelli Vincenzina — 17»

What the manuscript says

…è comparso MORELLI ANTONIO FIGLIO DI PAOLO di anni trentadue di professione bracciale domiciliato in questo comune… mi ha presentato una bambina di sesso femminile… e ha dichiarato che la stessa è nata dalla sua legittima moglie VINA MARIA di professione FILATRICE domiciliata in questo comune, nel giorno quattro del mese di aprile dell'anno corrente alle ore cinque pomeridiane nella casa di sua abitazione, sita in questo comune, STRADA MARCHESATO… il nome di VICENZINA.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and seventy-four, on the fifth day of the month of April, at nine in the morning, at the town hall of CASTROLIBERO, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra.

Before me, SILVESTRO VINA, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the aforesaid comune, there appeared ANTONIO MORELLI, SON OF PAOLO, AGED THIRTY-TWO, by trade a BRACCIALE (day labourer), resident in this comune.

Who, in the presence of two witnesses chosen by him — RAFFAELE PINCENTE, son of the late Gaetano, aged forty-nine, and RAFFAELE COSTABILE, son of Pasquale, aged thirty-two, both day labourers resident in this comune — presented to me A GIRL of the female sex, as I ascertained with my own eyes.

And he declared that the same was born of his lawful wife MARIA VINA, by trade a FILATRICE (spinner), resident in this comune, ON THE FOURTH DAY OF THE MONTH OF APRIL of the current year, AT FIVE IN THE AFTERNOON, in the house of their dwelling, situated in this comune, STRADA MARCHESATO.

The same informant bestowed on the girl the name VICENZINA.

Whereof the present record was drawn up, entered in both registers; read to the informant and the witnesses, it was signed by me alone, since the informant and the witnesses stated that they COULD NOT WRITE.

What you need to know about this record

  • It agrees with everything that was being sought: the parents are exactly the two names she declared at her Rende wedding of 1897 —Antonio Morelli and Maria Vina—, she was born in Castrolibero, and on 17 October 1897 she had turned twenty-three, which is the age she declared that day. A perfect fit, without forcing anything.
  • The record adds a new ancestor as a bonus: PAOLO MORELLI, father of Antonio. The record says «figlio di Paolo», without «fu», so in April 1874 he was probably still alive.
  • The father, Antonio Morelli, was thirty-two years old in April 1874: he was born c. 1842, and his record would have to be sought in the Nati of Castrolibero of those years.
  • BEWARE OF THE NAMESAKE: the same index of 1874 brings a MARCHESE VINCENZINA, record 9, who is not this one. Two Vincenzinas of the same year and the same village, distinguishable only by the surname.
  • The surname VINA is abundant in Castrolibero in 1874 —the index brings four more Vina— and the registrar of the civil status who signs the record was himself called Silvestro Vina.
  • Note on resolution: the original scan of this page measures 5082 × 3984 pixels, but the server does not deliver more than ten million pixels per request, so that the image of the full page comes at 3570 × 2799. The zoom crops ARE requested in the coordinates of the original and at native size: they are neither rescaled nor interpolated.

Marriage record

Rende · record 16 · 1 April 1894, 10:30 h

9 of 36

What the manuscript says

1° Salerno Francesco, di anni ventitré, celibe, nato in Castrolibero, residente in Rende, figlio di Michele, residente in Rende, e di Palermo Maria, residente in detto luogo;

2° Chiappetta Filomena, di anni sedici, nubile, nata in Rende, residente in Rende, figlia di Giovanni, residente in Rende, e di Massei Angela, residente in Rende.

Al presente matrimonio hanno dato il loro consenso Salerno Michele e Palermo Maria, genitori dello sposo, e Chiappetta Giovanni e Massei Angela, genitori della sposa.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-four, on the first day of April, at half past ten in the morning, at the town hall of Rende, open to the public. Before me, Lo Celso Giuseppe, senior assessor standing in for the mayor being indisposed, Registrar of the Civil Status, in official dress, there appeared in person:

1st SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged twenty-three, unmarried, born at Castrolibero, resident at Rende, son of Michele, resident at Rende, and of Palermo Maria, resident in the said place.

2nd CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, aged sixteen, unmarried, born at Rende, resident at Rende, daughter of Giovanni, resident at Rende, and of Massei Angela, resident at Rende.

Who requested me to unite them in marriage. To that end they presented me with a single document, the certificate of the banns celebrated in this comune on Sundays the fourth and the eleventh of March of the current year.

The articles one hundred and thirty, one hundred and thirty-one and one hundred and thirty-two of the Civil Code having been read to the spouses, and each having answered in the affirmative, I pronounced in the name of the law that they are united in marriage.

Witnesses: Rendano Alfonso, aged thirty-five, landholder; and Vercillo Salvatore, aged forty-five, farmer.

To the present marriage their consent was given by SALERNO MICHELE and PALERMO MARIA, the husband's parents, and CHIAPPETTA GIOVANNI and MASSEI ANGELA, the wife's parents.

What you need to know about this record

  • The parents' consent is not a formality: the Civil Code required it for males under twenty-five, and Francesco was twenty-three. Michele and Maria Antonia had to appear in person.
  • It is the latest trace we have of Michele: the foundling of the wheel was alive on 1 April 1894, aged about 69, watching his son marry. His death is later than that date.
  • The bride and groom do not sign, «per essere analfabeti come hanno dichiarato». The two witnesses and the officer sign.
  • The processetti of 1894 DO NOT exist: that series in Rende ends in 1865, with the Restoration holding. And the record itself says that a single document was presented.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and sixty-nine, on the TWELFTH day of the month of FEBRUARY, at the Town Hall of Atessa, at SIXTEEN HOURS.

Before me, GIUSEPPE FALCUCCI, Mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of ATESSA, District of VASTO, Province of CHIETI, there appeared DOMENICO PIZZI, SON OF THE LATE GIUSEPPE, aged THIRTY-NINE, STONEMASON, resident at Atessa in the QUARTER OF THE CASTELLO.

Who presented to me A GIRL of the female sex, whom he declares was born to him ON THE DAY AFORESAID, at EIGHT HOURS ITALIAN STYLE, OF HIS WIFE TERESA FLOCCO, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE PASQUALE, aged THIRTY-FIVE, SPINNER, resident with him; upon which daughter he declares he bestows the name ANGIOLINA.

The said declaration was made in the presence of FRANCESCO MARCUCCI (uncertain reading), son of the late Giuseppantonio, aged fifty, and of ALBERTO FARINA (uncertain reading), son of the late Domenico, aged thirty-six, farmhands resident at Atessa, witnesses chosen by the informant; to whom I read the present record, drawn up at the same time in both original registers, and signed by no one but me AND THE INFORMANT, the witnesses having stated that they could not write. — Domenico Pizzi. — The Registrar of the Civil Status, Giuseppe Falcucci.

IN THE MARGIN, in another hand: «In 1892, under number 101, she married NICOLA FAIENZA» — a note dated 26 August 1893.

What you need to know about this record

  • HER NAME IS ANGIOLINA, NOT ANGELA. The record imposes «Angiolina» on her and the register's index records her thus. «Angela» is how family memory names her. She is the same person: the parents prove it.
  • SHE WAS BORN ON 12 FEBRUARY, NOT ON THE 17th. The family block without sources gave 17 February 1869. The record says «il giorno DODICI del mese di Febbrajo» and that the girl was born «il dì sopradetto», that same day. Five days of error, and they are five days that confirm what was already known of that block: the data are there, but they are not reliable in detail.
  • THE PARENTS TALLY EXACTLY. «Domenico Pizzi FU GIUSEPPE, muratore» and «Teresa Flocco FU PASQUALE, filatrice». It is the proof that was being sought, and as a bonus it rescues two grandparents: Giuseppe Pizzi and Pasquale Flocco, both already dead in February 1869.
  • THE MARGINAL NOTE IS THE GREAT FIND: «nel 1892 al N. 101 SPOSÒ NICOLA FAIENZA». The marriage with the surname Faienza ceases to be a datum from someone else's tree and comes to be written in the margin of an original certificate. And it says where: the annotation is in the register of ATESSA, with a record number of that municipality itself, so the marriage was celebrated IN ATESSA in 1892 (record 101) and not in Paglieta.
  • IMMEDIATE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE SEARCH FOR DOMENICO FAIENZA: if his parents married in Atessa in 1892 and her family is from Atessa for several generations, the son's birth must be sought in ATESSA, not in Paglieta. The Paglieta index of 1899, which has been read in full, has not a single Faienza.
  • THE FATHER SIGNED. The form ends «e non firmato che da me, E DAL DICHIARANTE, avendo asserito i testimoni di non saper scrivere», and below is the signature «Domenico Pizzi». A mason who can write in 1869, with two illiterate witnesses beside him. In the whole Calabrian branch of this tree there is not a single signature of an ancestor.
  • «ORE OTTO ITALIANE»: the record still uses Italian hours, counted from nightfall. In Atessa, in mid-February, eight hours after sunset is approximately two in the morning.

Nicola Faienza

A farmhand from Atessa, born around 1869. He married Angiolina Pizzi on 29 December 1892, record 101 of the Atessa register. Until August 2026 absolutely nothing was known about him; the marriage record says it all at once: where he was born, what he did and whose son he was.

SETTLED IN AUGUST 2026 WITH RECORD 101 OF ATESSA, which is transcribed in full in the file of the marriage. The chain ran thus: in the margin of the birth record of Angiolina Pizzi (Atessa, 1869, record 65) a later hand noted «nel 1892 al N. 101 sposò NICOLA FAIENZA», dated 26 August 1893. With the year, the number and the comune the document was requested, and the document answered everything. WHAT IT SAYS: that he was TWENTY-THREE YEARS OLD —that is, that he was born c. 1869, the same year as his wife—, that he was a CONTADINO (farmhand), that he had been BORN IN ATESSA and lived in Atessa, and that he was the son of GIUSEPPE FAIENZA, contadino of Atessa and then living, and of the late MARIA STANISCIA. As he was not yet twenty-five, the Civil Code required his father's consent, and the record puts it on record: «ha prestato il consenso il padre dello sposo». That formality is, incidentally, the proof that Giuseppe Faienza was alive and traceable in Atessa in December 1892. AND WHAT IT KNOCKS DOWN: the PAGLIETA lead, definitively. Nicola was not from Paglieta nor from anywhere else: he was from Atessa, like his father and like his wife. That lead came from the same block of family information without a single citation which had gone wrong before, and with this one it has now been proved wrong three times. WHAT IS MISSING: his own birth record, in the Nati of Atessa around 1869, and that of his son Domenico, which is not in Atessa 1899 —the annual index has been read in full— and will have to be looked for in 1900.

No record obtained yet

NEW IN AUGUST 2026, and he is the first Faienza of a generation earlier than Nicola to appear written down in a document. He comes out of the marriage record of his son (Atessa, 29 December 1892, record 101, transcribed in the file of the marriage): «NICOLA FAIENZA… figlio di GIUSEPPE, contadino, residente in Atessa». THAT HE WAS ALIVE IS NOT AN INFERENCE FROM THE FORM, IT IS A FACT OF THE RECORD. Nicola was twenty-three and the Civil Code required paternal consent below the age of twenty-five; the record notes that «ha prestato il consenso il padre dello sposo, come risulta ed è spiegato dall'atto di richiesta delle pubblicazioni». Someone went to declare it before the registrar in December 1892. WHAT IS NOT KNOWN: when he was born, whose son he was and when he died. His marriage to Maria Staniscia would be from around 1865 and would be in the Matrimoni of Atessa; his death, in the Morti of Atessa later than 1892. It is worth bearing in mind that in Atessa there are several Faienza families at the same time —in the births of 1899 three different ones appear, that of Michele fu Carmine and those of Antonio and Vincenzo di Pasquale—, so the surname alone is not enough: the father's name will be needed in each record.

NEW IN AUGUST 2026. The marriage record of her son Nicola (Atessa, 29 December 1892, record 101) says «e della FU MARIA STANISCIA, residente in vita in Atessa». The «fu» and the «in vita» say the same thing twice over: by December 1892 she was already dead, and while she lived she lived in Atessa. She is the third mother in a row whom the Atessa records take the trouble to name —after Santa Menna in 1841, Rosa Iacobitti in 1847 and Antonia di Iorio in 1891—, as against the four twin forms from Rende that left the gap blank four times. STANISCIA is a common surname in Atessa: the marriage index of 1892 carries several, and in the births of 1899 it comes up again. Her death ought to be in the Morti of Atessa earlier than 1892.

How it was located
the record number was already known from the marginal note on record 65 of 1869. The alphabetical index runs over pages 135 to 138 and the highest marriage it indexes is 104. Page 110 is the title page of PART II, so part I occupies pages 1 to 109 at the rate of one record per image; a probe on page 60 gave record 58, that is, record = page − 2, and page 103 gave record 101 at the first try. Four images in all

Faienza Nicola · Angiolina Pizzi · Luzio Iannone testimone · Emidio Flocco
testimone · L'Ufficiale dello Stato Civile, Francesco Falcucci

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-two, on the TWENTY-NINTH OF DECEMBER, at a QUARTER PAST ELEVEN in the morning, at the town hall of ATESSA, open to the public.

Before me, FRANCESCO FALCUCCI, municipal assessor delegated by the mayor by act of the twenty-fifth of November eighteen hundred and eighty-nine, duly approved, Registrar of the Civil Status, in official dress, there appeared in person:

1st NICOLA FAIENZA, aged TWENTY-THREE, FARMHAND, BORN AT ATESSA, resident at Atessa, SON OF GIUSEPPE, farmhand, resident at Atessa, AND OF THE LATE MARIA STANISCIA, who in life resided at Atessa.

2nd ANGIOLINA PIZZI, aged TWENTY-THREE, housewife, born at Atessa, resident at Atessa, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE DOMENICO, who in life resided at Atessa, AND OF TERESA FLOCCO, resident at Atessa.

Who requested me to unite them in marriage. To that end they presented me with a single document; the articles one hundred and thirty, one hundred and thirty-one and one hundred and thirty-two of the Civil Code having been read to the spouses, and having asked the husband whether he wishes to take as his wife the ANGIOLINA PIZZI here present, and her whether she wishes to take as her husband the NICOLA FAIENZA here present, and each having answered in the affirmative, I pronounced in the name of the law that they are united in marriage.

Witnesses: LUZIO IANNONE, aged twenty-two, CLERK, and EMIDIO FLOCCO, aged fifty-six, CAFÉ KEEPER, both resident in this comune.

The document presented is the certificate of the banns made by myself as Registrar of the Civil Status of Atessa, the first on the EIGHTEENTH and the second on the TWENTY-FIFTH of the current December.

To the said marriage consent was given by THE HUSBAND'S FATHER, as appears and is set out in the act requesting the banns.

(a) The interlined words «vita in» are to be taken as not written.

The present record having been read to all present, the spouses and the witnesses signed it with me.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT WAS THE SHORTEST AND MOST PROFITABLE LOOSE END THAT HAD BEEN OPENED, AND IT HAS PAID OFF. Of Nicola Faienza absolutely nothing was known: not the age, nor the trade, nor the village, nor the parents. The record gives all four things in a single line.
  • IT KILLS THE PAGLIETA LEAD DEFINITIVELY. «Nato in Atessa, residente in Atessa, figlio di Giuseppe, contadino, residente in Atessa». Neither the bridegroom nor his father were from Paglieta. That village came from the family block without sources, and with this one that block has failed a direct check three times.
  • IT ADDS A GENERATION OF FAIENZA: Giuseppe Faienza and Maria Staniscia, the bridegroom's parents, who until today existed on no paper. He alive in 1892, she already dead.
  • THE PATERNAL CONSENT IS NOT A FORMALITY. The Civil Code required it of males under twenty-five and Nicola was twenty-three, just as happened to Francesco Salerno in Rende two years later. That it is set down in writing is the proof that Giuseppe Faienza was alive and at hand in December 1892.
  • BOTH BRIDE AND GROOM COULD WRITE, and in these registers that is exceptional: the usual formula of the period is «essendo i testimoni analfabeti». Here all four sign. Her father, Domenico Pizzi, also signed: he did so in 1869 when reporting the birth of this same bride.
  • IT CONFIRMS THE BRIDE POINT BY POINT: twenty-three years old (she was born on 12 February 1869), daughter of the late Domenico (he died on 28 September 1891) and of Teresa Flocco, who was still alive. The correction (a) at the foot strikes out the «in vita» that the clerk had interlined above her: she was not one more widow, she was alive.
  • IT CORRECTS AN EARLIER READING. The mayor's delegation to Francesco Falcucci was noted on the page of the 1891 record as «24 November 1889». Here it reads VENTICINQUE, and the Atessa births of 1899 repeat «venticinque». It was the 25th.
  • IT WAS LOCATED IN FOUR IMAGES AND AT THE FIRST ATTEMPT IN THE LAST ONE: the number was given by the marginal note of 1869, and the title page of PART II on page 110 left the arithmetic ready-made.
  • WHAT IT OPENS: the birth certificate of Nicola himself, in the Nati of Atessa c. 1869 —the same register and almost the same year as his wife's—, and the marriage of his parents, c. 1865.
Sibling

Giuseppe Salerno

The first son. Born at Rende in 1895; he was 24 in 1919.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

The first of nine

He was born on 23 February 1895 at ten in the morning, in the contrada Saporito at Rende. His father registered him the next day at the same hour, before Spina Raffaele, secretary delegated by the mayor. The witnesses were Zicarelli Carmine, a twenty-eight-year-old day labourer, and Marchese Vincenzo, a sixty-year-old farmer.

On that record Francesco declared thirty years. He was twenty-four. It is the first in a run of misdeclared ages that has forced every record of this family to be checked by the mother's name and the sequence number, and never by the father's age.

He is the first child of Francesco and Filomena, born eleven months after the wedding. In 1919 he was twenty-four and was among those who crossed to Argentina — though the year of the voyage is still not settled: Italy did not allow men of military age to emigrate until after the war, and emigration to Argentina did not resume until March 1920.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-five, on the twenty-fourth day of February, at ten in the morning, at the town hall, before Spina Raffaele, secretary delegated by act of the mayor of the sixteenth of December eighteen hundred and ninety-four, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, farmer, resident at Rende.

He declares that of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, housewife, his wife living with him, there was born on the twenty-third of the current month, at ten in the morning, in the house situated in contrada Saporito, a boy, upon whom he bestows the name GIUSEPPE.

What you need to know about this record

  • The father's declared age (30) is wrong: Francesco had been born on 7 December 1870, so he was 24. The ages declared in these registers are approximate.
  • The block of text at the top right of that same page —a wet-nurse agreement with Bruno Maria— does NOT belong to this record, but to record 42.
Sibling

Antonio Salerno

In the records: ANTHONY SALERNO in the United States

The brother who went to Chicago. Second son of Francesco and Filomena, born at Rende in 1896; he boarded at Naples at seventeen and landed in New York on 16 April 1914. He died in Illinois in 1981, aged eighty-four, after sixty-seven years in the same town and thirty-odd in the same feed mill. He is by far the brother of Settimo about whom most is known.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

Married Rosina Salerno

Parent of Domenico Salerno · Filomena Salerno

The record that exposed the catalogue

He was born on 13 June 1896 in the contrada S. Agostino at Rende and was registered the following day at seven in the morning. His father declared twenty-five years, and this time the figure was exact. The witnesses were two village farmers: Greco Pasquale and Serpe Serafino.

That exactness solved a serious problem. The register holding his record, Rende's number 87, is listed in the archive catalogue as «Births 1895», but the handwritten year in the heading says «novantasei» quite clearly and the father's age fits 1896. From this it emerged that the catalogue's dating is not reliable and that the Rende birth volumes do not run one per year: they split years in two. Since then there has been a rule in this investigation — read the handwritten year before accepting any record.

The family had moved from Saporito, where Giuseppe had been born, to S. Agostino, the contrada where they would live again from 1904.

Family memory places him in Chicago, alongside a Vincenzo. There is as yet no document to support it: it is a lead, not a fact.

Riverdale, Illinois · «The Star», 10 May 1981 · martes 5 May 1981

What the manuscript says

ANTONIO SALERNO. Services were Friday for Antonio Salerno of Riverdale. Mr. Salerno died Tuesday at Ingalls Memorial hospital, Harvey. The funeral mass was at St. Mary's church, Riverdale. Burial was at St. Benedict's cemetery, Chicago. A former laborer at the Hales and Hunter Feed company in Riverdale, Mr. Salerno was born June 13, 1896, in Italy. He is survived by his wife, Rose; a son, Dominic of Riverdale; a brothr, Vincenzo of Riverdale; two grandchildren, and five great-grandchildren.

Translation

ANTONIO SALERNO. Services for Antonio Salerno, of Riverdale, were held on Friday. Mr Salerno died on Tuesday at Ingalls Memorial Hospital in Harvey. Mass was at St Mary's Church, Riverdale. Burial was at St Benedict Cemetery, Chicago.

A former labourer at the Hales and Hunter Feed Company of Riverdale, Mr Salerno had been born on 13 JUNE 1896 IN ITALY.

He is survived by his wife, Rose; a son, Dominic, of Riverdale; a brother, Vincenzo, of Riverdale; two grandchildren and five great-grandchildren.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS OBITUARY CLOSES THE IDENTIFICATION. It gives «13 June 1896», which is EXACTLY the date of his birth record from Rende. His American papers —the naturalisation of 1925 and the draft card of 1942— said 1897; the newspaper is right where the forms failed. There is no doubt at all that he is Settimo's brother.
  • HE DIED ON A TUESDAY: 10 May 1981 was a Sunday, the funeral was on Friday the 8th, so the death falls on TUESDAY 5 MAY 1981. He was eighty-four years old.
  • «A BROTHR, VINCENZO OF RIVERDALE». It is the most intriguing line in the whole document and it is unresolved — see the loose end that bears his name.
  • SIXTY-SEVEN YEARS IN THE SAME PLACE. He arrived in Riverdale at seventeen and there he died at eighty-four. The factory where he worked, Hales and Hunter, was six blocks from his house.

United States District Court, Northern District of Illinois

Declaration of intention
no. 22117, August 1921

What the manuscript says

ANTONIO SALERNO. My place of residence is 13832 So. Emerald Ave., Riverdale, Illinois. My occupation is laborer. I was born on the 13th day of June, anno Domini 1897, at Cosenza, Italy. I emigrated to the United States from Naples, Italy, on or about the 8th day of April anno Domini 1914, and arrived at the United States, at the port of New York, on the 16th day of April anno Domini 1914, on the vessel BERLIN. […] My wife's name is Rosina. She was born in Italy and now resides with me. I have 2 children: Domenico born Feb. 11, 1922; Filomena, Feb. 8, 1925, both born at Riverdale, Ill., and reside with me.

Translation

Antonio Salerno, labourer, resident at 13832 South Emerald Avenue, Riverdale, Illinois, declares that he was born on 13 June 1897 at Cosenza, Italy.

HE EMIGRATED FROM NAPLES around 8 April 1914 and ARRIVED AT THE PORT OF NEW YORK ON 16 APRIL 1914, ABOARD THE STEAMER «BERLIN».

He has lived in Illinois since 21 October 1914. His wife is called ROSINA, born in Italy and living with him. He has two children: DOMENICO, born 11 February 1922, and FILOMENA, 8 February 1925, both born at Riverdale.

He renounces all allegiance to Victor Emmanuel III, King of Italy. Signing as witnesses were GABRIELE PRETE, labourer, of 800 South Loomis St., Chicago, and SARFINO ARIELLO, switchman, of 13643 South Halsted St., Riverdale, who declare that they have known him since 1 November 1919.

What you need to know about this record

  • HERE IS THE SHIP. After exhausting the CEMLA without finding anyone, it turns out the answer lay in the other hemisphere: Antonio did not go to Buenos Aires, he went to New York, and his name is on the list of the steamer BERLIN which docked on 16 April 1914. The passenger list for that voyage is a document that can now be ordered.
  • HE LEFT IN 1914, NOT IN 1919. The whole family reconstruction had taken for granted that the brothers emigrated together after the war. Antonio left FIVE YEARS EARLIER, and had only just turned seventeen. The family did not scatter all at once: it frayed away.
  • His year of birth here is 1897, and in his Rende record it is 1896. The day and the month match exactly. His 1981 obituary notice says 1896 and settles the matter.
  • The witness Gabriele Prete reappears in the 1950 census living two doors away. And the other, Sarfino Ariello, lived at 13643 Halsted, the same block where the census places the Cundari family. It was an entire neighbourhood of fellow villagers.
Certificate

Second World War draft card

Riverdale, Illinois · serial 798, order 11239 · 16 February 1942

Archive
National Personnel Records Center, St. Louis, Missouri
Via
FamilySearch — image 816 of 1081, group 104612912
Local board
no. 11, Harvey, Cook County, Illinois

What the manuscript says

Antonio (None) Salerno. 13838 Emerald Ave., Riverdale, Cook, Illinois. Telephone Riverdale 188 J. Age 44. Date of birth June 13, 1897. Place of birth Cosenza, Italy. Name and address of person who will always know your address: GUS CUNDARI, 13851 S. Halsted, Riverdale, Illinois. Employer: Hales and Hunter, 140th and Halsted. — White, 5'4", 145 lbs, eyes brown, hair black, complexion dark. Other obvious physical characteristics: None.

Translation

Registration card for what was called the «old man's registration», the census of men aged 45 to 64 that the United States took on entering the war. Antonio Salerno, no middle name, of 13838 Emerald Avenue, Riverdale. Telephone: Riverdale 188 J. Forty-four years old, born 13 June 1897 at Cosenza, Italy.

Person who will always know his address: GUS CUNDARI, of 13851 South Halsted, Riverdale. Employed at Hales and Hunter, at 140th and Halsted.

DESCRIPTION: white, FIVE FEET FOUR INCHES (1.63 m), ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-FIVE POUNDS (66 kg), BROWN EYES, BLACK HAIR, DARK COMPLEXION. No other distinguishing marks.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE ONLY PHYSICAL DESCRIPTION OF ANYONE IN THIS ENTIRE TREE. Out of a hundred and thirty people and two hundred years of paperwork, this is the only document that says what someone looked like: short, stocky, dark-skinned, black hair and brown eyes. His brother Settimo would probably have been similar.
  • GUS CUNDARI AS CONTACT. Cundari is the surname of his mother-in-law, Cherubina Cundari, and also that of Anna Cundari, the woman his brother Giovanni married in Rende in 1926. Two Salerno brothers related by marriage to Cundaris, one in Rende and the other in Illinois.
  • In 1942 he was still at the same factory and on the same street as in 1925. Only the number had changed: from 13832 to 13838.
Certificate

United States census

Riverdale, Cook County, Illinois · sheet 3, dwelling 21 — 13838 Emerald Ave. · April 1950

Archive
National Archives and Records Administration
Via
FamilySearch — image 4 of 39, group 108925561

What the manuscript says

SALERNO, Anthony — Head, W, M, 53, Mar., born Italy, naturalized. 40 hrs. Laborer, Feed Mill. / Rose — Wife, W, F, 43, Mar., born Italy, naturalized. / Dominick D. — Son, W, M, 27, Mar. 36 hrs. Switchman, Railroad Co. / Mary L. — Daughter-in-law, W, F, 21, Mar. / Beverlee — Granddaughter, W, F, 2, Sin.

Translation

In April 1950 the house at 13838 Emerald Avenue held three generations:

ANTHONY SALERNO, head of household, 53, married, born in Italy, naturalised. Forty hours a week as a labourer in a feed mill. ROSE, his wife, 43, born in Italy, naturalised. DOMINICK D., the son, 27, married, RAILROAD COMPANY SWITCHMAN, 36 hours. MARY L., his daughter-in-law, 21. BEVERLEE, the granddaughter, 2.

Two houses along, on the same census sheet, lived CONSTANTINO CUNDARI, 62, born in Italy, foreman with the railroad company, with his wife ANGELINA, 57.

What you need to know about this record

  • THREE GENERATIONS UNDER THE SAME ROOF, and a fourth two doors away: the Cundaris, his mother-in-law's family. Emerald Street in Riverdale was a Calabrian village transplanted.
  • His daughter Filomena, aged twenty-five in 1950, no longer lives at home: she would have married.
  • The son went into the railway, not into the feed mill. The next generation moved out of the father's trade.

What the manuscript says

Salerno Francesco, di anni venticinque, agricoltore…

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-six, on the fourteenth day of June, at seven in the morning, at the town hall, before Spina Raffaele, secretary delegated by act of the mayor of the first of December eighteen hundred and ninety-five, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged twenty-five, farmer, resident at Rende.

He declares that of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, housewife, his wife living with him, there was born on the thirteenth of the current month, in the house situated in contrada S. Agostino, a boy, upon whom he bestows the name ANTONIO.

Witnesses: Greco Pasquale, farmer; and Serpe Serafino, farmer.

What you need to know about this record

  • It is the record that made it possible to detect the offset in the Antenati catalogue. The handwritten year says «novantasei» perfectly clearly, and the father's age (25) tallies exactly with his birth in December 1870.
  • The family has moved from contrada Saporito to contrada S. Agostino, where they will still be in 1900.
Sibling

Umberto Salerno

Third son, born at Rende in 1897. He was 22 in 1919.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

Registered the same day

He was born on 22 November 1897 at six in the morning, in the contrada Saporito. By ten past ten that same morning his father was at the town hall declaring him: four hours and ten minutes between the birth and the registration.

The age Francesco declared is written in very faint ink; the likeliest reading is twenty-four, and he was twenty-six. The witnesses were two village farmers, Spizzirri Giovanni, forty-nine, and Picarelli Vincenzo, sixty-eight. Nobody signed but the secretary, Spina Raffaele.

His record was harder to find than any other. The 1897 register has re-photographed pages: when the volume has a loose sheet lying on it — death annotations that other town halls would send decades later — the digitiser takes two shots of the same opening. Page 86 carries a sheet covering half the spread; page 85 is the clean one, and that is the one that was saved.

With Umberto the family had gone back to the contrada Saporito, where they had already been in 1895. In 1919 he was twenty-two and was one of those who left.

What the manuscript says

Salerno Francesco, contadino… di anni ventiquattro…

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-seven, on the twenty-second day of November, at ten past ten in the morning, at the town hall, before Spina Raffaele, secretary delegated by act of the mayor of the sixteenth of December eighteen hundred and ninety-six, duly approved, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, farmhand, resident at Rende.

He declares that of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, countrywoman, his wife living with him, there was born on the twenty-second of the current month, at six in the morning, in the house situated in contrada Saporito, a child of the male sex, upon whom he bestows the name UMBERTO.

Witnesses: Spizzirri Giovanni, aged forty-nine, farmer; and Picarelli Vincenzo, aged sixty-eight, farmer, both resident in the comune. Illiterate: they do not sign; only the registrar signs.

What you need to know about this record

  • The father's declared age is written in very pale ink. The most probable reading is «ventiquattro» (24), but Francesco was 26 in November 1897. Another erroneous declared age.
  • The family has gone back to contrada Saporito, where they already were in 1895. In 1896 they were in S. Agostino and in 1899 they will be in Campagnano. They moved a great deal.
  • To locate this record it was necessary to discover that the digitiser rephotographs the same opening twice when a loose sheet is lying on top of it. The clean shot has been kept here.
Direct line

Settimo Salerno

In the records: Settimo — not «Settino»

The great-grandfather Gonzalo lived to know. A twin, the first born, at half past four in the morning of 24 October 1899 at Rende. He crossed the Atlantic, married at Santa Fe at twenty-three, and had to fight the Argentine civil registry for twenty-seven years to get his own name spelled correctly.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

Married Cristina Rizzo

Parent of Carlos Francisco Salerno · Nilda Salerno

Twenty-five minutes the elder

He was born on 24 October 1899 at half past four in the morning, in the contrada Campagnano at Rende. His brother Saverio was born twenty-five minutes later. Four days after, on 28 October at twenty to eleven in the morning, their father registered them in two consecutive records, with the same two witnesses and five minutes between one and the other.

That they were twins is not an inference from the closeness of the numbers: both records say so in writing and refer to each other. His says «e ch'è gemello un altro mi conserva l'atto seguente»; Saverio's, «l'atto precedente». And his adds a line nobody was obliged to write: «Il dichiarante ha inoltre attestato che il bambino suddetto è IL PRIMO NATO». The order of birth was supplied by the father himself, not by a midwife.

The index this investigation started from calls him «Settino». The original record says SETTIMO, quite clearly, both in the body and in the marginal heading. Settimo is the ordinary Italian name for a seventh child; on paper he is the fourth documented. Either the name is only a name, or there were earlier children who are not recorded. It proves nothing, but it points towards the fourteen children of family memory.

He belongs to the class of '99, the ragazzi del '99, the cohort Italy called up in 1917 at barely eighteen; the family keeps his First World War medals. He ended up at Santa Fe, Argentina, where he went by Setimio. His wife, Cristina Rizzo, died there in November 1967, and he between 1977 and 1980, aged seventy-eight or eighty-one. He does not appear in the index of arrivals at the port of Buenos Aires under any spelling of his name.

Marriage

Marriage record with Cristina Rizzo

city of Santa Fe, Argentina · record 140 · 26 March 1923

Archive
Registro Civil de la Provincia de Santa Fe — Archivo General
How it was obtained
digital application at santafe.gob.ar/rcdigital, requested by Gonzalo on 25 August 2026
Digital signature
César Gabriel Garrone, Archivo Central Santa Fe, 28-8-2026
Verification
application 26-00-284-316 · hash D436-F555-6EF1-EE25-20B7-FA23-3C5D-8A13-78B9-EA89

Translation

Record number one hundred and forty. In the city of SANTA FE, department of La Capital, province of Santa Fe, on the twenty-sixth day of March in the year nineteen hundred and twenty-three. Before me, head of the Registry of Civil Status, there appeared:

Don SETTIMO SALERNO, aged twenty-three, of ITALIAN nationality, BORN AT RENDE, resident in this city, by trade a BAKER; son of don FRANCISCO SALERNO, aged forty, Italian, RESIDENT AT RENDE, ITALY, farmer, and of doña FILOMENA CHIAPPETTA, aged fifty, Italian, resident at Rende;

and doña CRISTINA RIZZO, aged twenty-three, of ARGENTINE nationality; daughter of don SALVADOR RIZZO and of doña ÁNGELA COSTABILE, Italian, resident in this city.

Both parties stated that it was their will to marry, taking each other as husband and wife... I declare that don SETTIMO SALERNO and doña CRISTINA RIZZO are joined in lawful marriage.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT CONFIRMS HIS PARENTS BY THE ARGENTINE ROUTE. The Italian record of 1899 gave «Salerno Francesco, aged 28, and Chiappetta Filomena». This one, twenty-four years later and on another continent, gives the same two names — and adds that in 1923 they were STILL LIVING IN RENDE. They did not emigrate with their son.
  • AND AT LAST IT GIVES THE BRIDE'S PARENTS: SALVADOR RIZZO and ÁNGELA COSTABILE. Until this day the Rizzos were a single loose person in the whole tree.
  • HE WAS A BAKER. At twenty-three, newly arrived from Rende.
  • MIND THE FATHER'S AGE: the record puts him at forty, but the birth record of 1899 gave him twenty-eight, which would make him fifty-two in 1923. Twelve years out — understandable, because the father was in Italy and the age was given by the son from memory.
  • A MARGINAL NOTE OF 1950 CORRECTS HIS OWN NAME. The Dirección General del Registro Civil recorded, twenty-seven years later, «that the groom's true name is SETTIMO». On his marriage record it was misspelt.
Photograph

The four brothers in Argentina

Argentina — photographic studio · family photograph · c. 1925-1930

Provenance
family album, Argentine branch
Medium
studio print, painted backdrop with a palm tree

Translation

Four young men posing in a photographic studio, in front of a painted backdrop with a palm tree and a landscape. Two standing, in dark suits; two seated on a bench, in light summer suits with their hats on their knees — a straw boater with a dark band on the left, a soft-brimmed felt on the right. All in white stiff-collared shirts and wide dark ties.

The family identifies them as the FOUR SALERNO BROTHERS newly arrived in Argentina. SETTIMO and SAVERIO are certainly among them; about the other two there is no certainty.

What you need to know about this record

  • THE PHOTOGRAPH CORRECTS THE DATE OF THE EMIGRATION. The family always said they arrived «in 1919, after the war». The clothing says otherwise: the straw boater and the wide-striped, high-waisted trousers are from the mid-twenties onwards. And none of the four looks under a good twenty: if ARTURO, born in 1907, is one of them, in 1919 he would have been TWELVE YEARS OLD, and there is no boy of twelve there. Conclusion: either Arturo is not in the photograph, or THEY ARRIVED AROUND 1925-1930, not in 1919. And the second fits everything else: Antonio left alone in 1914, Giovanni was still in Rende marrying in 1926. The family did not break up all at once in 1919 — it frayed away over fifteen years. The 1919 date is memory, and memory has already failed once, with Antonio.
  • THE TWO SEATED MEN COULD BE THE TWINS. It is a hypothesis of the eye, not a fact: they are the two dressed alike — light summer suits — they are seated in symmetrical postures, both with their hats on their knees, and their faces resemble one another more than either of the other two pairs. Settimo and Saverio were born on the same day in 1899, twenty-five minutes apart. Nobody who knows that can look at the photograph without thinking of it.
  • WHO THE FOUR MIGHT BE, by documentary elimination. Of the seven sons, ANTONIO went to Chicago in 1914 and died there in 1981; GIOVANNI stayed in Rende and married there in 1926. That leaves five candidates for four chairs: GIUSEPPE (1895), UMBERTO (1897), SETTIMO (1899), SAVERIO (1899) and ARTURO (1907).
  • AND HERE IS SOMETHING WORTH THINKING ABOUT. The family remembers those who came as «Saverio, Settimo, and I would think Raffaele and Angelo». But Raffaele and Angelo appear in no record, whereas UMBERTO and ARTURO do exist and their fate is a complete blank. In this genealogy it has already happened twice that a person lived under a name different from the registered one: SETTIMO called himself Setimio in Argentina, and AQUILA appears as Rosalia on her own marriage record. It would not be strange if the men the family remembers as Raffaele and Angelo were Umberto and Arturo.
  • A fact so as not to get confused: ANGELINA, the sister born in 1902, DIED IN RENDE ON 29 AUGUST 1910, aged eight. She could not have emigrated. If family memory recalls an «Angelo», either it is another person or it is her, misremembered.
  • What the photograph does prove, beyond argument: THEY ARRIVED TOGETHER AND HAD A PORTRAIT TAKEN. Four brothers from a village in Calabria, in their good suits and with their hats in their hands, posing in front of a painted palm tree on the day they began another life. It is the only image of this family earlier than the grandparents' generation.

What the manuscript says

…a cui dà il nome di Settimo, e ch'è gemello un altro mi conserva l'atto seguente.

Il dichiarante ha inoltre attestato che il bambino suddetto è il primo nato.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-nine, on the twenty-eighth day of October, at twenty to eleven in the morning, at the town hall, before Pisani Domenico, senior assessor for the mayor being indisposed, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged twenty-eight, farmhand, resident at Rende.

He declares that of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, countrywoman, his wife living with him, there was born on the twenty-fourth of the current month, at half past four in the morning, in the house situated in contrada Campagnano, a child of the male sex, to whom he gives the name SETTIMO, and who is the twin of another recorded in the following entry.

Witnesses: Perri Pietro, aged sixty-one, farmhand; and Spizzirri Giovanni, aged forty-six, farmhand, both resident in the comune.

The informant further attested that the said child is THE FIRST BORN.

What you need to know about this record

  • THE NAME IS NOT «SETTINO» BUT SETTIMO. The index that was the starting point transcribes it wrongly. The original record says it clearly both in the body and in the marginal heading.
  • The father's age (28) is CORRECT, born in December 1870. It is one of the few times Francesco declares his age accurately.
  • «Settimo» means seventh, but by the index he is the fourth documented child. Either the name is only a name, or there were siblings before 1899 who are not on record. It is proof of nothing, but it points towards the fourteen children of family memory.
Sibling

Saverio Salerno

Settimo's twin, the second born, twenty-five minutes later. He was 20 in 1919.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

The second, by twenty-five minutes

He was born at four fifty-five in the morning on 24 October 1899, in the same house in the contrada Campagnano and twenty-five minutes after his brother.

His record was drawn up at ten forty-five on 28 October, five minutes after Settimo's, before the same official and with the same two witnesses — Perri Pietro, sixty-one, and Spizzirri Giovanni, forty-six, both farmhands. It closes with the mirror of the other's formula: «il bambino soddetto è IL SECONDO NATO». None of those present signed.

In 1919 he was twenty and was one of those who left. He does not appear in the Buenos Aires arrivals index, just like his twin.

What the manuscript says

…a cui dà il nome di Saverio, e ch'è gemello un altro mi conserva l'atto precedente.

Il dichiarante ha inoltre attestato che il bambino soddetto è il secondo nato.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-nine, on the twenty-eighth day of October, at a quarter to eleven in the morning — five minutes after the preceding record — at the town hall, before Pisani Domenico, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged twenty-eight, farmhand.

He declares that of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, countrywoman, his wife, there was born on the twenty-fourth of the current month, at four fifty-five in the morning, in the house situated in contrada Campagnano, a child of the male sex, to whom he gives the name SAVERIO, and who is the twin of another recorded in the preceding entry.

Witnesses: the same two as in the preceding record.

The informant further attested that the said child is THE SECOND BORN.

What you need to know about this record

  • The twins are not an inference from the proximity of the numbers: the two records say so in writing and refer to one another.
  • The fact of the double birth is supplied by the father himself («il dichiarante ha inoltre attestato»), not by the midwife.
Sibling

Angelina Salerno

Born in contrada Campagnano on 14 May 1902 and dead in contrada Sant'Agostino on 29 August 1910, aged eight years and three months. She did not emigrate.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

The record made her ten

She was born on 14 May 1902 at half past twelve, in the contrada Campagnano. Her father registered her two days later and declared thirty years: he was thirty-one. The witnesses were Presta Francesco, a twenty-six-year-old day labourer, and Bartucci Antonio, a seventy-year-old farmhand.

She died on 29 August 1910 at noon, in the contrada Sant'Agostino, in the same house where her brothers Giovanni and Arturo and her sister Maria had been born. The death record says «di anni dieci», ten years old. It is wrong, and the proof is two registers away: her own birth record. She was eight years and three months old.

It was not her parents who reported the death. It was her uncle Salerno Domenico, fifty, and her maternal grandfather, Chiappetta Giovanni, sixty, and between them they rounded the child's age badly. The grandfather signed the record; the others declared themselves illiterate. The witnesses were a twenty-six-year-old farmhand and a thirty-five-year-old barber.

The margin of her birth record is blank: the death was never noted there, although the record exists and sits in the next register along. For a time it was assumed that Angelina had emigrated to Argentina with the family. She did not: she died nine years before her brothers sailed.

What the manuscript says

è comparso Salerno Francesco, di anni trenta, bracciante, domiciliato in Rende…
…nella casa posta in contrada Campagnano, da Chiappetta Filomena sua moglie
contadina seco lui convivente… il nome di Angelina

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred and two, on the sixteenth day of May, at five past ten in the morning, at the town hall, before Pisani Domenico, assessor for the mayor being indisposed, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged thirty, BRACCIANTE (day labourer), resident at Rende.

He declares that at half past twelve on the fourteenth of the current month, in the house situated in CONTRADA CAMPAGNANO, of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, his wife, countrywoman, living with him, there was born a child of the female sex whom he presents to me, and to whom he gives the name ANGELINA.

Witnesses: Presta Francesco, aged twenty-six, day labourer; and Bartucci Antonio, aged seventy, farmhand, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to those present, they did not sign it with me, being illiterate, as they declared. Only Domenico Pisani signs.

What you need to know about this record

  • The margin of record 55 is BLANK: Angelina's death in 1910 was never annotated in the margin of her birth record, even though the death record exists and is right here alongside.
  • The father's trade shifts over the years: «contadino» in 1899, «bracciante» here in 1902, and «contadino» again in 1904, 1907 and 1909.
  • The contrada also changes: Campagnano in 1899 and 1902, Santo Agostino from 1904 onwards.

What the manuscript says

sono comparsi Salerno Domenico, di anni cinquanta, contadino, domiciliato in Rende,
e Chiappetta Giovanni, di anni sessanta, contadino, domiciliato in Rende…
…nella casa posta in contrada Sant'Agostino… è morta Salerno Angelina,
di anni dieci… nata in Rende da Francesco… e da Chiappetta Filomena

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred and ten, on the thirtieth day of August, at thirteen hours, at the town hall, before VERCILLO GIUSEPPE, assessor for the mayor, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared SALERNO DOMENICO, aged fifty, farmhand, resident at Rende, and CHIAPPETTA GIOVANNI, aged sixty, farmhand, resident at Rende.

Who declared to me that at twelve o'clock YESTERDAY, in the house situated in CONTRADA SANT'AGOSTINO, there died SALERNO ANGELINA, aged ten, countrywoman, resident at Rende, born at Rende, daughter of FRANCESCO, farmhand, resident at Rende, and of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, countrywoman, resident at Rende.

Witnesses: Perri Domenico, aged twenty-six, farmhand; and Vercillo Ferdinando, aged thirty-five, BARBER, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, I signed it with the informant CHIAPPETTA and with the witness VERCILLO, the others having stated that they were illiterate.

What you need to know about this record

  • THE AGE ON THE RECORD IS WRONG. It says «di anni dieci», ten years. Angelina was born on 14 May 1902 — her birth record is right here — so on 29 August 1910 she was EIGHT YEARS AND THREE MONTHS old. Those who declare are not the parents but an uncle and a grandfather, and they round upwards.
  • THE DECLARANTS ARE THE GOOD DATA. The first, SALERNO DOMENICO aged fifty, is Francesco's elder brother: ten weeks later he was marrying Giuseppina Cairo and declaring fifty-one. The two figures cross at a birth in about 1859.
  • The second, CHIAPPETTA GIOVANNI aged sixty, is Filomena's father, the maternal grandfather. He was already on record as her father in the birth record of 1877. He was still alive, and HE COULD SIGN: he is one of the few in the family who does not sign with a cross.
  • Neither of the girl's parents appears.
Sibling

Giovanni Salerno

Born on the first day of 1904, four years after the death of the uncle whose name he carried. He was 15 in 1919 — and in 1926 he married at Rende.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

He stayed at Rende

He was born on 1 January 1904 at two in the morning, in the contrada Santo Agostino: the family's first birth of the year, and one of the register's first records, number 5. His father registered him on the 3rd at ten in the morning and declared thirty-four years. He carries the name of his uncle, dead four years earlier at twenty-six.

In the margin of the record a later hand wrote, with a flourish: «Addì 24-7-1926 in Rende n° 34 sposò Cundari Anna». He married at Rende on 24 July 1926. That line changed the story: if he was in the village in 1926, either he never sailed or he came back. He was fifteen when the older ones left.

It is the document that best supports what the family tells: that the household split in two, that the elder brothers crossed, and that Francesco and Filomena stayed at Rende with the younger ones. And it means there is a living branch there, descended from Giovanni and Anna Cundari.

What the manuscript says

è comparso Salerno Francesco, di anni trentaquattro, contadino, domiciliato in Rende…
…del dì primo del corrente mese, nella casa posta in contrada Santo Agostino,

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred and four, on the third day of January, at ten in the morning, at the town hall, before MERCAVALLO MICHELANGELO, secretary delegated by act of the mayor dated the twentieth of August nineteen hundred and two, approved, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged thirty-four, farmhand, resident at Rende.

He declares that at two in the morning on the FIRST of the current month, in the house situated in CONTRADA SANTO AGOSTINO, of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, his wife, countrywoman, living with him, there was born a child of the male sex whom he presents to me, and to whom he gives the name GIOVANNI.

Witnesses: Cosentino Rosario, aged thirty-four, farmhand; and Alfano Domenico, aged forty-five, farmhand, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to those present, they did not sign it with me, being illiterate, as they declared. Only M. Mercavallo signs.

What you need to know about this record

  • MARGINAL ANNOTATION, in a later hand and with a flourish: «Addì 24-7-1926 in Rende n° 34 sposò Cundari Anna». Giovanni married in Rende on 24 July 1926, record no. 34, to CUNDARI ANNA. New information.
  • And weighty information: if he married in Rende in 1926, either he did not embark with the family in 1919 or he came back. The assumption that they all emigrated should be reviewed.
  • He was born on 1 January: he is among the first births of the year, and that is why his record bears the number 5 and is almost at the beginning of the volume — the index of this register is very short indeed.
  • The family is already living in contrada Santo Agostino; in 1902, with Angelina, they were in Campagnano.
Sibling

Arturo Salerno

Born before dawn on 21 December 1907 and registered that same morning. He was 12 in 1919.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

A carpenter and a shoemaker

He was born on 21 December 1907 at half past four in the morning, in the contrada S. Agostino, and his father registered him that same morning at ten to twelve: seven hours after the birth.

The witnesses on his record are not the usual day labourers. Signing as witnesses are Sicilia Pasquale, a sixty-one-year-old carpenter, and Bruno Giovanni, a seventy-seven-year-old shoemaker — two village trades and two old men. Francesco declared thirty-eight years on the very day he turned thirty-seven.

His record sits in a treacherous volume: Rende's «ordinary» register 99 has only two pages, and the real one is «supplement 2», with ninety-four. It is closed by Domenico Pisani at Rende on 1 January 1908.

He was twelve in 1919. He stayed in Italy with his parents.

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred and seven, on the twenty-first day of December, at ten to twelve in the morning, at the town hall, before PISANI DOMENICO, assessor for the mayor, Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged thirty-eight, farmhand, resident at Rende.

He declares that at half past four in the morning on the twenty-first of the current month, in the house situated in CONTRADA S. AGOSTINO, of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, countrywoman, his wife, living with him, there was born a child of the male sex whom he presents to me, and to whom he gives the name ARTURO.

Witnesses: Sicilia Pasquale, aged sixty-one, CARPENTER; and Bruno Giovanni, aged seventy-seven, SHOEMAKER, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to those present, they did not sign it with me, being illiterate, as they declared. Only Domenico Pisani signs.

What you need to know about this record

  • He was born and registered ON THE SAME DAY: birth at 4.30 in the morning, record at 11.50 in the morning, seven hours later. It is the fastest registration in the whole family.
  • The two witnesses are not countrymen, as is usually the case, but village artisans: a carpenter of 61 and a shoemaker of 77.
  • The volume is Reg. 99 «suppl. 2». The «normal» Reg. 99 has only two pages: it is a remnant. The supplement closes with the signature of Domenico Pisani on 1 January 1908.
Sibling

Maria Salerno

The youngest of the nine documented children. Born in contrada S. Agostino on the night of 23 September 1909; she was 10 in 1919.

Child of Francesco Salerno · Filomena Chiappetta

The clerk forgot his own name

She was born on the night of 23 September 1909, at twenty-five to twelve, in the contrada S. Agostino. She is the youngest of the nine documented children. Her father registered her the next morning and this time declared his age correctly: thirty-eight.

The record has an odd gap. The line «Avanti di me» — before me — was left blank: the clerk never got round to writing his own name. The man signing at the foot is Federico Costabile, who on neighbouring records appears as assessor delegated for civil registration. The witnesses were two farmhands, Ruffolo Serafino, fifty-three, and Rende Tommaso, thirty-five.

On the same opening of the register, the next record carries as a witness a «Salerno Francesco, di anni trentotto, contadino»: same age, same trade. It is him, staying on as a witness for the neighbour after registering his daughter. That record was reported by a midwife because the husband «è in America». The village was already emigrating.

She was ten in 1919 and stayed with her parents. The family remembers a «Marietta» who ended up in Canada; Marietta is the ordinary diminutive of Maria, so it could be her or her aunt Maria Chiappetta, born in 1901. No document settles it.

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred and nine, on the twenty-fourth day of September, at twenty to eleven in the morning, at the town hall. Before [the line for the registrar's name was left BLANK], Registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of Rende, there appeared SALERNO FRANCESCO, aged thirty-eight, farmhand, resident at Rende.

He declares that at twenty-three thirty-five on the twenty-third of the current month, in the house situated in CONTRADA S. AGOSTINO, of CHIAPPETTA FILOMENA, countrywoman, his LAWFUL wife, living with him, there was born a child of the female sex whom he presents to me, and to whom he gives the name MARIA.

Witnesses: Ruffolo Serafino, aged fifty-three, farmhand; and Rende Tommaso, aged thirty-five, farmhand, both resident in this comune.

The record having been read to all present, they did not sign it with me, being illiterate. Signed FEDERICO COSTABILE.

What you need to know about this record

  • The clerk left the line «Avanti di me» UNFILLED: the record does not say who authorised it. The signature at the foot resolves it, Federico Costabile, who in the neighbouring records of the same opening appears as «assessore delegato per gli atti di Stato Civile».
  • Here the father's declared age is EXACT for once: thirty-eight years, and he had been born in December 1870. In 1902 and 1904 he had fallen short or long by a year.
  • In the SAME opening, record 207 has as a witness a «Salerno Francesco, di anni trentotto, contadino»: same age and same trade. It is him, staying on as a witness for his neighbour after registering his daughter. And that record 207 is declared by a midwife because the husband «è in America»: the village was already emigrating.
Nephew/niece

Salvatore Salerno

A first cousin, not a brother. Son of Giovanni and Vincenzina. He was thirteen months old when his father died.

Child of Giovanni Salerno · Vincenzina Morelli

He was taken for a brother

He was born on 12 January 1899 at three in the morning, in a contrada written in a hand so fine that it reads «Mari», «Nari» or «Maio» and is none of the ones known to the family. His father registered him the following day at twenty past nine, before Pisani Domenico. The witnesses were a fifty-one-year-old farmer and a thirty-year-old farmhand.

His record was ordered on the assumption that he was another child of Francesco and Filomena. He is not, and the original is emphatic: the informant is SALERNO GIOVANNI and the mother MORELLI VINCENZINA. Salvatore is the son of Francesco's younger brother — that is, Settimo's first cousin and not his brother. It fits without forcing anything: his parents married in October 1897 and he was born in January 1899.

The find also produced the hardest fact yet about his father. When Giovanni died, on 23 February 1900, Salvatore was thirteen months old. His mother was left a widow with him. And one more age that does not add up: Giovanni declared twenty-four years when registering him, when he had turned twenty-six.

In the margin of the record a twentieth-century hand noted: «Addì 18.10.1921 in Rende n° 67 sposò Misuraca Caterina Santa». He married at Rende in October 1921, aged twenty-two.

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and ninety-nine, on the thirteenth day of January, at twenty past nine in the morning, at the town hall, before Pisani Domenico, senior assessor for the mayor being indisposed, there appeared SALERNO GIOVANNI, aged twenty-four, farmhand, resident at Rende.

He declares that of MORELLI VINCENZINA, countrywoman, his wife living with him, there was born on the twelfth of the current month, at three in the morning, in the house situated in contrada Mari, a child of the male sex, upon whom he bestows the name SALVATORE.

Witnesses: Cannata Giovanni, aged fifty-one, farmer; and Cannataro Giuseppe, aged thirty, farmhand.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS SALVATORE IS A NEPHEW, NOT A BROTHER. The record was ordered in the belief that he was a son of Francesco and Filomena, and the original is emphatic: the declarant is Salerno GIOVANNI and the mother MORELLI VINCENZINA.
  • It fits perfectly: his parents' wedding in October 1897, first child in January 1899. And it yields a hard fact: when Giovanni died on 23 February 1900, he left a son of thirteen months.
  • Marginal annotation in a twentieth-century hand: «Addì 18.10.1921 in Rende n° 67 sposò Misuraca Caterina Santa».
  • If the family was expecting a brother named Salvatore, he must be looked for in another year.

Pisani Domenico, assessor delegated by the mayor

What the manuscript says

1.° Salerno Domenico, vedovo, di anni cinquantuno, contadino, nato in Castrolibero,
residente in Rende, figlio di Michele, residente in Rende, e di Palermo Maria,
residente in Rende;
2.° Cairo Giuseppina, nubile, di anni trentadue, contadina, nata in Rende,
residente in Rende, figlia di fu Giovanni, residente in vita in Rende,
e di fu Busatta Rosa, residente in vita in Rende

Translation

In the year nineteen hundred and ten, on the fifth day of November, at half past nine in the morning, at the town hall of Rende, open to the public. Before PISANI DOMENICO, assessor delegated by the mayor, Registrar of the Civil Status, in official dress, there appeared in person:

1st SALERNO DOMENICO, WIDOWER, aged FIFTY-ONE, farmhand, BORN AT CASTROLIBERO, resident at Rende, son of MICHELE, resident at Rende, and of PALERMO MARIA, resident at Rende;

2nd CAIRO GIUSEPPINA, unmarried, aged THIRTY-TWO, countrywoman, born at Rende, resident at Rende, daughter of the late GIOVANNI, who in life resided at Rende, and of the late ROSA BUSATTA, who in life resided at Rende.

Who requested me to unite them in marriage. To that end they presented me with one document, described below; and from the examination of that, and of those already produced when the banns were requested — all of them, with my endorsement, incorporated into the volume of annexes of this register — it appearing to me that nothing stands in the way of the celebration of their marriage, I read to the spouses articles one hundred and thirty, one hundred and thirty-one and one hundred and thirty-two of the Civil Code, and then asked the husband whether he intends to take as his wife the CAIRO GIUSEPPINA here present, and her whether she intends to take as her husband the SALERNO DOMENICO here present; and each having answered me in the affirmative, in the full hearing also of the witnesses named below, I pronounced in the name of the law that they are united in marriage.

Present at this act were: LO CELSO RAFFAELE, aged forty-eight, TAILOR, and SOTTILE GASPARE, aged twenty-seven, GENTLEMAN, both resident in this comune.

The only document presented to me is the certificate of the banns celebrated by me on the successive Sundays the sixteenth and the twenty-third of October of the current year.

The record having been read to those present, I signed it only with the witnesses, the spouses having stated that they were illiterate. Signed by Lo Celso Raffaele, Sottile Gaspare and Domenico Pisani. THE COUPLE DO NOT SIGN.

What you need to know about this record

  • DOMENICO'S AGE, AT LAST. Fifty-one years on 5 November 1910: he was born between November 1858 and November 1859. And ten weeks earlier, declaring the death of his niece Angelina, he had said fifty. The two figures cross at 1859. It fits with his parents' wedding, celebrated in Rende on 22 August 1858: Domenico would be the eldest son, born some ten or eleven months later.
  • THE VILLAGE: CASTROLIBERO. The record says so unambiguously. It clashes with the sweep that had ruled Castrolibero out after reading the fourteen years from 1859 to 1872. The most probable explanation is that the sweep began in 1859 and that he was born at the end of 1858 or in the first months of 1859 — or that his record is registered as DE RANGO, the surname Michele used in Castrolibero, and was overlooked when searching for «Salerno». One must go back to Castrolibero, Nati 1858 and 1859, looking for DE RANGO DOMENICO of Michele and Maria Palermo.
  • A WIDOWER. It is confirmed that this was not his first wedding. The record does not say whom he was widowed of nor when, so that first marriage and the death of the first wife remain outstanding.
  • MICHELE AND MARIA ANTONIA WERE STILL ALIVE. It is the collateral finding and it is a weighty one. The form distinguishes the living from the dead with rigour: of the bride it says «figlia di FU Giovanni, residente IN VITA in Rende», and in record 43 on the same page, «figlio di FU Francesco». Of the groom, by contrast, it says «figlio di Michele, residente in Rende, e di Palermo Maria, residente in Rende», with no «fu» and no «in vita». Michele Salerno, the foundling of 1825, and Maria Antonia Palermo were alive in Rende on 5 November 1910, aged 85 and 71.
  • The surname of the bride's mother is at the limit of the scan's resolution: it reads «Busatta», but it could be «Bosatta» or «Barsatta». The original measures 3008 px and gives no more.
  • No parental consent is recorded, which is consistent with the ages of the bride and groom.
Ancestor

Domenico Faienza

In the records: Domingo Faienza in Argentina

Son of Nicola Faienza and Angiolina Pizzi. He emigrated to Argentina, where he died at Santa Fe on 13 December 1985, aged eighty-five. HIS ARGENTINE DEATH CERTIFICATE TURNED UP IN AUGUST 2026 AND CHANGED EVERYTHING: it says he was BORN AT PAGLIETA (Italy) on 1 March 1900, and it confirms his parents. The Italian record has still not been found, but now it is known exactly where and when to look — and why it never surfaced: it had been sought in 1899, and he was born in 1900.

Child of Nicola Faienza · Angela Pizzi

Married Brigida Maria Ana Nelli

Parent of Adelina Faienza

Birth
1 March 1900 at PAGLIETA (Chieti), according to his Argentine death certificate of 1985. The same date as the cemetery register already gave: two independent Argentine sources agree
Parents
NICOLA FAIENZA, contadino of Atessa, son of Giuseppe Faienza and Maria Staniscia; and ANGIOLINA (Angela) PIZZI. Married at Atessa on 29 December 1892, record 101
Death
13 December 1985, aged 85, buried at Santa Fe (La Capital), Argentina
Where he was NOT born (1)
PAGLIETA in 1899. The complete annual index read: 161 births, not one Faienza under any letter
Where he was NOT born (2)
ATESSA in 1899. The complete annual index read: 396 births and four Faienza — Antonio, Carmine, Luzio and Rosina —, no Domenico. And all four have been opened: they belong to three other Faienza families of the village, not to ours
Which village the FAIENZA come from
ATESSA: the marriage record of 1892 declares the father born and resident there, and the paternal grandfather too
Where HE was born
PAGLIETA — and that is not the same thing. The parents married at Atessa in December 1892 and moved to Paglieta, thirteen kilometres away, where the son was born eight years later
Where it has to be looked for
PAGLIETA, BIRTHS OF 1900, a male child born on 1 March. But NOT over the internet: Antenati has Paglieta only as far as 1899. It has to be asked of the TOWN COUNCIL OF PAGLIETA, which is the body that keeps the books the Archivio di Stato has not yet received. Letter written and ready, with the contact details checked on the comune's official website
Death
13 December 1985 at 7:30 p.m., at Santa Fe. Retired, and still of Italian nationality after a whole life in Argentina. He lived at calle JUAN PABLO LÓPEZ 2643

Death

Death certificate

Santa Fe, department of La Capital, Argentina · volume 4-C, record 1155 · 13 December 1985

Archive
Registro Civil de la Provincia de Santa Fe — Archivo General
How it was obtained
digital application at santafe.gob.ar/rcdigital, requested by Gonzalo on 25 August 2026
Digital signature
César Gabriel Garrone, Archivo Central Santa Fe, 26-8-2026
Verification
application 26-00-278-953 · hash EABE-3072-19D1-FC11-B4CB-603E-7871-EC78-4C2D-5E8C, checkable at santafe.gob.ar/rcdigital/valida-firma

Translation

At SANTA FE, department of La Capital, province of Santa Fe, Argentine Republic, on 19 December 1985. I, Public Officer of this Registry, record the DEATH of DOMINGO FAIENZA. Male, ITALIAN nationality. Status: married. Occupation: RETIRED. Identity document: Cédula 177,029. Address: J. P. López 2643.

SON OF NICOLÁS FAIENZA AND ÁNGELA PIZZI.

BORN AT PAGLIETA (ITALY) ON 1 MARCH 1900.

Occurring at Santa Fe on 13 December 1985 at 19:30. Cause of death: illness. Medical certificate of Doctor César Carli, 19 December 1985. Informant: SIXTO GÓMEZ, L.E. 5,261,009, address Berutti 5663, acting by virtue of being a person authorised for that purpose. — Arsenia del R. Ibáñez de Quintana, Deputy Head of the Deaths Subsection.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT PUTS PAGLIETA BACK ON THE MAP, AND CORRECTS SOMETHING I HAD TAKEN AS CLOSED. After reading the Atessa marriage record of 1892 I wrote that the Paglieta lead was «definitively» knocked down. That was wrong. That record proved that NICOLA, the father, had been born and was living in Atessa in 1892 — it said nothing about where his son would be born eight years later. This certificate says it in plain lettering: PAGLIETA. The two things are compatible.
  • AND IT EXPLAINS WHY I COULD NOT FIND HIM: I SEARCHED IN THE WRONG YEAR. The birth indexes of Paglieta 1899 and of Atessa 1899 were swept in full without finding any Domenico Faienza. Naturally: he was born on 1 March **1900**, a year that has never been looked at. Those negatives still hold good, but they were for the wrong year.
  • IT CONFIRMS HIS PARENTS BY THE ARGENTINE ROUTE. «Son of NICOLÁS FAIENZA and of ÁNGELA PIZZI» is exactly the couple of the Atessa marriage record of 1892. Two archives in two different countries, unconnected with one another, give the same two names.
  • HE DIED AN ITALIAN. After more than sixty years in Argentina the record still registers him as of Italian nationality: he never naturalised.
  • THE DECLARANT WAS NOT FAMILY. Sixto Gómez «acts by virtue of being a person authorised for that purpose»: he is an agent, almost certainly from the undertaker's. Berutti 5663 is HIS address, not the Faienzas'.
