What the manuscript says

8 · Celeri · Tebaldo · V · 33 · C · Italia · — · Peluquero · sí
9 · Fernandez · Concepción · M · 25 · C · Argentina · Córdoba · — · sí
10 · Celeri · Enriqueta · M · 3 · — · Argentina · Santa Fe · — · —

Translation

The columns of the census form are: surname, forename, whether male or female, how many years completed, whether single / married / widowed, which nation they belong to, if Argentine which province they were born in, what profession or trade they have, and whether they can read and write.

· **TEBALDO CELERI**, male, THIRTY-THREE, MARRIED, of ITALIAN nationality, by trade a **BARBER**, can read and write. · **CONCEPCIÓN FERNÁNDEZ**, female, TWENTY-FIVE, MARRIED, ARGENTINE, born in the province of **CÓRDOBA**, can read and write. · **ENRIQUETA CELERI**, female, THREE, ARGENTINE, born in **SANTA FE**.

On the same page live a Campana family of French nationality, some Arzongas, merchants and shop assistants, a Peralta and a Moirad: the portrait of a newly founded farming frontier town, with French, Italians and Argentines mixed together.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE PHOTOGRAPH OF TEBALDO'S FIRST FAMILY, FIVE YEARS BEFORE HE WAS WIDOWED. The three of them one after another on the schedule: the married couple and the girl. It is the only document in which they appear together.
  • THE AGE TALLIES TO THE DAY. Thirty-three years in 1895 → born in 1862, which is exactly what his Trassilico record says: 25 July 1862. Two archives separated by the Atlantic and by thirty-three years, with the same figure.
  • HE WAS A BARBER. Not a farmer or a day labourer, like almost all his neighbours. And he can read and write. It fits with the family he left behind in Trassilico: a father who was town clerk and a grandfather who was a physician.
  • AND HERE THE FIRST WIFE CEASES TO BE A LOOSE NAME. The death record of her daughter, in 1946, called her ENCARNACIÓN FERNÁNDEZ. The census, fifty-one years earlier and in her lifetime, calls her **CONCEPCIÓN FERNÁNDEZ**. They are two Marian invocations and are easily confused half a century later; most probably the good name is the one in the census, written down when she was standing there.
  • And it gives what was missing about her: **ARGENTINE, BORN IN THE PROVINCE OF CÓRDOBA, aged twenty-five in 1895 → born about 1870**. She was not Italian. Her birth record would be in Córdoba, and her marriage to Tebaldo — earlier than 1892 — in Santa Fe.
  • ENRIQUETA WAS THREE YEARS OLD IN 1895 → born about 1892. Her death record of 1946 gives her fifty-two years, which would give 1894. Two years' difference; the census is preferred, taken when the girl was alive and standing there.
  • VERA WAS A NEW TOWN. The department of Vera was colonised in the eighteen-eighties; by 1895 Tebaldo had already been some fifteen years in Argentina and was the local barber.

What the manuscript says

En Vera, el día veinticinco del mes de Octubre del año de mil novecientos, autorizó el Matrimonio de Don TEBALDO CELERI de treinta y ocho años, natural de Italia, de estado VIUDO, hijo legítimo de Don CALISTO CELERI y de Doña ENRIQUETA VANNI, domiciliado en Jobson (Est. Vera), con Doña MARGARITA ALASIA de diecinueve años, natural de Col. Emilia, de estado soltera, hija legítima de Don BENITO ALASIA y de Doña MAGDALENA BERTINI, domiciliada en Vera, siendo testigos del acto Don DOMINGO ALASIA y Doña MARÍA DE ALASIA.

Translation

The record is in Spanish and is transcribed as it stands. What it says, fact by fact:

· HIM: Tebaldo Celeri, THIRTY-EIGHT (born around 1862), a native of ITALY — no village specified — of WIDOWED status, lawful son of CALISTO CELERI and ENRIQUETA VANNI, resident at JOBSON, beside Vera station. · HER: Margarita Alasia, NINETEEN, a native of COLONIA EMILIA (Santa Fe), unmarried, lawful daughter of BENITO ALASIA and MAGDALENA BERTINI, resident at Vera. · WITNESSES: Domingo Alasia and María de Alasia — relatives of hers.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS RECORD TURNS A GAP INTO A PERSON. Until yesterday, Elba's father was «an Italian immigrant, from the north I think», without a name. Now he has a name, a year of birth, an Argentine town, two marriages and two parents.
  • «OF WIDOWED STATUS» — and there an entire family was hidden. The death record of his daughter Enriqueta, in Chaco in 1946, names as her mother ENCARNACIÓN FERNÁNDEZ. She is the first wife, the one who died before 1900. There is therefore an earlier Celeri-Fernández branch, unexplored.
  • NINETEEN YEARS OLD SHE, THIRTY-EIGHT HE. Exactly double. A middle-aged widower marrying the daughter of another settler family: the commonest pattern in the north of Santa Fe at the time.
  • THIS RECORD DOES NOT SAY WHICH VILLAGE HE WAS FROM — it puts only «a native of Italy». It was resolved, that same day, by a document nobody had gone looking for with that in mind: the death record of his MOTHER, who died in TRASSILICO (Lucca) in 1923. An emigrant's papers rarely say where he came from; those of the one who stayed behind always do.
  • JOBSON still exists: it is a locality of the department of Vera, in the north of Santa Fe, born around the railway station.
Spouse

Margarita Alasia

In the records: daughter of «Benito Alasia y Magdalena Bertini» in the record

Gonzalo's paternal great-grandmother. Born at Colonia Emilia, in the heart of the Piedmontese farming colonies of Santa Fe, and married at nineteen to an Italian widower twice her age.

Child of Benedetto Alasia · Maria Maddalena Bertino

Married Tebaldo Celeri

Parent of Elva Agustina Celeri · Olga Celeri · Ercilia Celeri · Rosa Celeri · Nerina Celeri

Birth
c. 1882, COLONIA EMILIA, province of Santa Fe — from the nineteen years she declares on marrying. Argentine by birth, Italian by blood
Parents
BENEDETTO (Benito) ALASIA and MARIA MADDALENA BERTINO — from her marriage record of 1900
Marriage
25 October 1900, Vera, Santa Fe, to Tebaldo Celeri
Daughters
Elba, Olga, Ercilia, Rosa and Nerina Celeri. There may be more
Where her family came from
SAVIGLIANO, province of CUNEO, PIEDMONT — her parents married there, in the church of San Giovanni Battista, on 13 April 1857

No record obtained yet

Her marriage record is filed in Tebaldo's file. Her birth certificate —Colonia Emilia, c. 1881— and her death are missing. AN INFERENCE THAT IS FIRM, AND THAT CHANGES WHERE ONE MUST LOOK. The 1900 record says that she was «a native of COL. EMILIA» and that she was NINETEEN YEARS OLD. She was born, therefore, IN ARGENTINA, c. 1881. And if she was born here, HER PARENTS HAD ALREADY DISEMBARKED BEFORE 1881 — that is, BEFORE THE CEMLA REGISTER BEGAN, which starts in 1882. For that reason there is no point in wasting time looking for them in the disembarkation lists: they arrived before those existed. AND BY THE SAME TOKEN A LEAD THAT LOOKED GOOD FALLS AWAY. In CISEI there figures a complete ALASIA family group disembarked on 25 February 1882 —Bartolomeo aged 17, Battista aged 14, Clemente aged 8 and a Bartolomeo of six months—. It was noted down as «to be verified». IT CANNOT BE THIS FAMILY: they arrive after Margarita was born in Colonia Emilia, and a mother with a six-month-old baby in February 1882 cannot have had another daughter in 1881. It is ruled out. SHE TURNED UP IN THE 1887 CENSUS, AGED FIVE. Colonia Emilia, volume 12, booklet 12: «ALAZIO MARGARITA, 5, Argentina, Santa Fe», daughter of OLAZIO BENEDETTO, 65, and of BERTINA MADALENA, 47. With her, her siblings DOMINGO (24, already married to MARÍA MOLINA), NICOLÁS (15), JUAN (11), FRANCISCO (9), MARÍA (7) and MADALENIA (1). Her mother declares FOURTEEN CHILDREN. HER TWO ELDER BROTHERS SIGNED HER WEDDING. The witnesses of the 1900 record, «Don DOMINGO ALASIA and Doña MARÍA DE ALASIA», are that Domingo and his wife. AND THE CENSUS DATES THE EMIGRATION without any need for ship lists: Domingo (b. 1863) and Nicolás (b. 1872) were born in Italy; Juan (b. 1876) was already born in Santa Fe. THE FAMILY DISEMBARKED BETWEEN 1872 AND 1876 — ten years before the CEMLA register existed, which is exactly why they never appeared in it. THE WITNESSES OF HER WEDDING ARE FAMILY OF HERS, almost certainly: Don DOMINGO ALASIA and Doña MARÍA DE ALASIA — «de Alasia» is the Argentine formula for a married woman. A brother and a sister-in-law. Looking for them is another way of getting to the parents. HER SURNAME OPENS A SECOND ITALY, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME ONE. The Alasia come from SAVIGLIANO, in the province of CUNEO, Piedmont; the Celeri, according to the family, from Tuscany. Grandmother Elba therefore had an Italian father and a mother who was the daughter of Italians, from two different regions of the north. Family memory said «from the north» and was right twice over.

Spouse

Concepción Fernández

In the records: «Encarnación Fernández» on her daughter's death record, of 1946

Tebaldo Celeri's first wife, and she was not Italian: an Argentine from CÓRDOBA. For one day she was only a name in this tree, taken from a daughter's death certificate; the 1895 census put her back among the living, with an age, a province and, beside her, her husband and the little girl.

Her parents, her birth certificate and her death are missing — but it is already known where to look for them. HER BIRTH, c. 1870, would be in the province of CÓRDOBA: civil registry or parish. HER MARRIAGE to Tebaldo is earlier than 1892 and would be from Santa Fe or from Córdoba; it would name her parents and —what for days was the open question of this branch— his Italian town. HER DEATH falls between May 1895 and October 1900, in Vera or thereabouts: a window of five and a half years, narrow and traceable. AND IF SHE HAD MORE CHILDREN, there are undiscovered half-siblings of Elba. In 1895 only Enriqueta is recorded, aged three, but between that date and her death she could have had more.

NÚMERO ochenta y ocho. En Villa Ángela, Departamento Río Zapallar, Territorio Nacional del Chaco, República Argentina, a diez y seis de Agosto de mil novecientos cuarenta y seis, Yo, Encargado del Registro del Estado Civil, procedo a inscribir la DEFUNCIÓN de: ENRIQUETA C. Esta inscripción se efectúa según declaración prestada por CASIMIRO GONZÁLEZ, estado casado, edad cincuenta y ocho años, domiciliado en Villa Ángela, quien ha visto el cadáver. Ante el testigo HÉCTOR J. CARRARA, estado soltero, edad veintinueve años.

Translation

The record is in Spanish and is transcribed literally.

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE RECORD THAT UNCOVERS TEBALDO'S FIRST MARRIAGE. The record of 1900 gave him as «widowed» and did not say of whom. This one, forty-six years later and in another province, names the mother: ENCARNACIÓN FERNÁNDEZ. Two documents that know nothing of each other, fitting together at one corner.
  • SHE WAS CALLED ENRIQUETA AFTER HER ITALIAN GRANDMOTHER, Enrichetta Vanni, who in 1894 was still alive in Italy and died in 1923. Tebaldo gave his daughter his mother's name, ten thousand kilometres away.
  • SHE DIED IN THE CHACO, not in Santa Fe. The family moved north, to the agricultural frontier: Villa Ángela was then a national territory, not a province.
  • Her siblings remain to be looked for: if Tebaldo and Encarnación had more children, they are Elba's half-siblings and there is Argentine descent still unlocated.
Ancestor

Callisto Celeri

In the records: «Calisto» in the Argentine record of 1900

TOWN CLERK OF TRASSILICO — the man who wrote the village's records. Born around 1831, son of a Desiderio Celeri already dead by then. Father of Tebaldo, and the proof that this family were not peasants.

Child of Desiderio Celeri · Angela Menghi

Married Enrichetta Vanni

Parent of Tebaldo Celeri · Ciro Celeri · Dante Giuseppe Celeri · Giuseppe Tullio Polinice Celeri

Name
CALLISTO in the Italian record of 1923; «Calisto» in the Argentine one of 1900. From the Latin Callistus. It is rare, and for that reason the easiest to trace in the whole branch
Birth
between November 1830 and July 1831 — he declares THIRTY-ONE YEARS in November 1861 and again in July 1862, which brackets the date
Father
DESIDERIO CELERI, already deceased in 1862 — «Celeri Callisto FU Desiderio»
Trade
POSSIDENTE E SEGRETARIO COMUNALE of the Comune di Trassilico. Both things at once: he had property and he kept the civil registration and the correspondence of the comune. Eight records of 1861 and 1862 describe him in exactly those terms
How he was addressed
the records call him SIGNORE: «alla presenza delli SIGNORI Andreini Dottor Pietro… e Celeri Callisto». He and the doctor were the two witnesses ex officio of the village — the only two lettered men in a comune where almost every declarant signs «per essere illetterato»

Photograph

His handwriting

Trassilico, Garfagnana · eight records from seven different registers of the Comune di Trassilico · 1858 a 1866

Archive
Archivio di Stato di MASSA (archive 240)
Collection
Stato civile della Restaurazione > Trassilico — registers 54.7 (Morti 1858), 53.1 (Matrimoni 1859), 53.3 (Matrimoni 1861), 51.5 (Nati 1862), 51.7 (Nati 1864) and 51.8 (Nati 1865)

What the manuscript says

Compilato dal sottoscritto SEGRETARIO COMUNALE il presente Indice questo giorno 30 Gennajo 1859. — CELERI

Translation

Four samples of the same hand, taken from four different registers:

· **1858** — at the foot of the alphabetical index of deaths: «This index compiled by the undersigned TOWN CLERK, today 30 January 1859. — Celeri». **He wrote that whole index**, and signed it SIX DAYS BEFORE HIS WEDDING. · **1859** — «Celeri Callisto, dichiarante», at the foot of his own marriage record. · **1861** — «Celeri Callisto, testimone», at the wedding of Maria Celeri, Nicolao's daughter. · **1862** — «Celeri Callisto, dichiarante», the day he went to register Tebaldo. · **1863, January** — «Compilato il presente indice da me sottoscritto — CELERI, Segretario Comunale», closing the index of marriages for 1862. · **1863, April** — «Celeri Callisto, testimone», at the wedding of his sister-in-law LUISA VANNI to Doctor Matteo Vincenti. · **1864** — «Celeri Callisto, testimone», on 3 July, standing as witness at the civil registration of a neighbour's child. · **1866** — «Compilato il presente indice da me sottoscritto — CELERI, Segretario Comunale», closing the index of births for 1865. **It is the last signature of his that is known.**

What you need to know about this record

  • HOW FAR THE CERTAINTY GOES, AND IT IS WORTH SAYING SO. What is proven is that these signatures say «CELERI» and the office «SEGRETARIO COMUNALE», and that Callisto was the town clerk of Trassilico — that is on record in eight documents between 1859 and 1862. From there to asserting that he wrote in his own hand the entire body of each index is a step that is NOT demonstrated: a clerk could sign the work of a copyist. What is certainly his are the SIGNATURES: «Celeri Callisto, dichiarante» at the foot of his own marriage record and of Tebaldo's, and «Celeri» closing the indexes. AND THESE SIGNATURES DATE HIS CAREER, WHICH WAS WHAT WAS BEING SOUGHT. It had been an ongoing attempt to establish when Callisto left the clerkship, because narrowing the date of his death depended on it. Now there is firm ground: in 1864 he signs as a witness in records 24, 25, 47, 48, 49, 52 and 53 of the register of births, always as «Celeri Callisto fu Desiderio, SEGRETARIO COMUNALE»; and at the beginning of 1866 it is still he who compiles and signs the index of births for 1865. **HE WAS STILL IN OFFICE IN 1866, aged thirty-six.** Whatever comes after that is already Italian civil registration and is not in Antenati. EVEN SO, IT IS THE ONLY PHYSICAL THING LEFT OF HIM. Of no other person in this tree — a hundred and forty-one of them, two hundred years — is any trace of the body preserved except the description of Antonio Salerno on his 1942 draft registration card. Of Callisto what remains is his handwriting.
  • He signs as clerk the annual indexes of deaths for 1856, 1857, 1858, 1860 and 1861 — every year, at the end of January, he closed the previous year's. The indexes we have swept looking for his father carry his signature at the foot.
  • AND THAT OPENS A ROUTE TO DATING HIS DEATH. If he signed every year, the year in which his signature disappears brackets when he left office. In the index for 1865 — the last of the holding — HE IS NO LONGER THERE: it is authenticated by «D.r PIETRO ANDREINI, ufficiale dello stato civile», the same physician who was a witness at Tebaldo's birth. BUT BEWARE: the format of the register changed in between, with the unification of Italy, and the clerk's line simply no longer appears. The absence does NOT prove that he had died. What would really bracket it are the indexes for 1862, 1863 and 1864.
  • THE COINCIDENCE OF 30 JANUARY 1859. That day he signed the index of the previous year's dead… and that same day he married Enrichetta in the church of Trassilico. The wedding in the morning or in the afternoon, and the work finished all the same.
  • His signature is short and decided — «Celeri», with a long flourish beneath it — set against the schoolroom, laborious hand of those declarants who could write, and against the dozens of records in the same register that end «omessa la firma per essere illetterato».
Ancestor

Enrichetta Vanni

In the records: «Doña Enriqueta Vanni» in the Argentine record of 1900

Gonzalo's paternal great-great-grandmother. She was born and died at TRASSILICO, a mountain village in the Garfagnana, province of Lucca, and lived eighty-eight years without leaving it. Her death record is the one that gave this whole branch a village: until it surfaced, all that was known of Tebaldo was that he was «a native of Italy».

What the manuscript says

Numero 10 — VANNI ENRICHETTA fu PIETRO, VEDOVA CELERI.
L'anno millenovecento ventitre, addì dieci di Settembre, a ore pomeridiane due e minuti venticinque, nella Casa Comunale. Avanti di me Biglini Pasquale Commissario Prefettizio ed Ufficiale dello stato civile del Comune di TRASSILICO, sono comparsi Bonetti Angelo di Jacopo, di anni quaranta, agricoltore, domiciliato in Trassilico, e Simoni Cirio fu Egisto, di anni trentadue, bracciante, domiciliato in Trassilico, i quali mi hanno dichiarato che a ore antimeridiane tre e minuti trenta di ieri, nella casa posta in Trassilico in VIA DEL CENTRO al numero diciassette, è morta VANNI ENRICHETTA di anni ottantotto, atta a casa, residente in Trassilico, nata in Trassilico, da fu PIETRO, domiciliato in vita a Trassilico, e da fu RIGHINI LIVIA, domiciliata in vita in detto luogo, MOGLIE DI CELERI CALLISTO. [al margen: anzi VEDOVA]
A quest'atto sono stati presenti quali testimoni Bonetti Jacopo fu Angelo, di anni settantaquattro, agricoltore, e Paoli Amilcare fu Giacomo, di anni trentadue, falegname, ambi residenti in questo Comune.

Translation

Number 10 — VANNI ENRICHETTA, daughter of the late Pietro, WIDOW CELERI.

In the year nineteen hundred and twenty-three, on the TENTH OF SEPTEMBER, at twenty-five past two in the afternoon, at the Town Hall. Before me, PASQUALE BIGLINI, prefectural commissioner and registrar of the civil status of the COMUNE OF TRASSILICO, there appeared ANGELO BONETTI, son of Jacopo, aged forty, farmer, resident at Trassilico, and CIRIO SIMONI, son of the late Egisto, aged thirty-two, day labourer, resident at Trassilico.

Who declared to me that AT HALF PAST THREE YESTERDAY MORNING, in the house situated at TRASSILICO, IN VIA DEL CENTRO, NUMBER SEVENTEEN, there died ENRICHETTA VANNI, AGED EIGHTY-EIGHT, keeping her own house, resident at Trassilico, BORN AT TRASSILICO, daughter of the late PIETRO, resident in life at Trassilico, and of the late LIVIA RIGHINI, resident in life in the same place, WIFE OF CALLISTO CELERI. — In the margin, correcting: «rather WIDOW».

Present as witnesses were JACOPO BONETTI, son of the late Angelo, aged seventy-four, farmer, and AMILCARE PAOLI, son of the late Giacomo, aged thirty-two, carpenter, both resident in this comune.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS IS THE RECORD THAT GIVES THE WHOLE BRANCH A VILLAGE. The two Argentine documents of her son Tebaldo — the marriage of 1900 and the death of his granddaughter in 1946 — say only «a native of Italy». Neither gives the village. This one does, and by the most indirect route: it is not the paper of the one who left, it is that of the one who stayed.
  • AND IT CONFIRMS FAMILY MEMORY TO THE LETTER. Gonzalo remembered her as «Trasilico de Galicanto, Lucca». The record says COMUNE DI TRASSILICO, province of Lucca. In 1923 Trassilico was a municipality in its own right; it was merged with GALLICANO shortly afterwards. The family kept both names, that of the village and that of the municipality that swallowed it, for a hundred years and at ten thousand kilometres' distance.
  • THE HOUSE: TRASSILICO, VIA DEL CENTRO, NUMBER 17. It is the only specific address known for this branch, and the place from which Tebaldo left for Argentina. Trassilico is a mountain village of the Garfagnana, in the Serchio valley, beneath the Apuan Alps, and it fits entire within two hundred and fifty metres. BEWARE: VIA DEL CENTRO NO LONGER EXISTS. Trassilico today has eleven named streets and none is called that — the names changed, in all probability when the municipality merged with Gallicano around 1928, five years after this record. The firmest candidate is the VIA ANTONIO VALLISNERI, which is the village's central street and bears the name of its illustrious son. All of that is explained in Tebaldo's file, with the measurements. On the map:ver en el mapa
  • «MOGLIE DI CELERI CALLISTO», CORRECTED IN THE MARGIN TO «ANZI VEDOVA». The clerk first wrote «wife» and then somebody emended it to «widow». It is the proof that Callisto was already dead in September 1923, even though nobody knows when.
  • HER PARENTS, CONFIRMED AND BOTH DEAD: PIETRO VANNI and LIVIA RIGHINI, both of Trassilico. The FamilySearch tree gave her as the daughter of Pietro Vanni and «Lucia» Righini; the record says LIVIA. The record is preferred.
  • NOT A SINGLE SIBLING OF HERS IS STILL KNOWN. A «Vanni Maria Francesca di Pietro» from the 1863 index was tried, in case that Pietro were a son of the doctor; on reading the record, she turned out to be a nailmaker's daughter from Le Fabbriche, the child of a Francesco. Ruled out.
  • SHE DIED AT THE AGE OF EIGHTY-EIGHT IN THE HOUSE IN WHICH SHE WAS BORN, in a village she probably never left. Her son had by then been more than forty years in Argentina, and her Argentine granddaughter — whom she never met — was called Enriqueta after her.
Sibling

Luisa Vanni

In the records: «ALOISA» in the body of the record; she signs LUISA

Translation

The year 1863, the 26th day of April. Before me, the undersigned BERTOLI BARSOTTI RANIERI, Mayor and Registrar of the Civil Status of the Comune of Trassilico, Province of Massa, Circondario of the Garfagnana, there appeared:

VINCENTI DOCTOR MATTEO, aged thirty-four, born and resident at COREGLIA ANTELMINELLI, by profession a PHYSICIAN, son of the late VINCENTI ANTONIO, aged eighty-three, a man of property, and of SERAFINI ELISABETTA, aged sixty, likewise of property, both born and resident in the said Coreglia;

and VANNI ALOISA, aged thirty-one, born and resident at Trassilico, A WOMAN OF PROPERTY, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE DOCTOR PIETRO VANNI — by profession a LAWYER, BORN AT LUCCA and resident at Trassilico — AND OF RIGHINI LIVIA, aged fifty, without profession, BORN AT CASTELNUOVO and resident at Trassilico;

who, in the presence of CELERI CALLISTO, son of the late Desiderio, aged thirty-two, TOWN CLERK, and of BONETTI PIETRO, son of the late Antonio, aged thirty-three, farmer, both born and resident at Trassilico, witnesses, declared to me that it was their intention to be joined in marriage.

What you need to know about this record

  • LUISA VANNI WAS ENRICHETTA'S ELDER SISTER. The record calls her «Aloisa» in the body, but she signs «LUISA VANNI» in her own hand, and it declares her the daughter of the late doctor Pietro Vanni and of Livia Righini: exactly the same parents.
  • AND THAT EXPLAINS THE ODDEST FACT IN THE TREE. Tebaldo was born on 25 July 1862 «in the house of Luisa Vanni, Alla Maestà». It was not a neighbour's house: it was his AUNT'S house. Enrichetta went to give birth at her sister's. Ten months later, Luisa married.
  • THE FATHER WAS NOT A PHYSICIAN, HE WAS A LAWYER, AND HE WAS NOT FROM TRASSILICO. «Di professione LEGALE, nato in LUCCA». He had been taken for the village doctor because Tebaldo's record calls him «dottor Pietro» — but in Italy «dottore» is any graduate. He was a man of law, and he came from the city.
  • LIVIA RIGHINI WAS ALIVE, WAS FIFTY YEARS OLD AND SIGNED. And the record confirms in black and white that SHE WAS BORN IN CASTELNUOVO, which was the doubtful reading of the 1860 record. Her signature and her daughter's are the first two women's signatures in this branch.
  • A DIFFICULTY THAT IS NOT COVERED UP: of the late Pietro Vanni the record says «aged thirty-two years». This register does give the age of dead parents — it gives the groom's father eighty-three — but thirty-two is incompatible with having one daughter in 1832 and another in 1835, unless he died about 1835 with Luisa aged three and Enrichetta newly born, leaving Livia a widow at twenty-two. It is possible, but extraordinary. It may also be a slip of the clerk's. It is not resolved by this document.
  • CALLISTO CELERI, aged thirty-two and town clerk, was a witness at his sister-in-law's wedding. It is the seventh known specimen of his signature.

No record obtained yet

He exists by four words: «del caporal Pietro». But with him the maternal branch reaches the eighteenth century. A CORPORAL, A LAWYER, A LAWYER AND A MISTRESS OF THE HOUSE. The sequence of three generations that comes out of this record says a great deal about a family on the way up: the father is a soldier of the ranks, the son becomes a lawyer, the grandson likewise, and the grandson marries the daughter of another property owner and goes up to a mountain village to practise. WHERE TO LOOK FOR HIM. His grandson Pietro WAS BORN IN LUCCA, so it is most probable that this family was from the city. Everything of his would be in the Archivio di Stato di Lucca or in the Archivio Storico Diocesano of Lucca — not in Massa.

Of her we know the given name, that she was alive and widowed in 1832, and that she was godmother to her granddaughter. Nothing more — no surname, no town, no dates. HER SURNAME WOULD BE in the marriage record of her son Pietro with Livia Righini, c. 1830, or in the baptismal record of Pietro himself, c. 1808 and probably in LUCCA. Neither of the two has been located.

He exists by five words: «del fu Bernardo Righini di Castelnuovo». AND WITH HIM THE SEARCH MOVES TOWN. Livia was from CASTELNUOVO DI GARFAGNANA, the capital of the valley, not from Trassilico. Her birth c. 1813, her wedding with the doctor Pietro Vanni c. 1830 and everything concerning Bernardo would be in the registers of Castelnuovo — another comune, another holding. THERE IS BESIDES ANOTHER LOOSE RIGHINI: Luisa's godfather was GIOVANNI DI CARLO RIGHINI. Son of a Carlo, and almost certainly a relative of Livia — a brother of hers or a cousin.

He exists by four words: «figlio del fu Giuseppe Vanni». But those four words go up a whole generation and carry the maternal branch back to the eighteenth century. WHERE TO LOOK FOR HIM. His son Pietro WAS BORN IN LUCCA —so says Luisa's marriage record, of 1863—, so it is most probable that this family was not from Trassilico but from the city, and that Pietro went up to the mountains already qualified as a lawyer. If so, Pietro's baptism, c. 1808, and everything concerning Giuseppe would be in LUCCA: in the Archivio di Stato di Lucca or in the Archivio Storico Diocesano of the city, not in Massa. THAT OPENS A WHOLE NEW FRONT. Until now this branch was shut up in a village of a hundred inhabitants; with Giuseppe Vanni it moves to a city with archives that begin in the Middle Ages.

AND BEWARE OF THE OTHER GIUSEPPE VANNI, WHO IS NOT HIS FATHER
his father was called GIUSEPPE VANNI, and at LE FABBRICHE there is ANOTHER GIUSEPPE VANNI, a nailmaker, with children documented in 1858 and 1859. They are not the same man: ours is the father of a graduate born at Lucca; the other, an artisan from a forge hamlet
A PIETRO VANNI MARRYING IN 1864 IS NOT HIM
the marriage index of 1864 carries a VANNI PIETRO marrying Barsotti Giovanna (record 10). For a day it stood as an open question, and it was not one: he died on 22 March 1841, so that groom is necessarily another man — in all probability the «Pietro Vanni fu Francesco, chiodajuolo di Le Fabbriche» already ruled out in 1863
NOT TO BE CONFUSED WITH THE OTHER VANNI
in the same comune there is a whole VANNI family, numerous and unrelated: the NAILMAKERS OF LE FABBRICHE

Parish death certificate

Trassilico — parish of the Holy Apostles Peter and Paul · book of the dead «Lettera D», page 32, entry 182 · 22 March 1841; sepultado the 23

Archive
Archivio di Stato di MASSA (archive 240)
Where it turned up
not in a register of deaths, but INSIDE THE MARRIAGE DOSSIER OF HIS DAUGHTER LUISA: Trassilico, Matrimoni allegati 1863, Registro 64.11, page 103 of 119
Shelfmark
ark:/12657/an_ua35853779 — hash Lok9x2p

Translation

I, the undersigned Parish Priest, certify that from the BOOK OF THE DEAD of this my Church erected under the title of the HOLY APOSTLES PETER AND PAUL, marked with the LETTER D, at page 32, number 182, the following appears:

ON 23 MARCH 1841. DOCTOR PIETRO, SON OF THE LATE GIUSEPPE VANNI, fortified with the sacraments of confession, communion and the commendation of the soul, DIED YESTERDAY, and today, after solemn obsequies, has been buried. I so certify, DOMENICO GRANDI, Rector.

And in witness that I have extracted it from an authentic book. Trassilico, 25 April 1863.

On 26 April 1863. Seen, the signature and standing of signor Dom. Grandi are certified true. The Mayor, R. Bertoli Barsotti.

What you need to know about this record

  • HE DIED ON 22 MARCH 1841. Until today it was known only that he was dead «before 1859». Now there is a day.
  • AND HIS FATHER APPEARS: GIUSEPPE VANNI, already deceased in 1841. He is the earliest documented ancestor of the whole maternal branch.
  • A DIFFICULTY THAT HAD BEEN LEFT OPEN IS RESOLVED. The marriage record of his daughter Luisa, of 1863, gave him «aged thirty-two years», a figure that seemed impossible with daughters born in 1832 and 1835. It is not: if he died in March 1841 aged thirty-two, he was born about 1808-1809, had Luisa at twenty-three and Enrichetta at twenty-six. The clerk was not mistaken; the fact was missing.
  • AND SO LIVIA RIGHINI WAS WIDOWED AT TWENTY-EIGHT, with Luisa aged nine and Enrichetta six. She did not remarry: in 1863, at fifty, she is still signing «Righini Livia». Twenty-two years of widowhood, and many more still to come.
  • WHERE IT TURNED UP, WHICH IS THE LESSON. Not in a register of deaths — he died eleven years before the civil registration of Trassilico existed — but INSIDE THE MARRIAGE DOSSIER OF HIS DAUGHTER, in 1863. The allegati transcribe parish records verbatim, with book, page and number. By that same route one must look for DESIDERIO CELERI and ANGELA MENGHI.
  • THE EXACT REFERENCE OF THE ORIGINAL BOOK: parish of SS. Pietro e Paolo of Trassilico, book of the dead «Lettera D», page 32, entry 182. With that the whole book can be requested from the Archivio Diocesano di Massa.
  • IT IS SIGNED BY DON DOMENICO GRANDI, the same priest who married Callisto and Enrichetta in 1859, baptised Tebaldo in 1862 and married Desiderio Celeri in 1863. In 1841 he was already rector: twenty-two years later he copied out of his own book a record he had himself written as a young man.

ENRICHETTA VANNI (c. 1835 – 1923)

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED BY RECORD: the death record of her daughter, Trassilico 1923, which gives her as mother, dead and a resident of the same village. RIGHINI is a Tuscan and Emilian surname, and it fits the Garfagnana. Together with VANNI, they are the two surnames that even before this document appeared pointed to Tuscany when there was as yet nothing to prove it. AND ON 27 AUGUST 2026 SHE CAME OUT OF THE VILLAGE. The birth record of her grandson Giuseppe —Trassilico, 18 November 1860, record 76— says that of Enrichetta's parents «the first was born and is domiciled in Trassilico, and THE SECOND WAS BORN IN CASTELNUOVO and is domiciled in Trassilico». It is the first time this branch sets foot in another town. Castelnuovo di Garfagnana is the capital of the valley, some ten kilometres away: the doctor's wife came from the big town. THE READING IS VERY PROBABLE, NOT CERTAIN. The word carries an «st» ligature which at first sight looks like a double s. The stroke was compared with the «Trassilico» on the same line, where the double s is short and tight, whereas here there are two tall ascenders — which points to CA-ST-E-L-NUOVO. It would be as well to reconfirm it in another record. IF IT IS CONFIRMED, THE SEARCH MOVES: her birth, c. 1810, and her wedding with the doctor Pietro Vanni, c. 1830, would have to be looked for in CASTELNUOVO DI GARFAGNANA and not in Trassilico. And she was ALIVE in November 1860, so her death falls between that date and 1923.

He has no record of his own as yet, but he is proven by two documents that cross: the 1887 census puts him as son of Benedetto and Madalena, and the marriage record of 1900 puts him as witness for his sister. IT WAS HE WHO CLINCHED THE IDENTIFICATION OF THIS WHOLE BRANCH. For days there were doubts as to whether the Alasia of the tree were really Margarita's parents. What settled it was not a date: it was seeing that the two witnesses who sign the wedding of 1900 —«Don Domingo Alasia and Doña María de Alasia»— had been sitting thirteen years earlier at the table of the same household, in the census, as son and daughter-in-law.

MARIA MADDALENA BERTINO (1839-1899)
Parents
BARTOLOMEO ALLASIA and LUCIA GAIDO, from the FamilySearch tree
Daughter
MARGARITA ALASIA, born c. 1882 at Colonia Emilia, Santa Fe
Surname
ALLASIA in Piedmont, ALASIA in Argentina — an l was lost along the way

No record obtained yet

CONFIRMED ON 27 AUGUST 2026, AND BY A DOCUMENT, NOT BY A RESEMBLANCE. The provincial census of Santa Fe of June 1887 has, in COLONIA EMILIA, volume 12, booklet 12, this whole household: OLAZIO BENEDETTO, aged SIXTY-FIVE, Italian, head of the family; BERTINA MADALENA, aged FORTY-SEVEN, his wife, WHO DECLARES FOURTEEN CHILDREN; and of these ALAZIO DOMINGO, aged twenty-four and married, with his wife MOLINA MARÍA beside him, plus Nicolás, Juan, Francisco, María, MARGARITA AGED FIVE BORN IN SANTA FE, and Madalenia aged one. AND ON 29 AUGUST, ON REREADING THAT ENTRY WITH THE FIELDS THAT THE CENSUS DOES PUBLISH AND THAT WERE BEING LOST, MORE CAME OUT. He is recorded as PATRÓN —a colonist with land, not a day labourer— and neither he nor his wife could read or write; she declares the fourteen children and thirty-one years married, which places the wedding in 1856, within a year of the 13 April 1857 that the record gives. And his son Domingo declares two years married: he married María Molina in 1885, not «around 1885-86». AND THERE ARE TWO OTHER ALASIA HOUSEHOLDS IN THE SAME COLONY, AND NOW SOMETHING CAN BE SAID ABOUT THEM. One is that of ALACIO DOMINGO, aged sixty-four —one year less than him—, also a patrón, married to a Luigia who declares eight children and thirty-two years married, that is, a wedding in 1855, a year before his own. Two men of the same age, married in consecutive years, both patrones, in a village of a hundred households: it looks like a textbook case of their being brothers. The other is that of ALAZIO JUAN, aged forty-five, a patrón and illiterate like him, married in 1872 to Lucia Silvestre, with seven children all born already in Santa Fe — a household that until today was not noted down anywhere. BUT THE TEST OF THE NAMES SAYS THAT THEY ARE NOT HIS BROTHERS. Custom dictates that the first male child bear the name of the paternal grandfather, and he follows it to the letter: his father was called BARTOLOMEO and his eldest son is called BARTOLOMEO. If Domingo and Juan were brothers of his, their first sons should be called the same — and the eldest of Alacio Domingo is TOMASO and that of Alazio Juan is ANGELO. Most probably they were cousins: three Alasia families from the same Piedmontese valley arrived in the same colony, which is how those chains worked. It is not proof —custom binds only for the first male, and a child who died young breaks the series— but for the first time the question ceases to be at «cannot be decided». THE MISSING DAUGHTERS: ONE MORE ROUTE THAT CLOSES (29 August 2026). Magdalena declared FOURTEEN children and only eight appear alive in the census. Married daughters are lost because the census roll enters them under the husband's surname and does not give the maiden name. The blog of Hugo Alloa, built on the records of the parish of Emilia, names nine surnames that married into the Allasia — BARONI, BUSATTO, CHARTIER, DACARRO, GIUPPONI, PALMIER, RONZONI, ROSSINI and ZANUTICH — and their households were searched for an Italian woman of the right age. There are 92 people of those surnames in the colony, 17 married women, and NONE passes: two are French, six were born before 1856 (that is, before Benedetto's wedding, and cannot be daughters of his), and eight break the Italian custom of names, because their second son ought to be called Benito and none of them is. In fact THERE IS NOT A SINGLE GRANDSON CALLED BENITO in the 1,385 entries of the colony: the five Benito or Benedetto that come up are Benedetto himself (indexed OLAZIO), a man of 32, a French boy, a newborn and a girl of eight whom the census roll entered as male. ONE remains impossible to judge: BARONI TERESA, 25 years old, married in 1884, the only one of the seventeen who declares «Italy, TORINO», which is where the Alasia are from — but she has only one child, and without a second son custom says nothing. Neither ruled out nor confirmed. And three of the nine surnames —DACARRO, GIUPPONI and ZANUTICH— DO NOT EXIST in the colony in 1887, so those weddings are later than the census. THIS DOES NOT PROVE that no daughter married a Baroni or a Rossini: it proves that the census cannot identify her. That is decided by the records of the parish of Emilia, already requested by letter. AND LATER THE SAME DAY THE WHOLE CENSUS ROLL OF THE COLONY WAS DOWNLOADED —1,368 people, with the twelve fields— and the test of the names ceased to be a sounding. Filtering by who could be a daughter of his —Italian, born after 1858, married after 1873— FIFTY-NINE women come out, not the eight from the nine surnames that had been looked at. And searching the 1,368 entries for every Benito, Benedetto, Magdalena and Bartolomeo, ten people appear, of whom none is a grandchild of his: he and his wife, his youngest daughter Madalenia, the girl of the Bertino household, two infants from a criollo and a Swiss household, an adult man and two Maddalena born already in Italy. There is not a single grandson called Benito in the whole of Colonia Emilia. The negative is exhaustive, not a sample — although it is conclusive only for the fourteen women married before 1880 and with three children or more; for those married later the second son had not yet been born in 1887, and for those the proof of the 1895 census remains. And that says something about the four children of his that are missing: they are not married daughters living in the colony. Added to the fact that the three eldest do not appear anywhere in the province, the most economical explanation —and it is an inference, not a fact— is that a good part of the fourteen died in infancy. That a woman married in 1857 should declare fourteen births and only ten identifiable children was the norm, not the exception. AND THERE IS A SECOND DOOR, WHICH OPENED ON LISTING THE THIRTEEN «NEPHEWS AND NIECES» OF THE COLONY. In the household of the Chartier there lives a girl of three registered as «ALASIA LUISA, niece», born in Santa Fe. That the enumerator gave her a surname DIFFERENT from that of the head of household is a deliberate act: Luisa is not a Chartier, she is an Alasia. And if she is a niece of that house and bears that surname, she is so on INÉS's side, not on that of the Frenchman Eduardo — that is, Inés was an Allasia by birth, just as the parish indexes said. And with the whole census roll before one, something can be seen that could not be seen before: Luisa's father is not in the colony. Having gone over the three Alasia households and all their children, there is no Luisa anywhere. Her father —an Alasia male with a daughter born c. 1884— was not living there in June 1887; and that the girl is being brought up by her aunt suggests that he had died. That opens another explanation for his eldest children, and it must be stated as a hypothesis: if Inés is a daughter of his, Luisa's father would be another son of his — and the candidates are Bartolomeo, Salvatore Fortunato Antonio and Domenico Carlo, who in 1884 would have been between twenty-one and twenty-four. Instead of having died as children in Italy, at least one would have reached Argentina, married, had a daughter c. 1884 and died young. Both explanations still stand; this one holds only if Inés turns out to be a daughter of his, and that is settled by the Chartier × Allasia marriage record already requested. AND THERE IS A FOURTH ALASIA IN THE COLONY THAT NOBODY HAD COUNTED, AND HE SAYS WHERE THEY WERE FROM. Listing the twenty-eight Alasia of the census roll under the seven spellings there appears, alone and on his own line, «ALASIO ANTONIO, 22 years old, single, PATRÓN, born in ITALY, CUNEO». He belongs to none of the three known households and no relationship to anyone is recorded. He is the only Alasia in the whole colony who declares the Italian province. Until today the Piedmontese origin of this family rested on the FamilySearch tree and on the 1857 record from Savigliano; now there is an Argentine document, an independent one, that ties an Alasia of that same colony to the province of Cuneo — where Savigliano has been since 1859. And it fits with the other piece of provincial information we had, Teresa Baroni's «Italy, TORINO»: Savigliano belonged to Turin until 1859 and to Cuneo afterwards, so the two do not contradict each other but reinforce each other. And he is a candidate to be one of the four children of his still unlocated: born c. 1865 in Italy, he would have crossed as a child, and between Domenico Carlo (1863) and Nicolás (1872) there is a gap of nine years in which he fits. Besides, he is a patrón, like him: he has land of his own at twenty-two, which is what a colonist does when he sets up a son. It is not proven — he does not live in his household and no relationship is recorded, and he could be a nephew or a cousin. AND THE NATIONAL CENSUS OF 1895 CONFIRMS IT AGAIN, SEPARATELY. In the same Colonia Emilia, eight years later: «BERTINO MAGDALENA, 56 years old, WIDOW, Italian, farm labourer, can read and write, OWNS REAL PROPERTY, FOURTEEN CHILDREN», and with her Domingo Juan aged 19, Francisco aged 17, María aged 15 and MARGARITA AGED 13. The four siblings have aged EXACTLY EIGHT YEARS with respect to the 1887 census —11→19, 9→17, 7→15, 5→13—, which is precisely the interval between the two census rolls. It is not a resemblance of names: it is the same family documented twice. And that second census adds three things. BENEDETTO HAD DIED: she is recorded as a WIDOW in 1895 and he was alive and head of the family in 1887, so he died between those two dates — the tree said 1892 and that falls within. She is 56, that is, BORN c. 1839, the exact date in the tree. And she was no day labourer: SHE HAD LAND OF HER OWN AND COULD READ, being a woman, a widow and an immigrant, in 1895. SIX THINGS FIT AT ONCE. The father is called BENEDETTO, not a hispanicised «Benito». He is sixty-five years old, that is, he was born c. 1822 — the exact date in the tree. The mother was born c. 1840; the tree said 1839. There is a Margarita of five born in Santa Fe, and the Vera record of 1900 gives her nineteen and makes her «a native of Col. Emilia». And there they are, in the same household, THE TWO WITNESSES OF HER WEDDING: «Don DOMINGO ALASIA and Doña MARÍA DE ALASIA» of the record are DOMINGO, her eldest brother, and MARÍA MOLINA, Domingo's wife. Thirteen years later they signed their little sister's wedding. AND THE ONLY SERIOUS OBJECTION LEFT FALLS AWAY. It used to be said that the chronology was tight: a mother of forty-two at Margarita's birth, twenty-four years after marrying. It was not tight: SHE HAD FOURTEEN CHILDREN, and Margarita was the twelfth. THEY WERE NOT ALONE IN THE COLONY. The same census roll of 1887 has TWO OTHER HOUSEHOLDS of the surname in Colonia Emilia: that of ALACIO DOMINGO, aged SIXTY-FOUR, with his wife Luigia and two already married sons living under his roof —Tomaso and Bernardo, with their wives and their children—, and that of ALAZIO JUAN, aged forty-five, with seven children. Domingo is sixty-four and Benedetto sixty-five: two men of the same surname and the same age in a colony of thirteen hundred souls. Most probably they were BROTHERS, and the emigration was a chain migration. IT IS NOT PROVEN —the census gives no filiations— and it would be checked with the Argentine deaths of the two of them or with the books of the parish of Natividad de la Virgen Niña of Emilia. IT WAS FOUND BECAUSE THE SEARCH BY SURNAME WAS ABANDONED. Searching for «Alasia» in Emilia gave a single person and had been blocking this for two days. The WHOLE CENSUS ROLL of the district had to be downloaded —1,383 people, 348 surnames— and compared by similarity: in the same colony FOUR SPELLINGS of the surname lived side by side, ALASIA, ALASIO, ALACIO and ALAZIO, and the family was written OLAZIO and ALAZIO. No search engine working by name would have produced it. AND ON THE AFTERNOON OF 27 AUGUST THE LAST LOOSE END WAS TIED. One difficulty remained: the «how many years married» box of the 1895 census read TWENTY-FIVE, which would give a wedding around 1870 and not in 1857. The complete list of children resolves it. The first, LUCIA, is born in 1859 —two years after the wedding— and after her come BARTOLOMEO in 1860, SALVATORE in 1861, DOMENICO CARLO in 1863, DOMINGO in 1864 and another LUCIA in 1866. With six children before 1870, a wedding in 1870 is impossible. The census figure is a THIRTY-FIVE badly written or badly read: 1857 plus 35 is 1892, the year in which she was widowed. She counted how long the marriage LASTED, not the time elapsed up to 1895. AND THERE IS AN ONOMASTIC DETAIL THAT SUPPORTS THE GRANDPARENTS: the first male child was called BARTOLOMEO, and Benedetto's father would be BARTOLOMEO ALLASIA. Piedmontese custom gave the first male the name of the paternal grandfather. It is not proof, but it is exactly what one would expect. The same goes for the two LUCIA, of 1859 and 1866, and the maternal grandmother LUCIA GAIDO. THE TEN CHILDREN KNOWN TO HIM, adding together the tree and the two censuses: Lucia (1859-1859), Bartolomeo (1860), Salvatore Fortunato (1861), Domenico Carlo (1863), Domingo (1864), Lucia Margarita (1866-1866), Nicolás (~1872), Juan (1875), Francisco (1878), María Magdalena (1879), MARGARITA (1882) and Madalenia (~1886). Twelve of the fourteen that she declared. WHAT FOLLOWS IS THE EARLIER REASONING, WHICH IS LEFT AS IT STOOD BECAUSE IT IS THE STORY OF HOW ONE GOT THERE: THE ONLY THING A RECORD SAYS is that Margarita Alasia was the «legitimate daughter of Don BENITO ALASIA and of Doña MAGDALENA BERTINI» (Vera, 25-10-1900). Nothing more. No dates, no town for them, no word as to whether they were alive. EVERYTHING ELSE —that he was born in 1822, that he died in 1892, that he married in Savigliano on 13 April 1857, and with it the four grandparents— comes from a FamilySearch tree, not from a document. To identify the «Benito Alasia» of the Argentine record with the «Benedetto Alasia» of Savigliano is an INFERENCE, and these are its two faces: IN FAVOUR. BOTH surnames and BOTH given names coincide at once (Benito = Benedetto, Magdalena = Maddalena): that is much stronger than a surname on its own. ALASIA/ALLASIA is almost exclusively Piedmontese —of 101 occurrences in Italy, 89 in Piedmont— and Colonia Emilia, in the department of San Justo, was an area of Piedmontese colonists. And the tree has already shown that it works from documents: it said that Enrichetta Vanni died in 1923 and the Trassilico record confirmed it. AGAINST. If the wife was born in 1839, at Margarita's birth c. 1881 she would have been FORTY-TWO and twenty-four years would have passed since the wedding: possible, but right at the limit. And BERTINO is not only Piedmontese: of 240 occurrences, 98 are Sicilian and 92 Piedmontese. Besides, the Piedmontese colonists of Santa Fe arrived en bloc from the same towns, so one and the same pair of surnames may be repeated within the same colony. VERDICT: probable, not proven. It stands as long as no other Benito Alasia married to a Magdalena Bertini turns up in the same area. A FIRST SWEEP OF THE 1887 CENSUS SEEMED TO WEAKEN IT, AND IT WAS A FALSE ALARM OF MINE. I looked for the family under the spellings ALASIA, ALLASIA, ALACIA, BERTINO and BERTINI and found nobody: no Benito of sixty-five, no Magdalena, no Margarita at all. I even noted it down as three worrying absences. THEY WERE ALL THERE. THE FAULT WAS MINE: SPELLINGS WERE MISSING. In Argentine census rolls the surname is written by ear, and this one has SEVEN different forms in the same census — Alasia, Alazio, Alassia, Alacio, Alasio, Allasia and Olazio. **Benedetto is indexed OLAZIO and his children ALAZIO**, which were precisely the two I had not tried. With them the whole household appeared in Colonia Emilia, with Margarita aged five. THE LESSON, and it holds for every search in census rolls: NEVER GIVE A NEGATIVE WITHOUT HAVING TRIED ALL THE PHONETIC VARIANTS. IF HE MARRIED IN SAVIGLIANO IN 1857 AND HIS DAUGHTER WAS BORN IN SANTA FE c. 1882, THE FAMILY EMIGRATED BETWEEN THOSE TWO DATES. Colonia Emilia and the colonies of the department of San Justo were settled above all by Piedmontese in the eighteen-seventies: it fits. WHERE EACH THING IS, verified on 27-8-2026: 1802-1813 · HERE I WAS WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS. I wrote that Savigliano was digitised on Antenati with Napoleonic Nati, Matrimoni and Morti, and that the same series was indexed on FamilySearch. **Both things are false.** They came from a search-engine summary, not from the catalogue. On reading the catalogue itself: the Archivio di Stato di CUNEO has 191 comuni and 50,891 registers, and **of Savigliano there is ONE SINGLE ONE**. And FamilySearch collection 1392991 covers the tribunals of Saluzzo and Mondovì, not Savigliano. AND ON 28 AUGUST THAT SINGLE REGISTER WAS READ, IN FULL. They are the MARRIAGE ANNEXES OF 1808 for the district of Savigliano: sixteen pages, four wedding dossiers, papers from Lagnasco, Cherasco, Cavallermaggiore, Saluzzo and Casale. **Not one Alasia nor one Bertino in any of them.** Firm negative: Antenati is closed for this branch. BUT IT DID YIELD SOMETHING USEFUL. The extracts of 1808 cite book and page of the parishes of Savigliano, and they name two that we did not know of: **SAN SALVATORE and SAN PIETRO**, besides the San Giovanni Battista that the tree gave. Savigliano had at least THREE parishes, so the letters now ask that all three be looked at. 1814-1865 · THE HOLE. In the Kingdom of Sardinia there was no civil registration: between the fall of Napoleon and unification, EVERYTHING is parish. And precisely the three dates that matter fall there: Benedetto's birth (1822), that of his wife (1839) and the wedding of the two (1857). It must be requested from the parish or from the Archivio Diocesano di Torino. 1866 ONWARDS · the civil registration of the Comune di Savigliano. But by then the family was already in Santa Fe. AND THERE IS A FOURTH ROUTE, ARGENTINE AND CHEAPER: the NATIONAL CENSUS OF 1895. Benedetto had died (1892), but his widow was still living —she died in 1899— and Margarita was thirteen. The 1895 census asks for the YEAR OF ARRIVAL in the country: a single sheet of Colonia Emilia would give the date of the emigration, the whole list of the children and which Italian province they declared themselves from.

that is what she declares herself in the national census of 1895, already a widow. Ten are known from the tree, and only eight appear alive in the census of 1887: six are missing
The three eldest never reached Argentina
BARTOLOMEO (1860), SALVATORE FORTUNATO ANTONIO (1861) and DOMENICO CARLO (1863) do NOT appear in the census of 1887 anywhere in the province, under any of the seven spellings of the surname. The likeliest thing is that they died as children at Savigliano, before the emigration — but that is a deduction, not a proven fact
Surname
BERTINO in Piedmont, «Bertini» in the Argentine record

No record obtained yet

Proven as Margarita's mother by the marriage record of 1900. If she died in 1899, her daughter married in recent mourning, aged nineteen and with no mother. She married aged eighteen. All of it still needs to be verified against records. 29 AUGUST 2026 — THE NET TIGHTENS AROUND THE MISSING DAUGHTERS. Of the fourteen children she herself declared, six are still without a name, and the married daughters are the hardest to find because they disappear beneath the husband's surname: the census gives no maiden surname. The only lead are the indexes of the parish of Emilia published by Hugo Alloa, which list nine surnames married to ALLASIA — Baroni, Busatto, Chartier, Dacarro, Giupponi, Palmier, Ronzoni, Rossini and Zanutich. All nine were searched for in the census of 1887. Four gave zero, but that zero was badly made: they had been searched with a single spelling, when her husband's own surname appears in seven ways in that same census roll. Repeated with thirty-two phonetic variants, two of the four zeros were false. The one that counts is ZANUTICH, indexed without the final «ch»: in the whole province there were three, and all three unmarried — ALBERTO aged twenty, JUAN aged eighteen and LUDOVICO aged sixteen, Italians, in San Justo, Colonia Angeloni, among Friulian neighbours, which is exactly where that surname comes from. So the Zanutich × Allasia wedding of the parish of Emilia is later than June 1887 and the groom is almost certainly one of those three. That turns a vague question into a concrete request to the Archdiocesan Archive, already added to the letter. If that Allasia turns out to be a daughter of hers, she is one of the six that are missing. It is not proven: what is proven is that there was no other Zanutich in the province and that these three were unmarried. The other three are closed: BUSATTO and GIUPPONI do not exist in the province, not even with eight variants each, so those weddings are also later than 1887 or were celebrated elsewhere; and the only DACARO is a woman of thirty, alone in a boarding house in the town centre of Santa Fe, not in the colony, and she is ruled out. THE SAME DAY, LATER — A NEW METHOD WAS TRIED: THE GRANDCHILDREN'S NAMES. In these families the order of names was not free: the first male child bore the name of the paternal grandfather and the second that of the maternal grandfather; the first daughter that of the paternal grandmother and the second that of the maternal grandmother. That means that a daughter of hers married to anyone at all ought to have among her children a BENITO — for her father — and a MAGDALENA — for herself. And those names the census does give, even though it does not give the maiden surname. It is the only way of looking inside a family without knowing what surname the mother was born with. The thirty-one children of the eight households of those surnames in Colonia Emilia were read. Not a single Benito, Benedetto or Magdalena. The negative weakens the hypothesis, but does not kill it: the custom is strong and is not a law, in Piedmont it is followed less than in the south, and only five of the eight households have two children or more, which is the minimum for the test to mean anything. WHAT IS FIRM IS THE ARITHMETIC, AND IT ELIMINATES THREE. She married on 13 April 1857 and her eldest known child is of 1860, so no daughter of hers could have been born before 1858. That rules out RONZONI PAULA (35 years old, that is, born c. 1852, five years before the wedding), BARONI JUANA, Enrique's (32, c. 1855) and BARONI ANTONIA (30, c. 1857 — and besides, her four children were born in Italy, that is, she married there c. 1877, when this family had been in Argentina for years). Five survive: Inés Chartier, Teresa, Juana, María and Clementina Baroni. AND OUT OF THAT COMES A TEST THAT CAN BE MADE AND THAT CAN FAIL, which is exactly what was missing. The entire household of the best candidate was reconstructed and it is three people: Juan Baroni aged thirty-two, Teresa aged twenty-five and a single child, JOSÉ, aged one. By the rule of names that José bears the name of Juan's father — and the second male child ought to bear that of Teresa's father. If in the census of 1895 that couple has a second boy called BENITO, Teresa Baroni is a daughter of hers and one of the fourteen is recovered; if he is called something else, she is ruled out. It is a concrete request for the FamilySearch session, and until now this line produced only candidates that could neither be confirmed nor discarded. AND ON RECOVERING THE FIELDS THAT THE CENSUS DOES PUBLISH, THE LIST ORDERS ITSELF PROPERLY. The YEARS MARRIED give the year of the wedding directly, which is far more decisive than the age. With that, of the eight candidates three are eliminated beyond argument: RONZONI PAULA married in 1872, when Benedetto's eldest daughter was twelve; BARONI JUANA, Enrique's, was born c. 1855, two years before Benedetto's wedding; and BARONI ANTONIA married in 1877 and had her four children in Italy, when this family had been five years in Argentina. And a pattern appears that had not been visible: three Baroni — Juan, Jesús and Antonio, who by their ages and by living in consecutive libretos (census booklets) look like brothers — all three married in 1884, and all three have a single child in 1887. Three brothers marrying in the same season is common; with three sisters, even more so. And she still has four of the fourteen to locate: four daughters born between 1864 and 1872 would be between fifteen and twenty-three years old in 1887 and would already be married and out of the house — which is exactly the band into which Teresa, Juana, María and Clementina fall. It is a striking coincidence and nothing more: the census gives no maiden surnames and Baroni is a common surname. AND APPLYING THE SAME TEST TO THE WHOLE COLONY, INSTEAD OF TO NINE SURNAMES, SOMETHING CAME OUT THAT NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR: HER OWN FAMILY. The census search engine also finds given names, not only surnames, so BENITO and MAGDALENA were searched across the whole province and Colonia Emilia was filtered out. The method validated itself: among the results appear «Olazio Benedetto, 65» and «Bertina Madalena, 47», one beside the other — she and her husband in person. And one of the hits was a one-year-old girl called MAGDALENA BERTHA. On reconstructing her surroundings there appeared, all together and in the same volume, a whole household of nine people with her surname: Juan Bertha aged 45, Dominga Bertina aged 28, CATARINA BERTINA aged 57, Gabriel Bertina aged 26, Valentín Bertha aged 55, and four children — María aged 5, Domingo aged 4, Caterina aged 3 and Magdalena aged 1. The indexer writes the same family «Bertina» and «Bertha» on consecutive lines, which is exactly why nobody had seen them: her surname had never been swept with variants. With the ten done, in Colonia Emilia there are exactly TEN: those nine and her. (I correct here a figure I gave wrongly the same day: I wrote that in the whole province there were 216 Bertino. There are 105. The census search engine searches within the full name, and BERTA and BERTHA are extremely common given names among the Swiss and Germans who settled Las Colonias: of the 216, one hundred and eleven are girls called Berta, not people bearing that surname. The real surnames are distributed across Las Colonias 44, Iriondo 23 and La Capital 22.) Until today her branch was a total void in Argentina — of her we had only the name, the wedding of 1857 and parents with no record. And there are three things that fit: Magdalena's mother was called CATTERINA and in that household there is a Catarina aged 57; Juan aged 45 is of her own year-group and a candidate for brother; and the one-year-old girl is called MAGDALENA, which by the custom of names comes from a grandmother or an aunt — and she lived a few blocks away. It is not proven: nowhere does the census say that these people are family of hers, and the colonies were settled by chains of countrymen from the same valley, so two unrelated Bertino families in the same village is perfectly possible. It would be closed by the marriage of Juan Bertino to Dominga, c. 1880 in the parish of Emilia, which would name his parents; or by the baptism of the girl Magdalena, c. 1886, where it is very probable that the godparents were she herself or Benedetto. Both things have been added to the letter to the Archdiocesan Archive. AND THE SAME DAY, LATER, THE MOST CONCRETE COINCIDENCE OF ALL APPEARED. The census entries publish seven fields that until today were being lost through a fault in my own script — relationship to the head, marital status, CHILDREN BORNE, YEARS MARRIED, dependency, and whether they could read and write. Recovered, they say this: MAGDALENA BERTINO... 47 years old, 31 years married, 14 children CATARINA BERTINO.... 57 years old, 31 years married, 3 children Two women of the same surname, in the same village of a hundred houses, declaring exactly the same year of marriage: 1856. Marrying off two sisters in the same season was the commonest thing in the world. And the four children of that household are called María, Domingo, CATERINA and MAGDALENA — which are precisely her own name and that of her mother, Catterina Bonavia. That is the fourth coincidence, and still nothing is proven: the census does not say that the two households are related, nor does it give maiden surnames. And looking for her outside Emilia has yielded nothing. In LAS COLONIAS, the neighbouring department, there are forty-four people with her surname, and not one can be proposed as a sister or brother of hers. The only one of her own year-group is BERTINI DOMINGO, aged forty-six, an employer, with a daughter Caterina and another Magdalena — but he reached Argentina c. 1880-1884, eight or ten years after this family, and he can read and write, which she could not. A weak candidate. The household of Colonia Emilia remains, by far, the best lead: it is the only one in the whole province where a Bertino woman declares exactly the same thirty-one years married. AND IN IRIONDO, THE LAST DEPARTMENT LEFT, THERE IS A SECOND HOUSEHOLD. COSTANZO BERTINO, aged sixty, an employer, married since 1847 to a Margarita of fifty-five who declares six children; four sons and another, Marco, with his own house next door. He was born c. 1827, thirteen years before her, and emigrated in the same wave. By age and by condition he is the most plausible candidate for an elder brother that has appeared in the whole province, and his surname is written exactly BERTINO, which is the correct Piedmontese spelling. But there is a criterion that had not occurred to me to use and that tips the balance: literacy. She was illiterate, her husband too, and of her ten known children only two could read. In the Colonia Emilia household not one of the nine can read; in the Iriondo one the father and the four sons read and write. The level of schooling is inherited within a family and within a village. Added to the rest — the same colony, the same thirty-one years married, and children called Caterina and Magdalena —, the Colonia Emilia household remains clearly the best candidate; Iriondo is not ruled out. (And a refinement of the figure: of the 105 real surnames of the province, forty-six are BERTON — which is not a spelling of Bertino but a Venetian and Piedmontese surname in its own right. Removing it, the set that really matters is some thirty-eight people in the whole province, and all three departments where they are have now been looked at.) A detail that orders the request: if Catarina married in 1856 it cannot have been in Emilia — the colony did not yet exist — but in Piedmont. Her marriage record would name her parents, and if they are Giovanni Domenico Bertino and Catterina Bonavia, she is a sister of hers. And in passing the census confirms her own case by a second route: she declares the FOURTEEN CHILDREN already in June 1887, eight years earlier than in the national census of 1895 and while still married, not widowed. And her 31 years married point to 1856, within a year of the 13 April 1857 that the record gives — normal in a figure given from memory.

No record. If the date of 1797 is good, he is the oldest ancestor on the whole Rivas side of the tree — almost a century further back than there was two days ago, when this entire branch was a void. Savigliano preserves parish registers from the sixteenth century. There is much to be gained here. RECTIFICATION, OF THAT SAME 27-8-2026. I wrote in the morning that Savigliano was digitised on Antenati with Nati, Matrimoni and Morti of the Napoleonic period. IT WAS FALSE: I had taken it from a search engine's summary, not from the catalogue. Having read the real catalogue of the Archivio di Stato di Cuneo — 191 municipalities and 50,891 registers —, SAVIGLIANO HAS A SINGLE REGISTER, in the same batch as Racconigi, Ruffia or Villafranca Piemonte. And MONASTEROLO DI SAVIGLIANO IS NOT THERE: the only «Monasterolo» in the catalogue is Monasterolo Casotto, which is a different village and in the Mondovì area. SO THE ANTENATI ROUTE FOR THIS BRANCH IS PRACTICALLY DEAD. Three remain: the FamilySearch index (collection 1392991, which requires a session and could not be tested), the PARISH BOOKS of Savigliano — one has to write —, and above all the ARGENTINE SIDE, which is where the documents that can actually be consulted are. WHAT REMAINS TRUE OF THE REASONING: Bartolomeo was born c. 1797 and Lucia c. 1800: both fall just below the start of the fonds. But HIS SIBLINGS born between 1802 and 1813 are indeed there, and a single record of a sibling names Bartolomeo's father and mother with age and trade. And the DEATHS of 1802-1813 would already reach his grandparents, people born c. 1740. MOREOVER IT IS INDEXED BY NAME, free, on FamilySearch: collection 1392991, «Italy, Cuneo, Civil Registration (State Archive), 1795-1915», with index and images. ALLASIA and GAIDO can be searched in Savigliano without turning a single page.

Marriage

Marriage of Giacomo and Carmina

The record that was hunted for months at Marano and was at Rende all along. Besides marrying them, it is the one that proves whose son Francesco Giacomo was.

Date
19 October 1837, 9 p.m. — the church wedding the next day
Place
town hall of Rende, district of Cosenza
Registrar

Francesco Giacomo Palermo, 28 years old, PECORAJO, born at Marano Marchesato — son of Giuseppe Palermo, pecorajo, and of Carmela Spizzirri, both alive
Her

Marriage

Marriage record

Rende · record 20 · 19 October 1837, 21 h

Archive
Archivio di Stato di Cosenza
Collection
Stato civile della Restaurazione > Rende > Registro 29 «Matrimoni» 1837
Register
ark an_ua35976844 · container hash: wbVEAb4 (28 images)
Page
21 of 28 — folio 20 of the book; one record to the image, in the right-hand half
Registrar
Mario Magdalone, sindaco of Rende
Handwritten year
«mille ottocento trentasette» — agrees with the catalogue
How it was located
first in the scanned index of the marriages of Rende for 1837, row 20; then the record itself, right at the first try with the straight line page = record + 1

What the manuscript says

…sono comparsi nella casa comunale FRANCESCO GIACOMO PALERMO di anni VENTOTTO, nato in MARANO MARCHESATO, professione PECORAJO, domiciliato ivi, figlio di GIUSEPPE, domiciliato ivi, di professione PECORAJO, e di CARMELA SPIZZIRRI, domiciliata ivi; e CARMINA VOLPENTESTA di anni VENTITRE, nata in MARANO MARCHESATO, domiciliata RENDE, figlia del FU VINCENZO, domiciliato ————, e di TERESA LUCHETTA, domiciliata ivi…

Translation

In the year eighteen hundred and thirty-seven, on the NINETEENTH day of the month of OCTOBER, at TWENTY-ONE hours, before Us, MARIO MAGDALONE, mayor and registrar of the Civil Status of the comune of RENDE, district of Cosenza, province of Calabria Citra, there appeared at the town hall:

FRANCESCO GIACOMO PALERMO, AGED TWENTY-EIGHT, born at MARANO MARCHESATO, by trade a PECORAJO (shepherd), resident there, SON OF GIUSEPPE, resident there, by trade a PECORAJO, AND OF CARMELA SPIZZIRRI, resident there;

and CARMINA VOLPENTESTA, AGED TWENTY-THREE, born at MARANO MARCHESATO, resident at RENDE, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE VINCENZO and OF TERESA LUCHETTA, resident there.

Notice of the promise was posted on the door of the town hall of Rende on Sunday the first of October, AND AT MARANO MARCHESATO on the same day — in both villages, because he was from Marano and she was already living at Rende.

Documents presented: 1st the husband's birth record; 2nd the wife's; 3rd the certificate that the husband is unmarried and under no military obligation; 4th that the wife is unmarried; 5th THE DEATH CERTIFICATE OF THE WIFE'S FATHER; 6th THE DEATH CERTIFICATE OF HER PATERNAL GRANDFATHER; 7th the death certificate of her mother; 8th the extract of the notices, against which there was no opposition.

Four witnesses: SALVATORE PORRO, aged thirty, bracciale, of Rende; SALVATORE COSTABILE, aged twenty-three, SARTORE (tailor); GIUSEPPE DE PAOLA, aged twenty-eight, FALEGNAME (carpenter); and FRANCESCO DE GENNARO, aged thirty-three, sartore. All of Rende.

[Right-hand column] The parish priest of SANTA MARIA MAGGIORE returned one of the copies, certifying at the foot that the canonical celebration of the marriage followed on the TWENTIETH of the same month, before the witnesses IGNAZIO MANDARINO and PIETRO PERUGINI.

What you need to know about this record

  • THIS IS THE RECORD THAT CLOSES THE FILIATION OF FRANCESCO GIACOMO. Until 26 August 2026, that he was the son of Giuseppe Palermo and Carmina Spizzirri was an inference by convergence: it rested on his sister Rosalia's record of 1838 and on the coincidence of village, name and trade. Here his own marriage record says it. Therefore ROSALIA and AQUILA PALERMO are his sisters of both father and mother, and Carmina Spizzirri is a great-grandmother in the direct line without any reservation.
  • This wedding was looked for over months in MARANO MARCHESATO, and it is not there: nine indexes, from 1830 to 1838, a hundred and eighty-three weddings, not a single male Palermo with any Volpintesta. It was in RENDE. The lesson, noted down: when both bride and groom are from one village but she is already living in another, the wedding is celebrated where SHE lives. The double notification — Rende and Marano — says so in the record itself.
  • THE MOTHER IS CALLED «CARMELA» HERE AND «CARMINA» IN EVERYTHING ELSE. They are variants of the same root and the Rende clerk did not know her. The father, the village and the trade all match, so there is no reason to doubt that it is the same woman, but it is noted.
  • MICHELE VOLPINTESTA, CONFIRMED BY RECORD. Document number 6 is the death certificate of the bride's paternal grandfather: it is Michele, the father of the late Vincenzo. Until now that grandfather depended on the blog index and on the index of the dossier; now an original record backs him up.
  • POINT 7 JARS, and it is worth saying so: it calls for the death certificate «della di lei madre», and the bride's mother, Teresa Luchetta, appears ALIVE three lines higher up in this very record and does not die until 1851. Most probably it is boilerplate: the law required proof of who was to give consent — the father dead, it passes to the paternal grandfather, and he dead, to the mother — and the clerk copied out the whole list. It is NOT to be taken as an indication of another mother.
  • The five Marano records fit with this one without forcing anything: Vincenzo appears as «fu» and died in 1823; Teresa Luchetta appears alive and died in 1851; Giuseppe and Carmela appear alive and died in 1863 and 1864. And the groom's twenty-eight years give 1809, which is exactly the year the dossier index attributes to his birth.
  • WHAT IS MISSING NOW: the PROCESSETTO of Rende 1837. Documents 1 and 2 of the list are the birth records of the two spouses, and the dossier preserves them. That of Francesco Giacomo (1809, Marano) is the piece that has been missing for years: it is not in the civil registration of Marano for 1809, read in full, so that extract may be the only copy left.
Ancestor

Teresa Tenuta

Wife of Michele Volpentesta. For two years she was in this tree as an index name with nothing behind it, and on 26 August 2026 a parish extract of 1793 finally named her.

Spouse
MICHELE VOLPENTESTA († Rende, 20 August 1793, aged seventy)
Children
VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA (c. 1776 – 19 March 1823), and PIETRO VOLPINTESTA, married to Anna Belmonte († 1827) — the latter by index only
Where this comes from
the death extract of her husband, which calls him «MICHAEL VOLPENTESTA VIR THERESIAE TENUTA» — husband of Teresa Tenuta
Widowed since
August 1793

No record obtained yet

Her case is the best example of why in this tree the index does not count as proof. The scanned indexes from the blog gave her as Vincenzo's mother from the very beginning, in two different rows (Vincenzo 1823/8 and his son Pietro 1827/34). But the record of 1823 was read twice — the original and the certified copy of 1837 — and both leave the mother's box BLANK, with the printed rule left unfilled. So it was decided not to open an entry for her: a name without a document does not get in. What rescued her was the parish extract of her husband's death, hidden inside her granddaughter's marriage dossier. A document in Latin, of 1793, that names her in three words: VIR THERESIAE TENUTA. Of herself nothing more is known: neither year, nor village, nor parents, nor when she died. In 1793 she was widowed. Everything of hers would be parochial, because civil registration does not begin until 1809. A side fact worth not forgetting: in Marano itself there is another Vincenzo Volpintesta, son of a Ferdinando and of an ANNA MARIA TENUTA, who died in 1855. Identical mother's surname and a different family. 29 AUGUST 2026 — SHE NOW HAS CHILDREN WITH NAMES AND YEARS. The baptismal books of the parish of Rende begin in 1731 and are transcribed on the Calabrian blog. Swept year by year, THREE children of hers come out, all three with the same formula «Volpintesta, Michele — Tenuta, Teresa»: PIETRO GAETANO in 1767 (record 13), VINCENZO MERCURIO in 1770 (record 48) and GREGORIO GIOACCHINO in 1773 (record 41). That is: she was living in RENDE at least from 1767 to 1773, and there she was widowed in 1793. AND THE ONE OF 1770 IS CALLED VINCENZO. If that child is our Vincenzo, then the box that the record of 1823 left blank is at last filled in by an independent route, and she ceases to depend on the three Latin words of 1793. But there is a clash — the death record says that Vincenzo was born in Marano, and the ages give 1773 or 1776, not 1770 — which is explained in full in his own entry. It is still not accepted as good until the original book is seen, which is on FamilySearch and not on Antenati. OF HER OWN PARENTS NOTHING IS STILL KNOWN, and it will not be easy: the marriage to Michele is not in the thirty-nine years of Rende marriage indexes that have been read (1733-1750 and 1752-1772). Either it is in the one year that is missing, 1751, or they married in her own parish. TENUTA families of Rende of those years, in case one of them is hers: Gaetano Tenuta with Isabella Chiappetta, Tommaso Tenuta with Chiara Russo, Biaggio Tenuta with Teresa Rocchetto, Vincenzo Tenuta with Rosa Rizzo and Antonio Tenuta with Carmina Di Simone. SHE WAS SEARCHED FOR IN SIXTEEN YEARS OF BAPTISMS AND SHE IS NOT THERE — AND IT IS KNOWN WHY. On 29 August 2026 the Rende baptisms of 1735 and of 1741 to 1755 were swept, year by year, in the alphabetical band where TENUTA falls. Not one Teresa. But the fault is not hers: the transcriptions jump from 1735 to 1741 — 1736, 1737, 1738, 1739 and 1740 are missing, and those five years fall right in the centre of the range in which she must have been born. Her children are baptised in 1767, 1770 and 1773; born before 1735 she would have been over thirty-two at the first, and born after 1755, twelve. Either she is in those five missing years, or she was not from Rende. The sweep was not in vain: it left FIVE Tenuta families of Rende documented with children in those years, and one of them will be hers. The first one to look at is NICOLA TENUTA with TERESA CHIAPPETTA, with children in 1745, 1748, 1751 and 1753 — because the mother is called Teresa, and giving a daughter the mother's name was the commonest thing in the world. The other four: Giuseppe Tenuta with Innocenza Turano, Tommaso Tenuta with Chiara, Biaggio Tenuta with Teresa Rocchetto and Michele Tenuta with Innocenza Mirabelli. NOR IS SHE BURIED IN RENDE BETWEEN 1802 AND 1809, the eight years having been swept. She was widowed in August 1793, so either she died between 1793 and 1801 — years the transcriptions do not have — or she died from 1809 onwards, and then her record is already CIVIL and is on Antenati. And a warning that comes out of the same sweep and that must be kept in mind when reading anything that comes from those tables: the wife of Tommaso Tenuta is called Chiara in five rows, and her surname is transcribed in THREE different ways — Ruffolo, Russo and Di Bartolo. The column of the mother's surname is not always to be trusted. That does not touch her, because her name does not come from a transcription but from a document: the three Latin words of 1793. WHERE SHE IS NOT, AND THAT IS ALREADY A FACT (29 August 2026). Four Rende parish tables have been read — the marriages of 1796 and 1798 and the burials of 1804 and 1807, several hundred people — and NOT A SINGLE TENUTA APPEARS: not as bride, not as mother, not as deceased, not as wife of a deceased. This is added to the earlier search for her baptism in Rende (1735 and 1741-1755), which also gave zero. That was put down to the blog lacking the years 1736-1740; it can no longer be put down to that. The simple explanation is that TENUTA IS NOT A RENDE SURNAME, and it fits the conflict already noted in the entry of her son Vincenzo, whose death record says that he was born in MARANO. So the search here stops: she must be looked for in MARANO MARCHESATO, which is where this branch lives. This does not prove that no Tenuta exists in Rende in other years — it is a strong indication, not a demonstration.
