How it was located
by the alphabetical index at the end (page 18), the row «Celeri Callisto e Vanni Enrichetta — 1859 Febbrajo 4 — effettuazione — 3». 1861 and 1860 had already been ruled out by reading their indexes in full

What the manuscript says

3. L'anno 1859 milleottocentocinquantanove, il giorno 4 quattro del mese di Febbrajo. Davanti me sottoscritto Ufficiale dello Stato Civile del Comune di TRASSILICO, Provincia di Garfagnana, è comparso CELERI CALLISTO di anni 28 ventotto, POSSIDENTE E SEGRETARIO DELLA COMUNE, nato e domiciliato in Trassilico, FIGLIO DEL FU DESIDERIO, ed alla presenza di GALANTI ERMENEGILDO figlio del vivo Felice, e di REBECHI GIOVANNI del fu Paolino, ambedue maggiori di età, possidenti e domiciliati in Trassilico, testimoni, ha esposto: che nel giorno 30 trenta Gennajo del corrente anno è stato celebrato il matrimonio di esso comparente colla Signora VANNI ENRICHETTA, FIGLIA DEL FU DR. PIETRO, dell'età di anni 24 ventiquattro, senza professione, nata e domiciliata a Trassilico, davanti il Sig. Don Grandi Parroco…

Il sottoscritto Parroco fa fede che il Sig. CALLISTO figlio del fu DESIDERIO CELERI dell'età di anni 28, nato e domiciliato a Trassilico, POSSIDENTE e SEGRETARIO COMUNALE, e la Sig.ra VANNI ENRICHETTA dell'età di anni 24, nata essa pure e domiciliata a Trassilico, POSSIDENTE, hanno nel giorno 30 trenta Gennajo 1859 celebrato il loro matrimonio giusta i riti della Chiesa cattolica davanti me sottoscritto, e alla presenza di PIETRO fu ANTONIO BONETTI, e PIETRO fu LUIGI MENGHI, testimoni ambedue di Trassilico. Ed in fede, dalla Canonica di Trassilico, oggi 4 Febbrajo 1859. DOMENICO GRANDI Rettore.

Translation

Record 3. The year 1859, the FOURTH OF FEBRUARY. Before me, registrar of the Civil Status of the COMUNE OF TRASSILICO, province of Garfagnana, there appeared CALLISTO CELERI, aged TWENTY-EIGHT, A MAN OF PROPERTY AND TOWN CLERK, born and resident at Trassilico, SON OF THE LATE DESIDERIO.

In the presence of ERMENEGILDO GALANTI, son of the living Felice, and of GIOVANNI REBECHI, son of the late Paolino, both of full age, men of property and resident at Trassilico, he stated: THAT ON THE THIRTIETH OF JANUARY of the current year his marriage was celebrated with SIGNORA ENRICHETTA VANNI, DAUGHTER OF THE LATE DOCTOR PIETRO, aged TWENTY-FOUR, without profession, born and resident at Trassilico, before signor don Grandi, parish priest.

THE PARISH PRIEST'S CERTIFICATE: «The undersigned parish priest certifies that signor CALLISTO, son of the late DESIDERIO CELERI, aged twenty-eight, born and resident at Trassilico, A MAN OF PROPERTY and TOWN CLERK, and signora ENRICHETTA VANNI, aged twenty-four, likewise born and resident at Trassilico, A WOMAN OF PROPERTY, celebrated on the thirtieth of January 1859 their marriage according to the rites of the Catholic Church before me, in the presence of PIETRO BONETTI, son of the late Antonio, and PIETRO MENGHI, son of the late Luigi, both witnesses of Trassilico. And in witness whereof, from the parish house of Trassilico, today 4 February 1859. DOMENICO GRANDI, rector.»

What you need to know about this record

  • IT IS THE WEDDING THAT WAS BEING SOUGHT, AND IT COST THREE YEARS OF INDEXES. First 1861 (forty weddings) and 1860 were ruled out, by reading their alphabetical indexes in full, before it was found in 1859. That method — one image of an index per year instead of a whole register — is what made the search viable.
  • THE AGES TALLY WITH EVERYTHING ELSE. He, twenty-eight in February 1859 → born about 1830-31, the same as the thirty-one he declares in 1861 and 1862. She, twenty-four → born about 1834-35, the same as the twenty-seven of 1862 and the eighty-eight of her death record of 1923. Four documents, sixty-four years and two different archives, all pointing to the same pair of dates.
  • BOTH WERE «POSSIDENTI» — PROPERTY OWNERS. And she in her own right: the parish priest's certificate calls her possidente, not «daughter of». In a mountain village where the neighbouring record describes the couple as «agricoltore» and «agricoltrice», and where most sign «per essere illetterato», this marriage is that of two property owners who can write. Their two civil witnesses are possidenti too.
  • WHAT THE RECORD DOES NOT GIVE, AND IT MUST BE SAID: IT DOES NOT NAME THE MOTHERS. Of him only «figlio del fu Desiderio»; of her only «figlia del fu Dr. Pietro». Callisto's mother remains unknown. Enrichetta's — LIVIA RIGHINI — we know by another route: her death record of 1923.
  • THEY MARRIED IN JANUARY AND TEBALDO WAS BORN IN JULY 1862 — three and a half years later. If there were no children in between, and the indexes of births for 1861 and 1862 carry none, 1859 and 1860 remain to be swept.
  • DON DOMENICO GRANDI married them in 1859 and baptised Tebaldo in 1862. The same priest, in the same village, in the two most important records of this branch.
Pendiente

What is missing

Each loose end, why it stays open, and where one would have to look.

The fourteen children — and now we know where the missing five are

Family memory in Santa Fe speaks of FOURTEEN children of Francesco and Filomena, and remembers among them a VINCENZO, a Raffaele, a Marietta and an Angelo. The documents give nine. For months that was filed away as memory exaggerating. NOT ANY MORE. IN AUGUST 2026 THREE INDEPENDENT THINGS CAME TOGETHER: 1. Antonio's descendant in Chicago, contacted separately, says exactly the same thing from the other side of the Atlantic: fourteen. Two branches separated by sixty years and ten thousand kilometres, which never spoke to each other, remember the same number. 2. Antonio's obituary, «The Star», 10 May 1981: «He is survived by […] a brothr, VINCENZO of Riverdale». A BROTHER VINCENZO, ALIVE IN 1981. That is printed paper, not memory. 3. And above all the cadence of the births, which is the real proof: 1895 · 1896 · 1897 · 1899 (twins) · 1902 · 1904 · 1907 · 1909 A child every eighteen or twenty-four months without a break, from the wedding of 1894. AND THE LAST DOCUMENTED ONE IS MARIA, OF SEPTEMBER 1909 — that is, right at the edge. The Rende holdings on Antenati STOP IN 1910. FROM THIS CAME THE FIRST HYPOTHESIS: five children are not missing, five years of registration are. Filomena married in 1894 and was still bearing children in 1909 at the same rate. If she went on, 1911, 1913, 1915, 1917 and 1919 give five more children. Nine plus five, fourteen. BUT THERE IS A SERIOUS OBJECTION, and Gonzalo himself raised it looking at the photograph of the four brothers in Argentina: ANTONIO LEFT RENDE IN 1914. If Vincenzo had been born after 1910, Antonio left when his brother was a baby or not yet born — and yet in 1981 they were living in the same Illinois town and the newspaper calls them brothers. It is not impossible: that is how chain migration works, the eldest sends for the youngest twenty years later. But it weakens the hypothesis. AND IT FORCES US TO LOOK AT THE GAPS. Among the nine documented children there are SIX EMPTY YEARS: 1898, 1900, 1901, 1903, 1905, 1906 and 1908. It had been taken for granted that the blog's transcription covers Rende from 1893 to 1910 without a crack. But this very project has shown that THE REGISTERS HAVE HOLES: the birth of Francesco Giacomo is NOT in the Marano civil register for 1809 — 84 records, read in full — and it had to be hunted down in the parish book, inside a marriage dossier. If the Salernos had a child baptised without registering him, or if a year's register was lost, those children exist and are not there. TWO LIVE HYPOTHESES, AND BOTH THINGS MUST BE REQUESTED A) BORN AFTER 1909 — to the COMUNE DI RENDE, births from 1910 to 1920, children of Francesco Salerno and Filomena Chiappetta. It goes in the same letter as the four deaths, so it costs nothing to add. B) BORN IN THE GAPS OF 1898-1908 — to the PARISH BOOKS OF RENDE, via the Archivio Diocesano di Cosenza-Bisignano. The civil register for those years has already been swept; if there are children there, they are in the baptisms and not at the town hall. ALREADY RULED OUT — SAN FILI. On 26 August 2026 the idea was tested that a child might have been registered in a neighbouring village, if the family were away from Rende when the birth came. San Fili is ten kilometres off and its indexes are on disk. All SEVEN gap years were read in full — 1898, 1900, 1901, 1903, 1905, 1906 and 1908 — and there is not one child of Francesco Salerno and Filomena Chiappetta. There are five Salerno families native to the village, and one of them is a FRANCESCO SALERNO married to an ASSUNTA CAIRO who had a daughter there in 1908: not ours, but any future search will trip over him. Marano Principato and San Vincenzo la Costa remain to be checked, and they are free. AND THE QUICKEST THING OF ALL, which does not even cost a stamp: ask Antonio's descendant in Chicago for VINCENZO'S DATE AND PLACE OF BIRTH. If she says Rende and a year before 1910, hypothesis B wins. If she says 1913, A wins. A single fact settles it, and that lady is already in touch.

The other Celeri of Trassilico — three households, one surname

Celeri is an extremely rare surname in the Garfagnana: today not one remains in the whole valley, and in the Trassilico index of births for 1862 there is ONE Celeri among seventy-three births. And yet in 1861 there were at least THREE Celeri households in the comune. · **CALLISTO, son of the late DESIDERIO** — ours. In the village of Trassilico. Possidente and town clerk. · **The household of the late NICOLAO CELERI**, married to ROSARIA DOMINICI, who was still alive in November 1861. Their daughter MARIA, twenty-seven, agricultrice, married on 7 November 1861 to PIETRO BATTAGLIA, of S. Pellegrinetto, a resident of the Eremo di Calomini (Trassilico, Marriages 1861, records 30 and 39). · **The brothers DOMENICO and ILARIO CELERI**, owners of a house at VALICO SOTTO, in the place called Capanne (Trassilico, Births 1861, record 45). A SISTER OF CALLISTO WAS SOUGHT AND A WHOLE BRANCH CAME OUT. Maria Celeri looked like the perfect candidate — the right age, the same village, and Callisto signing as a witness at her wedding — but the record says «figlia del fu NICOLAO» and not of Desiderio. That Callisto was her witness proves nothing: he appears as a witness on five consecutive records that year, because he was the village's standing witness. THAT THEY ARE RELATED IS VERY LIKELY AND IS NOT PROVEN. With so rare a surname in a mountain village, Desiderio, Nicolao and the father of Domenico and Ilario would be brothers or cousins. It would be settled by **the death record of any one of them**, which would name their parents. All three predate 1862 and would be in the Trassilico death registers from 1852 to 1861, all digitised and with a calculable ark.

Who is the «brothr Vincenzo of Riverdale»

Antonio's obituary, of May 1981, names him among the survivors: «a brothr, VINCENZO of Riverdale». It is one line away from solving the riddle of the fourteen children, and one must be honest: three readings are possible. A) HE IS A REAL BROTHER, son of Francesco and Filomena, born at Rende after 1909 and therefore invisible on Antenati. It is the reading that fits the memory of both branches of the family, the cadence of the births, and the name: the Santa Fe family remembers precisely a VINCENZO among the fourteen. An American obituary says «brother» when it means brother. B) HE IS HIS BROTHER-IN-LAW. His wife, Rosina, was a Salerno by birth, and the FamilySearch tree gives her a brother called VINCENZO SALERNO. A brother-in-law who also shares the surname can end up printed as «brother» with nobody correcting it. Against this reading: obituaries of the period usually distinguished «brother-in-law». C) HE IS ANOTHER VINCENZO SALERNO, unrelated. And here great care is needed, because there are ALREADY FOUR different Vincenzo Salernos in the Chicago orbit: · The one from BROGNATURO, born 13 December 1896, husband of Maria Emilia Squillacioti, with five children in Chicago. Province of Vibo Valentia: another family. · The one from SAN FILI, born 14 October 1889, arrived in New York on the steamer Tuscania on 7 September 1923, a mixer at the Sawyer Biscuit Co. in Chicago. His record was located in the San Fili index — record 110 of 1889 — and HE IS THE SON OF VINCENZO ANTONIO SALERNO AND CAROLINA FILOMENA RINALDI. RULED OUT: born five years before Francesco and Filomena's wedding, in another village and of other parents. BEWARE OF HIM, because he is a perfect trap: his mother was called CAROLINA FILOMENA. A Vincenzo Salerno, son of a Filomena, from the province of Cosenza, emigrated to Chicago and of roughly the right age. Everything fits except her surname. · Rosina's brother, Antonio's wife, according to the FamilySearch tree. No record. · And the one in the obituary, unidentified. HOW IT GETS SOLVED, in order of ease: · The obituary of the Riverdale Vincenzo himself, which would name his parents. If it says Francesco and Filomena, that is that. · His draft card or his naturalisation, which would give village and date of birth. If it says RENDE and a year after 1909, he is a brother. · The Rende births from 1910 to 1920, at the Comune di Rende. · And the quickest route of all, which settles everything with a single fact: ask Antonio's descendant in Chicago for THE DATE AND PLACE OF BIRTH of the Riverdale Vincenzo. If she says RENDE, he is a brother and the fourteen children are proven. If she says SAN FILI, he is the one born in 1889 and he is not.

A Vincenzo at San Sisto

In 1914 a VINCENZO SALERNO, son of a Domenico, married Assunta Buccieri at San Sisto; and in 1912 a ROSA SALERNO, daughter of the same Domenico, married Raffaele Mirandola. San Sisto is the only series on the blog that crosses the 1910 wall. If that Domenico is Francesco's brother, Vincenzo is Settimo's first cousin — and the first of the four names to appear at the right genealogical distance. The index gives only the father's forename, so the record itself is needed.

The deaths of Michele and Maria Antonia

Both were alive on 1 April 1894, giving their consent at Francesco's wedding, and still alive in October 1897, at Giovanni's. Domenico's wedding, of November 1910, extends the window by twelve more years: there they both appear as «resident at Rende», without the «fu» that the same form uses for the bride's dead parents. Michele was 85 and she was 71, and they were still living. Their deaths are therefore later than November 1910 — precisely the year the Rende holdings on Antenati end, so they will most likely have to be requested from the Comune di Rende.

Where Domenico was born

Half solved in 2026: on his marriage record of November 1910 he declares he was born at CASTROLIBERO and is fifty-one, which places him around 1859 — some ten months after his parents' wedding, that is, as the first-born. The problem is that the Castrolibero sweep, which covered 1859 to 1872, did not find him. Either he was born at the end of 1858, outside the window, or his record is entered under DE RANGO, the surname Michele used to register his children there, and it was overlooked when searching for «Salerno». Castrolibero births for 1858 and 1859 must be read again, looking for a Domenico son of Michele and Maria Palermo.

Whom Domenico was widowed of

The 1910 record calls him «vedovo» and says no more. Before Giuseppina Cairo there was another wife, and with her probably the children Salvatore (1906) and Michele (1909) whom the index attributes to him alongside a Maria Russo. That first marriage and the first wife's death are both missing.

Above Michele there is nothing

And not for want of searching. His birth record says he was found at the wheel of the Cosenza hospice with no mark on him. There is no parentage to look for because there never was any in writing. All that might remain is in the books of the Cosenza foundling house and in the parish books of the Archivio Diocesano di Cosenza-Bisignano.

SOLVED · The wedding of Giacomo and Carmina, and with it the parentage

It was searched for at Marano Marchesato for months and was not there: nine indexes, from 1830 to 1838, one hundred and eighty-three weddings, not a single male Palermo with any Volpintesta. Marano's 1837 register carries an index at the end with its twenty-five weddings one by one, and the dossier file — 235 pages — corresponds exactly to those twenty-five, with no room for a twenty-sixth. IT WAS NOT AT MARANO BECAUSE IT IS AT RENDE. RENDE, MARRIAGES 1837, RECORD 20, of 19 October. Downloaded and read on 26 August 2026. He, twenty-eight, pecorajo, born at Marano; she, twenty-three, born at Marano and already resident at Rende. The church wedding at Santa Maria Maggiore the following day. THE LESSON, so as never to lose months again: when both parties are from one village but she already lives in another, the wedding takes place where SHE lives. The record says so without meaning to: the notice was posted on the doors of both town halls, Rende's and Marano's, on the same Sunday. AND IT SETTLES SOMETHING BIGGER THAN A DATE. That Francesco Giacomo was the son of Giuseppe Palermo and Carmina Spizzirri had until that day been an inference by convergence, resting on his sister Rosalia's record and on the coincidence of village, name and trade. His marriage record says it in so many words. So Rosalia and Aquila are his sisters by both father AND mother, and Carmina Spizzirri stands as a great-grandmother in the direct line without any reservation. WHAT REMAINS: the PROCESSETTO of Rende 1837. The record lists the eight documents the couple presented, and the first two are their birth certificates. The dossier preserves them. That of Francesco Giacomo — 1809, Marano — is the piece that has been missing for years: it is not in the Marano civil register for 1809, which has been read in full, so that extract may be the only surviving copy.

SOLVED · The generation of 1780, and the one of 1740 behind it

There were four forebears without a single paper — Giuseppe Palermo, Carmina Spizzirri, Vincenzo Volpintesta and Teresa Luchetta. In August 2026 they became two, and with the batch of five Marano Marchesato records they have become NONE. All four now have a record of their own, an age, a trade and a date of death: GIUSEPPE PALERMO — Deaths 1863, record 24. Ninety years old, bracciale, died 6 March 1863. «Sposo di Carmina Spizzirri», «figlio di fu LORENZO... e di fu GIULIA CHIAPPETTA». CARMINA SPIZZIRRI — Deaths 1864, record 74. Seventy years old, contadina, died 11 October 1864. «Vedova di Giuseppe Palermo». Her parents are NOT named. VINCENZO VOLPENTESTA — Deaths 1823, record 8. Fifty years old, bracciale, died 19 March 1823. «Sposo di Teresa Luchetta», «figlio del fu MICHELE». The mother is not named. TERESA LUCHETTA — Deaths 1851, record 44. Seventy-two years old, contadina, died 6 June 1851. «Vedova di Vincenzo Volpentesta», «figlia del fu GIO: ANTONIO... e della fu MARIANNA CONFORTI». The one who had no paper at all turned out to have the best of the five records. And with them five new names of the 1740-1760 generation enter the tree, already proven: Lorenzo Palermo, Giulia Chiappetta, Michele Volpintesta, Gio: Antonio Luchetta and Marianna Conforti. Above them there is no civil registration at all: the Napoleonic register in Calabria begins in 1809 and these people were born and married long before. What remains are the books of the parish of S. Maria del Carmine at Marano.

Carmina Spizzirri's parents — and now we know where to look for them

Her death record was sought precisely in order to get her parents out of it, and it is the one document in the tree that leaves those two boxes deliberately blank: «figlia del fu ————» and «della fu ————». All it proves is that by October 1864 both were dead. BUT THE WINDOW HAS NARROWED SHARPLY. The baptism of her son Francesco Giacomo — Marano, 14 February 1809, found inside the 1837 dossier — says that she and Giuseppe Palermo were already «LEGITIMIS CONJUGIBUS», lawfully married. Until now all that was known was that they were married before 1812, from the first child on record. Now we know their marriage is EARLIER THAN FEBRUARY 1809. And that takes it out of Antenati for good: civil registration in the Kingdom of Naples does not begin until 1809, so that wedding can only be in the parish books of Marano Marchesato — the church of SANTA MARIA DEL MONTE CARMELO, which is where the son was baptised. A parish marriage record names the parents of both parties: it would open the Spizzirri branch at a stroke. With her born around 1789, the wedding will fall between 1805 and 1808. That is a four-year range in a small village: perfectly searchable for anyone able to get into the Archivio Diocesano di Cosenza-Bisignano.

SOLVED · Yes: Carmina Spizzirri is the mother of Francesco Giacomo

This question was open, and wide open. It had been raised by Carmina's death record, which has her dying in October 1864 AGED SEVENTY, that is born around 1794. With that figure, and a Francesco Giacomo born between 1805 and 1812, she would have been between eleven and eighteen when she bore him. With the other children there was no problem — Antonio Benedetto (1812), Francesca (1816), Vincenzo (1818), Aquila (1821), Francesca (1823) place her between eighteen and twenty-nine; the one who did not fit was him, and by a good margin. So it was set down in writing that a first marriage of Giuseppe's was possible, and that Francesco Giacomo might be Rosalia's half-brother and not her full brother. HIS OWN MARRIAGE RECORD SETTLES IT, read on 26 August 2026: RENDE, 1837, RECORD 20. It says, beyond interpretation, «figlio di GIUSEPPE… e di CARMELA SPIZZIRRI, domiciliata ivi». Carmela and Carmina are the same woman: the husband, the village and the trade all agree, and the clerk was from Rende, not Marano. AND THE OBJECTION FROM THE AGES UNDOES ITSELF. The seventy years of the 1864 record were the bad figure. In 1821, registering Aquila's birth, her own husband gives her as THIRTY-TWO, which makes her born around 1789. With that date — stated by someone who knew her, not by an informant standing over a corpse — she was about twenty when Francesco Giacomo was born in 1809. Nothing left to explain. CONSEQUENCE: Rosalia and Aquila Palermo are his sisters by both father AND mother, not half-sisters. And Carmina Spizzirri stands as a direct-line great-grandmother without reservation. The silence of the 1867 record about the mother was, after all, mere slackness in the Rende form.

SOLVED · Teresa Tenuta exists, and a document of 1793 saved her

She spent two years in limbo. The scanned indexes gave her as the mother of Vincenzo Volpentesta in two separate rows, but the 1823 death record was read TWICE — the original and the certified copy of 1837 inside the marriage dossier — and both leave the mother's box BLANK, with the printed rule unfilled. It was decided not to open a card for her: an index name with no document does not enter this tree. She was rescued from the least expected place: the parish extract of her husband's death, kept inside the marriage dossier of her GRANDDAUGHTER Carmina, at Rende, 1837. Three words of Latin: «MICHAEL VOLPENTESTA VIR THERESIAE TENUTA» — Michele Volpentesta, husband of Teresa Tenuta. She now has a card. Nothing else is known of her: no year, no village, no parents, no date of death. In August 1793 she was widowed. Everything of hers would be parochial, because civil registration does not exist until 1809. THE LESSON, which holds for everything still open: the document that confirms someone is almost never their own. It is somebody else's.

The Chiappettas also come from Castrolibero

It is the incidental find of the 1873 marriage. Giovanni Chiappetta and Angela Massei, Filomena's parents, both declare they were born at CASTROLIBERO and are resident at Rende. It is the same village Michele Salerno came from — registered there as De Rango — and Maria Palermo, and from which their children reached Rende. Two separate families making the same journey in the same years, and ending up related in 1894. Practical consequence: the births of Giovanni (c. 1848) and Angela (c. 1858) must be sought at Castrolibero, not at Rende, exactly as had to be done with the Salernos.

How many siblings Maria Antonia had

Four children of Giacomo Palermo and Carmina Volpintesta are known: Maria Antonia (March 1839), Antonio (October 1841), Annunciata (February 1845) and Teresa (January 1852). Thirteen years between the first and the last, and only three gaps looked at. In a Calabrian peasant family of those years six or eight children would be normal, so there are almost certainly siblings missing between 1839 and 1852 — and perhaps one before 1839. The Marano Marchesato birth registers are complete for those years and easy to run through: two records per image, with the number written out in the heading. It is sweeping work, not detective work.

The ship

The family emigrated to Argentina around 1919. The passenger list of the arrival at Buenos Aires names the whole family with each one's age: a single document with an exact photograph of who the Salernos were the day they sailed. It is the next objective. And there is a new reason to look for it: in the margin of GIOVANNI's birth record (1904) someone noted that on 24 July 1926 he married Cundari Anna AT RENDE. If he was in the village in 1926, either he did not sail in 1919 or he came back. The assumption that they all emigrated has to be checked, not taken on trust.

SOLVED · The Atessa marriage of 1892, record 101

It was the shortest and most profitable loose end still open, and it has paid off handsomely. Record 101 of the Atessa Marriages for 1892 has been downloaded, read and transcribed on the card for Nicola and Angiolina's marriage. They married on 29 December 1892 at a quarter past eleven in the morning. He was twenty-three, a CONTADINO, had been BORN AT ATESSA and was the son of GIUSEPPE FAIENZA, a farmhand of Atessa and living at the time, and of the late MARIA STANISCIA. With that the question of where the family comes from is closed: from Atessa, not Paglieta. What it opens now is Nicola's own birth record, around 1869 at Atessa, and his parents' marriage, around 1865.

CLOSED ON ANTENATI · Domenico Faienza must be requested from the Comune di Atessa

His birth was searched for in both possible villages and both possible years, and the result is negative on all four corners. PAGLIETA 1899 and ATESSA 1899: read in full, no Faienza son of Nicola. PAGLIETA 1900 and ATESSA 1900: they do not exist on Antenati — the query answers «nessun risultato», which is not a network failure but the catalogue's answer. Conclusion, and it is worth stopping spending windows on this: THE ATESSA HOLDINGS AT THE ARCHIVIO DI STATO DI CHIETI DO NOT REACH 1900. His record must be requested from the COMUNE DI ATESSA, which keeps what the archive has not yet received or scanned. And it is a scanning problem, not a privacy one: a record of 1900 carries no legal restriction. When requesting it, the identifying detail is NOT the surname: at Atessa there were several Faienza families at once — the 1899 births show three different ones. What identifies him is «di Nicola», and better still «di Nicola e di Angela Pizzi».

SOLVED · Who Giuseppe Pizzi's father was, and by which of the three wives

Both questions have fallen to a single line. The Atessa tree had two halves and a gap: at the top Domenico Pizzi and Santa Menna, around 1730, parents of Leone Pizzi (d. 1841); at the bottom Giuseppe Pizzi, father of Domenico (1824-1891) and great-grandfather of Angiolina. That Giuseppe was Leone's son fitted, but it was written nowhere and rested solely on an unsourced family compilation that had already been wrong three times over. Now it is written: the death record of GIUSEPPE PIZZI — Atessa, 29 April 1847, record 106, downloaded and transcribed on his card — says «figlio del fu LEONE PIZZI di professione MURATORE, e della fu ROSA IACOBITTI». The two halves are one, and with them Domenico Pizzi the elder and Santa Menna enter the tree, proven, from the generation of 1730-1740. The record also settles the second question: Leone was widowed three times — Anna Iano, Rosa Iacobitti and Erminia Vitulli — and it was not known which had borne him children. It was ROSA IACOBITTI, the second. And in passing it fixes the reading of her name, which the 1841 record had left wavering between «Nora» and «Rosa».

Rosa Iacobitti's parents, and Leone's marriage

The new ceiling of this branch. Rosa Iacobitti married Leone Pizzi before 1787 — their son Giuseppe was born that year — and therefore before Napoleonic civil registration existed, which in Abruzzo begins in 1809. Neither her birth, nor her marriage, nor her death can be on Antenati. What remains are the parish books of Atessa, in the Archivio Diocesano di Chieti-Vasto. The same holds for Domenico Pizzi the elder and Santa Menna, and for Leone's other two wives.

Nicola Faienza's birth, and his parents

The marriage record of 1892 gives him twenty-three years and declares him born at Atessa, so his birth record is in the Atessa Births for 1868, 1869 or 1870 — the same register, and almost the same year, as that of his wife Angiolina, who is from February 1869. Two alphabetical indexes close it. And behind that lies the marriage of his parents, GIUSEPPE FAIENZA and MARIA STANISCIA, which would be around 1865 at Atessa, and her death, before December 1892. A warning for that search: at Atessa there are several Faienza families at once — the 1899 births show three different ones — so the surname identifies nobody. What identifies him is «di Giuseppe».

Ningún cabo abierto en la rama Rivas

Los cabos abiertos que hay apuntan a otras ramas. Pulsa «Todas» arriba para verlos todos.
